# Pending: the v3.4.* and architecture audit - 29 slices and 7 interaction hunts read, 117 findings, the contained ones applied

Status: **PENDING** - recorded 2026-11-20. It decides nothing, opens no stage
and licenses no code; each finding waits for the operator's word to be taken
up, deferred or dropped. **The contained defects and dead code were applied
on 2026-11-20** (section 10; on `main` at `d436b7b1`), and later milestones
closed three more: **H33** by DL-S2 (`f511df53`, merged at `f0c95a09`: the
session registry takes no latch) and **H36** and **H39** by DO-S2's E4a
(`a97bd952`, merged at `bf289a82`: the write fetch's image debt taken in
the exclusive grant's own hold). The rest of section 6 is not re-read
against DK, DL, DN or DO.
Raised: 2026-11-20, in conversation with the operator.
Read at: `fba5529f` (`v3.4.17`), on worktree `audit-v3-4-and-architecture`.
Every `path:line` below is at that commit.
Related: `docs/pending/whole-engine-audit-unverified-findings.md` (F1-F32)
and `docs/pending/whole-engine-audit-second-run-twelve-slices.md` (G1-G66) -
every reader here was told to leave out what those two, `known-gaps.md`, the
other pending entries and the work orders already record;
`docs/inflight/README.md` (its rule is why nothing here is under
`docs/inflight/bugs/`).

## 1. What prompted it

`[operator]` On 2026-11-20: *"look at the 3.4.* work for inefficiency,
quiet-wrong not yet found, code duplication, dead code and performance
improvements, and run an audit. Not only 3.4.*: the code's overall
architecture must be examined too. Limit concurrently running agents to 4
and use only 4 hours."*

## 2. What bounds it

- **Nothing was built, run or changed.** Every finder and every verifier read
  the tree and grepped it; every claim below is `[source-read]`. "Survived"
  means one or two readers told to refute it could not; it is not a
  reproduction, and no cell was written.
- **The four-agent cap held**: both workflows ran every agent behind one
  four-slot limiter.
- **The four-hour budget was overrun.** The work began at 07:29 UTC, so the
  budget ended at 11:30 UTC. Round 1 ran 07:31-08:58 UTC and round 2
  09:02-09:36 UTC; the report was written from 12:36 UTC, after the
  operator's go-ahead, with no agent running. The writing is outside the
  budget.
- **Round 2 was cut short by the account's usage limit**, not by the clock:
  14 of its 28 agents died on it. Five of its twelve hunts never ran
  (section 4), one new finding has no verifier at all (`x-insert-conflict#1`),
  one has only its first, and 14 of the 22 round-1 correctness findings sent
  for a second verifier got none.
- **Round 1's verifiers refuted none of 114 findings.** That is not credible
  as a measure of accuracy: it is read here as verification too soft, which
  is why round 2 re-sent the correctness findings to a reader with a
  different lens. Of the eight that came back, five held and three were
  narrowed; none was refuted. The 98 findings marked "survived one" below
  carry one reader's agreement only, and the 43 non-correctness findings
  were verified in batches, never singly.
- **Finders' coverage was uneven.** Several readers returned after about
  half their budget; what each did not read is in sections 8 and 9, in its
  own words. The absence of a finding for anything a reader did not read
  says nothing about it.
- Severity and confidence are the finder's; the verifier's severity, where
  given, is what the tables use.

## 3. The method

Two workflows over one worktree at `fba5529f`.

- **Round 1** - 29 finders, each a slice with seven lenses (quiet-wrong,
  logic defect, inefficiency the 3.4.* work added, performance opportunity,
  duplication, dead code, architecture), at most five findings each: the
  dispatcher in four line ranges, CX's plans, the statistics stores, the
  step compiler, the step VM, the pool's delta since `0b98bc4f`, the WAL's,
  txn and locks', the catalog's, `sys` views, the scheduler and wait events,
  index/FK/assertion's delta, the expeditor and runtime, the query layer
  and expressions, Cabin and Waystone, the network and wire, the parser,
  and nine architecture and sweep slices (the per-statement hot path,
  layering, instance-wide latches, the dispatcher's shape, the memory
  stores, settings and refusals, two dead-code sweeps, a duplication
  sweep). Each finding went to a refuter: a high-severity correctness
  finding alone, then a second refuter with a different lens if it was
  quiet-wrong and held; the rest in batches per slice.
- **Round 2** - twelve narrow hunts, the quiet-wrong lens only, each along
  one interaction of 3.4.* features, at most three findings, each to two
  refuters in turn; and a second, differently-lensed refuter for the 22
  round-1 correctness findings that had only one.

## 4. What was not read

- **Round 2's five hunts that never ran** (the usage limit):
  `x-recovery-images` (DB + DH + CZ under a crash schedule: owed images,
  the packed codec, redo start; round 1 read only the diff and analysis,
  recovery and the packed codec not at all), `x-ddl-vs-compiled` (a portal
  or chain compiled before a DDL and run after it), `x-prepared-portals`
  (parameter binding, suspension boundaries, typed batches),
  `x-query-layer` (wrong answers against SQL semantics in the layer) and
  `x-advisory-authority` (trails, page epoch, the scan kernel's byte
  reject, the inner build). These are the five largest holes this audit
  leaves.
- Per slice, what each round-1 reader says it did not read is in section 8:
  notably the dispatcher's `MetaSections`, `HandleBackup`, the UPDATE path
  past `UpdateParsed` for the hot-path trace, `step_compiler.cpp` in about
  half its ranges, `wal/analysis.cpp`, `wal/recovery.cpp`, `scram.cpp`,
  `error_registry.cpp`, and parts of `parser.cpp`'s DDL grammar.
- `tests/`, `sim/`, `fuzz/` and `tools/` were read only as evidence for or
  against a claim, never audited.

## 5. Where the findings stand

| Outcome | Count |
|---|---|
| Survived two verifiers | 11 |
| Survived one verifier (no second sent, or the second died) | 98 |
| Already recorded (a verifier found the document) | 7 |
| Not verified (its verifier died) | 1 |
| Refuted | 0 |

By kind, of the 109 that survived: quiet-wrong 6, other logic defects 20,
inefficiency 13, performance 21, architecture 16, duplication 15, dead code
18.

**The five that matter most**, in the order this record would take them up.
Each is set out in full in section 6.

1. **A Cabin set banked after a `READ ONLY` view's snapshot is served to
   it** (`exec-cabin-transition#1`, quiet-wrong, high, two verifiers). CS's
   view carries no transaction id, so the banking gate (`cabin.md` §6a,
   whose own text names the eager-id premise) cannot see it; a set banked
   by a later statement is served to the older view and rows go missing.
2. **A locking read, `UPDATE` or `DELETE` whose `WHERE` holds a subquery
   claims a collected row without testing its `WHERE` again**
   (`x-locking-read#1`, quiet-wrong, high by its second verifier, two verifiers). An undecided
   writer's head matched in pass 1; that writer rolls back; pass 2 claims
   the restored, non-matching version - `FOR UPDATE` returns it, `UPDATE`
   writes it, `DELETE` deletes it. For `UPDATE`/`DELETE` a parked and
   resumed walk reaches it without a race. `txn.md` (lines 901-910) says
   the residue is only ever a retryable refusal. Pre-3.4 in its two writes;
   DC added the third consumer.
3. **Integer literals past 64 bits wrap and are accepted** as an
   assertion's bound and in type arguments (`parser#2`, quiet-wrong,
   medium): `CHECK (SUM(x) <= 18446744073709551716)` enforces `<= 100`.
4. **An unterminated string literal is accepted** (`parser#1`,
   quiet-wrong, narrowed to low): a dropped closing quote swallows a
   `WHERE`. Not reachable over KWP (`C_BIND`'s placeholder scan refuses
   it); reachable on `debug_text_port` and through direct dispatcher
   callers.
5. **The forward FK check refuses `TxnConflict` and logs a false
   lock-protocol warning** when the parent's writer committed above the
   snapshot ceiling (`dispatcher-2#1`, logic defect, medium, two
   verifiers; CP-S2b).

**What the 3.4.* work added that buys nothing**, of the inefficiency
findings: DB's owed-image test takes the pool's structure latch a further
time and looks the frame up again on every write fetch, owed or not (found
independently by two slices); DF-S2 grew the per-core visibility slot from
64 to 72 bytes, so neighbours' slots now straddle shared lines (two slices
independently; unmeasured); since DE-S1 a one-core instance writes load
words nothing reads on every statement; CJ's session wait registry is one
instance-wide mutex held across a view's copy of every session, which
`rules.md` §3a forbids and `known-gaps.md`'s DL section lists as meeting the
rule; CY's `forgotten_` set grows with every `DROP TABLE` for the run.

**Architecture, in one paragraph.** Every source directory but `base` is
one include cycle, with `stats` the hub that 3.4 made a dependency of
`sched`, `storage`, `txn` and `catalog`; the core ceiling and page 0's
format live in `server/`, so `txn` includes the top layer;
`command_dispatcher.cpp` grew 34 % in 3.4 to 15,675 lines, its header's
closure is 130 headers and 101 test translation units recompile on any
change to it; per-statement state lives in about twenty dispatcher members
under a one-statement-per-core premise its own header says no longer holds;
the write walk's arm ladder is written three times (UPDATE, DELETE, FOR
UPDATE); the bounded keyed table is built about ten times with seven full-
table rules; a committed `DROP TABLE` reaches the memory stores through a
hand-kept list written twice, which one store already missed; about 57
dispatcher refusals are bare `"ERR ..."` strings that reach a KWP client as
`InvalidArgument` whatever they are.

Read with that: `docs/pending/multicore-scaling-cores4-below-cores2.md` is
the open question the latch and line findings bear on, and DL is the
milestone written for the statistics stores' record paths.

## 6. The findings that survived

Ordered by kind, then by the severity the last verifier gave. Every `path:line` is at `fba5529f`. A finding's text is its reader's; where a verifier narrowed it, the narrowed claim is what stands.

## 6.1 Quiet-wrong (6)

### H1. A Cabin set banked after a reader's snapshot is served to that reader: a REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY transaction loses rows

- **Kind**: quiet-wrong; severity high (the finder's: high, confidence high); round 1, `exec-cabin-transition` (`exec-cabin-transition#1`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/step_vm.cpp:950`; origin: CS-S1/S2 (adbea55b, 3.4.x) made it reachable by plain statements; the missing serve-side age check is pre-3.4
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**; second verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** A banked set is a superset only for snapshots at or after the banking walk's snapshot, but CabinStore::Find takes no view and the serve path never compares the reader's snapshot with the set's. The banking gate was the only protection, and it relied on every older view belonging to a transaction with an id. cabin.md section 6a names that assumption: Begin allocates an id eagerly "for read-only and REPEATABLE READ transactions alike", and if that stops, "the break returns, and no test fails". CS's BEGIN READ ONLY holds a view across statements with "no id ... no in-flight slot" (command_dispatcher.cpp:12887-12895), so in_flight_at_mint and AnyUnresolved() no longer see it. CS-Q11 examined a READ ONLY view banking a set, not being served one.

**Evidence.** step_vm.cpp:950 `if (const stats::CabinSet set = cabins_->Find(*key); set.valid()) {` goes straight to ServeFromCabin. cabin_store.cpp:112 `CabinSet CabinStore::Find(const CabinKey& key)` has no view parameter. command_dispatcher.cpp:13222-13224 runs a READ ONLY transaction's SELECT on `ro->view`, and EnsureStatementBoundary (8992-9003) re-mints it only under READ COMMITTED. grep finds no snapshot_lsn or in_flight_at_mint test in step_vm.cpp outside WalkAndRecord's banking gate (1026, 1088). No file under tests/ with cabin in its name mentions READ ONLY.

**Scenario.** Relation t has row R (k=1, v='a') and a Cabin on t(v) that a statement's arm reaches. S1: `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY; SELECT ...` mints a view at L1. S2 (autocommit): `UPDATE t SET v='b' WHERE k=1` (or DELETE) commits at L2; 'a' is unobserved, so the hook appends nothing to it. S3 (autocommit) runs `SELECT * FROM t WHERE v='a'` twice. The second run passes the gate (S1 holds no id, nothing is unresolved, CommitCeiling equals its snapshot), walks, and banks an empty set for 'a'. S1 then runs `SELECT * FROM t WHERE v='a'`: Find serves the empty set and returns 0 rows, although R is visible at L1 with v='a'. The window is the transaction's life, up to the 60 s ceiling. CabinOptimizerExecutor::BuildSeededSets banks under the same gate. A suspended or yielding autocommit SELECT with an inner Cabin probe is the same hazard in a narrower, pre-3.4 form.

**Suggested.** Stamp each set at bank time with the banking view's snapshot_lsn, and have Find (or the three serve sites) answer "not observed" when the reader's view.snapshot_lsn is below it, so the reader walks. Alternatively make the banking gate decline while ReadHorizon() is below the banking snapshot. Add a contract test with a REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY view held across a bank, and correct cabin.md section 6a's eager-id paragraph.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f; nothing built or run. I traced the path the claim needs and found no guard. (1) The held view has no id. `BeginReadOnly` (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:12887-12905) calls `txn::AutocommitSnapshot`, not `TransactionManager::Begin`: a view minted with `kNoTrxId` plus a reader lease. `RegisterReader` (src/txn/manager.cpp:763-781) only stores the snapshot LSN in a reader slot and calls `PublishCoreBounds`, which publishes `OldestActiveTrxId()` from `live_` (manager.cpp:637-661). `cores_with_unresolved_` moves only in `PublishOldestUnresolved` on a trx-id transition (src/txn/instance_visibility.cpp:46-51). So a READ ONLY transaction never makes `AnyUnresolved()` true; it holds the horizon only. (2) The view is kept under REPEATABLE READ. `EnsureStatementBoundary` (command_dispatcher.cpp:8992-9003) re-mints only for `kReadCommitted`, and the SELECT's snapshot is `ro->view` (13222-13224). (3) The banking gate passes for a later autocommit SELECT. `MintCheckView` sets `in_flight_at_mint = AnyUnresolved()` (manager.cpp:106), false with only the READ ONLY session open. `WalkAndRecord` (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1026-1027, 1085-1088) tests `in_flight_at_mint`, `own_trx_id`, `AnyUnresolved()` and `CommitCeiling() != snapshot_lsn`; all pass once the writer has committed before the banker's mint. None of them asks about an older registered reader or `ReadHorizon()`. (4) The banked set reflects the banker's snapshot only. Recording evaluates the Cabin's conjunct on the decoded visible version (`version_`, step_vm.cpp:2916-2948), so a row moved off 'a' or deleted at L2 is not recorded. The write hook leaves an unobserved value alone (docs/spec/cabin.md §5, lines 405-407). (5) The serve side has no age check. `CabinStore::Find(const CabinKey&)` (src/stats/cabin_store.cpp:112-129) takes no view and declines only an announced build. step_vm.cpp:950-954 goes straight to `ServeFromCabin`, which iterates the set's entries (2128-2150); an empty set yields zero rows. grep of include/kds/stats/cabin_store.hpp finds no banked LSN or snapshot stamp on a set. The only `in_flight_at_mint` readers are step_vm.cpp:1026 and src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:145, both bank-side. (6) No document states this as a gap; they assert the opposite. cabin.md §1 (lines 58-63) claims the superset for "every active snapshot". cabin.md §6a (lines 542-549) names the eager-id assumption and says that if it stops "the break returns, and no test fails". docs/spec/txn.md:192-194 and CS-Q11 (instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-cs-read-only-transaction.md:270) examine only a bank FROM a READ ONLY view ("a view that passes both is indistinguishable from an autocommit view"), never an older READ ONLY view being SERVED a set banked after its snapshot. The known-gaps.md Cabin entries (536-546, 964-971) do not cover it. tests/read_only_txn_test.cpp mentions Cabin only at lines 245-246, as `CREATE CABIN` / `DROP CABIN` statement strings; no test holds a READ ONLY view across a bank. Not verified: the finder's side remarks that `BuildSeededSets` has the same exposure and that a yielding autocommit SELECT with an inner Cabin probe is a pre-3.4 form of it. I did not trace either. Reachability also needs the reader's statement to take the Cabin arm: an operator-declared Cabin, or a controller-made one named by an administrator's plan (cabin.md 664-668). Severity high: a REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY snapshot silently loses rows, for up to the 60 s transaction ceiling.

**Second verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing built or run. Code path, read end to end: - Serve side takes no view. src/exec/step_vm.cpp:950 `if (const stats::CabinSet set = cabins_->Find(*key); set.valid())` goes straight to ServeFromCabin (2128 ff.), which only iterates the set's entries and filters them by visibility; an entry absent from the set is never looked for. src/stats/cabin_store.cpp:112-128 `CabinStore::Find(const CabinKey&)` has no view parameter and declines only for an announced, still-building set. include/kds/stats/cabin_store.hpp carries no bank-time LSN or snapshot stamp on a set (grep for snapshot/lsn/view finds only comments). In step_vm.cpp, snapshot_lsn / in_flight_at_mint / AnyUnresolved / CommitCeiling appear only in the banking gate (1026-1027, 1087-1088). The key-set serve site (861-873) is the same shape. - Banking gate does not see a READ ONLY holder. src/txn/manager.cpp:106 `view.in_flight_at_mint = visibility_->AnyUnresolved()`, and include/kds/txn/instance_visibility.hpp:372-373 AnyUnresolved is `cores_with_unresolved_ != 0`, i.e. transactions with an id only. Reader leases and ReadHorizon() are not consulted by the gate or by cabin_store. src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:145 uses the same test. - CS made a held view with no id. src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:12887-12895 BeginReadOnly: "No id is drawn, no record appended, no in-flight slot taken"; 13222-13224 runs the transaction's SELECT on `ro->view`; EnsureStatementBoundary 8992-9001 re-mints only under kReadCommitted, so a REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY view is held for the transaction's life. Nothing on that path withholds the Cabin store from the statement. The record: - docs/spec/cabin.md section 6a (lines 541-549) names exactly this assumption: Begin allocates an id eagerly "for read-only and REPEATABLE READ transactions alike"; if not, "a session holding a pre-record view stops being visible to this guard, the break returns, and no test fails". The paragraph is still worded as if the assumption held, so the spec does not record the gap, it records the premise CS broke. - instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-cs-read-only-transaction.md CS-Q11 (line 270) and its census (line 342) examine only the READ ONLY view as the banker ("a view that passes both is indistinguishable from an autocommit view minted at the same instant"); docs/spec/txn.md:192 repeats that. Neither considers a READ ONLY view being served a set banked after its snapshot. - docs/inflight/known-gaps.md Cabin entries (536-546, 964-971, 1619-1650) cover build rarity, orphan sets and controller keying; none covers this. docs/pending/lazy-id-assignment-census.md lists the bank's kNoTrxId decline as a site only. The F and G audit files have no Cabin entry on served-to-older-view. - Tests: grep for REPEATABLE / READ ONLY in tests/*cabin*.cpp returns nothing; tests/read_only_txn_test.cpp mentions Cabin only as refused DDL (245-246). No test pins the opposite. Reachability: BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY is plain SQL; an operator's CREATE CABIN column Cabin is on the ladder. The scenario's steps follow from append-only maintenance (cabin_store.hpp rule 1: UPDATE v to v' leaves v alone and appends nothing to an unobserved v), so a set for 'a' banked after the committed UPDATE lacks R, and the held L1 view is served it. Limits on what I established: default isolation is READ COMMITTED (include/kds/txn/manager.hpp:168), so the full-window form needs an explicit REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY transaction. I did not trace the finder's secondary claim (a yielding autocommit SELECT with an inner Cabin probe, pre-3.4), nor why the bank happens on S3's second run rather than its first; neither affects the main claim. Severity high because it is a quiet wrong answer the spec itself predicted; the trigger is narrow (operator Cabin, explicit REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY, a first bank of that value inside the transaction's life, at most the 60 s ceiling).

### H2. A collected target is claimed without its WHERE being tested again, so a holder's rollback between the two passes makes a locking read return (and an UPDATE or DELETE write) a row that matches in no committed state

- **Kind**: quiet-wrong; severity high (the finder's: medium, confidence medium); round 2, `x-locking-read` (`x-locking-read#1`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11571`; origin: The collection pass at 7e7bb45f (2026-10-14, before v3.3.0); kept unchanged through F4 (4f5b2e17) and F26's WriteWalk fold (3f8c499c) in 3.4.*; DC (v3.4.14) added the locking read as a third consumer at RunLockingRead :12699-12716. docs/spec/txn.md:901-910 (written with F4) states the residue as 'never a wrong answer'.
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**; second verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Where the WHERE holds a subquery, pass 1 (CollectTargets) collects a row whose page version matches even when that version was written by an undecided transaction, and pass 2 claims every collected key with no WHERE test. If that writer rolls back and releases between the two visits of the row, pass 2 meets the restored version - visible, unheld, and not matching - and claims it. SELECT ... FOR UPDATE returns the row, UPDATE overwrites it, DELETE delete-marks it, with no refusal. The spec says this residue is only ever a retryable refusal; it considered the holder committing, not rolling back, and describes the collection as by the snapshot's version when Qualify tests the page's head first.

**As narrowed by verification.** Holds as written. Addition: for UPDATE and DELETE no cross-core race is needed. A statement that has already written a row and parks mid-walk on the held row resumes with its collected targets (ParkedOutcome :11749, RunWalkWrite :10857-10863, WriteWalk built with resume_from.targets :11930/:15427). After the holder's ROLLBACK the resumed walk writes or delete-marks the restored, non-matching row without testing its WHERE (:11571). The locking read always re-runs whole and collects afresh, so only the between-passes race reaches it.

**Evidence.** [source-read] Pass 1: WriteWalk::CollectTargets (command_dispatcher.cpp:11676-11693) calls Qualify with targets == nullptr. Qualify (:11521-11606) skips only kNoVersion (:11541); a head by an invisible writer with an undo pointer is kNeedsUndoWalk (include/kds/txn/visibility.hpp:70-77) and is tested on the page's bytes: MatchesWhere true returns the row at :11572-11574 and CollectTargets inserts its id at :11685. Nothing records which writer's version matched. Pass 2: RunLockingRead :12703-12712 visits with the claim lambda (:12661-12670); Qualify now has targets set and returns the row at :11571 ('A collected target matched in the collection pass, and its WHERE is not evaluated again') with the restored head's trx_id. Claim (:11619-11663): BorrowChain grants the tuple U because the holder released; CheckWriteConflictBlocking -> TransactionManager::CheckWriteConflict (src/txn/manager.cpp:202) passes because the restored writer is visible; through_undo is false, so the Corruption guard at :11637 does not fire. The emit pass's DC-R1 re-check (:12744-12751) asks only present / not deleted / writer visible, all true, and the row is encoded and emitted. Rollback order making the window real: TransactionManager::Abort compensates every trail entry first (manager.cpp:544-551; the overwrite arm restores prior_trx_id and prior_undo_ptr, :516-519), then RetireInFlight (:568), then releases the borrows last (:583). The two passes are two whole-relation walks in one synchronous statement, so the window is the rest of pass 1 plus pass 2 up to the row, during which other cores run. The guard would have to be at :11571 (or a writer stamp kept with each collected id) and is not there. UpdateParsed runs the same two passes (:12527-12550) and, per parser-v2.md:228, checks its WHERE once; I did not read UPDATE's apply or DELETE's mark end to end, so the write-side consequence rests on the shared Qualify/Claim and that spec line. No test cell pins either outcome: the only mention of the collection pass under tests/ and sim/ is tests/locking_read_test.cpp:275, a quiet-instance cell.

**Scenario.** Schema: CREATE TABLE t (id int64, v int64) with many rows, row 7 committed as v = 0; CREATE TABLE s (k int64, t_id int64) holding t_id = 7. cores >= 2. Session B: BEGIN; UPDATE t SET v = 1 WHERE id = 7; (undecided, holds U on t:7, head is B's version over v = 0). Session A on another core: BEGIN; SELECT * FROM t WHERE v = 1 AND id IN (SELECT t_id FROM s) FOR UPDATE; - pass 1 reaches row 7, Classify is kNeedsUndoWalk, the head v = 1 matches, id 7 is collected, and pass 1 walks on. Session B: ROLLBACK - row 7 is restored to v = 0 under its prior writer, B is retired and its U released. Session A's pass 2 reaches row 7: visible, in targets, no WHERE test; U granted; conflict check passes; the emit returns (7, 0). Wrong: the reply holds a row with v = 0 for WHERE v = 1, and A holds a claim on it. Right: row 7 is not returned and not claimed - it has v = 0 in A's view and in every committed state. The same interleaving with UPDATE t SET ... WHERE v = 1 AND id IN (SELECT t_id FROM s) writes row 7, and with DELETE delete-marks it, durably once A commits. Had B committed instead, pass 2 refuses TxnConflict (correct); had B still been undecided, A parks and re-runs whole (correct).

**Suggested.** Make pass 2 able to tell that the version it meets is the one pass 1 judged. Keep each collected id with the head's (trx_id, undo_ptr) read in pass 1, and in Qualify's targets arm compare them with the tuple's: a mismatch is answered as a conflict a re-run gets past - a statement that wrote nothing is re-run whole by DispatchAsync and collects afresh, one that wrote rows keeps the retryable refusal. That also turns the documented 'holder commits' residue into the same re-run. For the locking read alone, which holds its leaves shared and writes nothing, re-evaluating the WHERE in pass 2 is the other option. Correct docs/spec/txn.md:901-910 either way: the collection is by the page's version first, and the rollback case is not a refusal today.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f, worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. I looked for a guard on every step of the path and found none. Pass 1 collects the uncommitted head: - `WriteWalk::CollectTargets` (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11676-11693) calls `Qualify` with `targets == nullptr`. - `Qualify` drops only `kNoVersion` (:11541). A head by an invisible writer with an undo pointer is `kNeedsUndoWalk` (include/kds/txn/visibility.hpp `ClassifyFor`). - That head is tested on the page's bytes (:11562-11574); a match returns the row and only its id is kept (:11685). No writer or undo stamp is recorded. Pass 2 claims without a WHERE test: - With `targets` set, `Qualify` returns at :11571 before `MatchesWhere`, carrying the restored head's `trx_id` and `through_undo == false`. - `Claim` (:11619-11663): `BorrowChain` grants once the holder released, so `blocker == 0`. - `CheckWriteConflictBlocking` (:13817) calls `TransactionManager::CheckWriteConflict` (src/txn/manager.cpp:194-213), which is OK because the restored prior writer is visible. - The Corruption guard at :11637 needs `through_undo`, so it does not fire. The locking read's emit does not catch it: the DC-R1 re-check (:12744-12751) asks only present, not deleted, and writer admitted. It never asks the WHERE. The rollback order makes the window real: `Abort` (manager.cpp:524-585) compensates first (`CompensateOverwrite` restores image, `prior_trx_id`, `prior_undo_ptr`), then `RetireInFlight`, then releases the borrows last. If pass 2 arrives before the release, the borrow is refused and the statement parks and re-runs whole, collecting afresh (correct). If it arrives after, the row is claimed. Guards I checked and ruled out: - `DeclaredWriteBorrow` declares nothing for `v = 1 AND id IN (subquery)`. - A read takes no row unit, so pass 1 never waits on the holder. - `RunLockingRead` always builds its walk with `resumed_targets = nullptr` (:12643), so only the between-passes race reaches it. - UPDATE's `apply` (:12082-12096) and DELETE's `mark` (:15449-15485) call the same `Qualify` and `Claim` and make no further WHERE test. The write side is wider than the finder stated, and needs no race. A statement that has written rows and then parks on the held row returns `ParkedOutcome` (:11749-11758) with `walk_cursor.targets` (:12531, :15657). `RunWalkWrite` resumes it with the same snapshot and cursor (:10857-10863), and the walk is rebuilt with `resume_from.targets` (:11930, :15427). `CollectTargets` then returns at once (:11677). If the holder rolls back during the park, the resumed walk meets the restored, non-matching version, skips the WHERE at :11571, and writes or delete-marks it. That is a plain two-session sequence. Not already known: - docs/spec/txn.md:901-910 names only the holder-commits residue and asserts "never a wrong answer". It also says the pass collects by the snapshot's version, where the code tests the page's head first. - known-gaps.md:2748-2756 and docs/pending/keyed-write-beside-a-subquery.md cover only the cost of the two walks. - F4's entry and raft-marks-2026-11-15 §1 do not state the rollback case. - The only test mention is the quiet-instance cell at tests/locking_read_test.cpp:275. Severity: medium. It needs a WHERE holding a subquery, a concurrent holder whose uncommitted version matches where the committed one does not, and that holder's rollback. When those meet, the wrong row is returned or durably written with no refusal.

**Second verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] In worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture at fba5529f, nothing built or run. I looked for a guard, a record and a test, and found none. Code path: - Pass 1: WriteWalk::CollectTargets (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11676-11693) calls Qualify with targets == nullptr. Qualify skips only kNoVersion (:11541). An undecided overwriter's head is kNeedsUndoWalk (include/kds/txn/visibility.hpp:64-78) and is tested on the page's bytes (:11572-11574). A match is inserted by id only (:11685); no writer stamp is kept and Claim is not called in this pass. - Pass 2: Qualify returns any collected id at :11571 with the head's current trx_id and no WHERE test. Claim (:11619-11663) is granted the unit once the holder has released. CheckWriteConflict (src/txn/manager.cpp:194-203) passes because the restored writer is visible. through_undo is false, so the Corruption guard at :11637 does not fire. - Rollback order: TransactionManager::Abort compensates every trail entry (manager.cpp:544-551, CompensateOverwrite restores prior_trx_id and prior_undo_ptr), then RetireInFlight (:568), then releases the borrows (:583). A waiter or a later claimant therefore sees the restored, non-matching version, visible and unheld. - Locking read: RunLockingRead :12699-12716 runs the two passes. Its emit re-check (:12744-12751) asks only present, not deleted and writer admitted, so the row is emitted. - UPDATE and DELETE, which the finder did not read end to end: UPDATE's apply (:12082-12096) and DELETE's (:15450-15482) go Qualify, then Claim, then write, with no WHERE test between. Reachability: - The statement can be written. DC-Q5 admits 'the WHERE an UPDATE admits, a subquery in it included' (instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-dc-select-for-update.md:410), and tests/locking_read_test.cpp:275 runs a collection pass. - No declared window exists unless the WHERE bounds the tuple key (:12646), so nothing makes the reader wait for the holder before pass 1. - The locking read needs cores >= 2: its two passes carry no suspension, so the rollback must run on another core between them. - UPDATE and DELETE need no race. A statement that has written rows and parks on the held row resumes with its collected targets (ParkedOutcome :11749-11758, WriteWalk built from resume_from.targets at :11930 and :15427). After the holder's ROLLBACK the resumed walk writes or delete-marks the restored row. tests/lock_family_test.cpp:2916-2943 drives exactly this park, rollback and resume on one dispatcher. Record: - docs/spec/txn.md:901-910 calls the residue 'a retryable refusal ... never a wrong answer', says the pass collects 'by its snapshot's version', and considers only the holder committing. The spec asserts the opposite of what the code does, so this is not an accepted cost. - docs/inflight/known-gaps.md 'Locking reads (DC)' (:2899-2970) and its one collection-pass entry (:2748-2756, which is about cost) do not record it. - F4's entry and raft-marks-2026-11-15.md cover the head-fails case only; :12 of that ruling leaves 'the residues txn.md §5 names' unsettled. - docs/pending/keyed-write-beside-a-subquery.md is about keying the passes. Tests: none pins either outcome. The lock_family_test.cpp:2916 cell's WHERE does not read the held row's changed column, so the row matches before and after the rollback. The four F4 cells use no subquery. Severity high rather than the finder's medium: the UPDATE/DELETE variant is deterministic and commits a write chosen by another transaction's rolled-back value. It is narrowed in practice by needing a subquery in the WHERE plus a conjunct on a column the holder changed.

### H3. Integer literals past 64 bits wrap and are accepted in an assertion's bound and in type arguments (char/varchar width, decimal precision/scale, SPARE)

- **Kind**: quiet-wrong; severity medium (the finder's: medium, confidence high); round 1, `parser` (`parser#2`)
- **Where**: `src/parser/parser.cpp:2281`; origin: pre-3.4 (blame: boundary commit 1a34afbe for both sites; SPARE's use of ParseTypeArgument is BW)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Token::int_val is documented as a decode that silently wraps past 64 bits (lexer.cpp:173-182, token.hpp:180). ParsePaginationCount was rewritten to decode from digits() for exactly this reason (parser.cpp:3177-3180: 'a wrapped count silently means a number the client did not write, so past-uint64 refuses instead'), but two other sites still read int_val: the assertion bound (2281-2290) and ParseTypeArgument (217-229), which serves char(N)/varchar(N), decimal(p,s), CAST targets and CREATE TABLE ... SPARE n (1807). A literal of 2^64 + k is accepted as k. This is the truthfulness rule's case: a spelling is accepted and something else is enforced.

**Evidence.** parser.cpp:2281 `if (bound.type != TokenType::kIntLit || bound.negative || bound.int_val < 0) {` ... 2290 `stmt.bound = bound.int_val;`. parser.cpp:224 `if (tok.int_val < 0 || tok.int_val > 0xFFFF) {` ... 229 `return static_cast<std::uint32_t>(tok.int_val);` under the comment 'what a syntax layer can say is that the digits fit at all'. No consumer outside the parser re-reads the digits (grep for bound_byte_offset / raw digits in src/exec/assertion_*.cpp: none; AssertionStmt::bound is std::int64_t, ast.hpp:1227).

**Scenario.** `CREATE ASSERTION cap ON ledger CHECK (SUM(amount) <= 18446744073709551716)` (2^64 + 100): the lexer's unsigned accumulation wraps to 100, int_val = 100 passes `>= 0`, stmt.bound = 100. The assertion is created and enforces SUM(amount) <= 100, refusing writes the declared bound admits, while sys.assertions.source_text shows the declaration verbatim with the 20-digit bound. Same mechanism: `varchar(18446744073709551632)` creates a column of inline width 16; `decimal(18446744073709551634, 2)` creates decimal(18,2); `SPARE 18446744073709551620` reserves 4 slots. A bound of exactly 2^63..2^64-1 is refused, but with the misleading 'non-negative integer literal' message.

**Suggested.** Decode both from Token::digits() with std::from_chars as ParsePaginationCount does (one shared helper returning uint64 or a refusal at the literal's byte), then apply the range check; for the assertion bound refuse above INT64_MAX by name.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. src/parser/lexer.cpp:173-182 accumulates the digit run in a uint64 that wraps, then casts to int64 (`tok.int_val = static_cast<std::int64_t>(tok.negative ? 0 - v : v)`); the digit scan at lexer.cpp:140-147 has no length cap. include/kds/parser/token.hpp:180-181 says the same (`int_val` 'silently wraps past 64 bits, so a range check ... needs the digits themselves'). Assertion bound: src/parser/parser.cpp:2280-2291 tests only `bound.type != kIntLit || bound.negative || bound.int_val < 0` and then `stmt.bound = bound.int_val`; 2^64+100 has digits 18446744073709551716, wraps to 100, passes. `bound_byte_offset` is read only at parser.cpp:2309 (grep over src and include: parser.cpp:2291, 2309, ast.hpp:1228), so nothing downstream re-reads the digits; AssertionStmt::bound is int64. Type argument: parser.cpp:215-229 checks `tok.int_val < 0 || tok.int_val > 0xFFFF` on the wrapped value and returns it; callers are width (185), precision (194), scale (201) and SPARE (1807). The contrast is real: ParsePaginationCount (parser.cpp:3177-3199) decodes digits() with from_chars for exactly this reason, and parser.cpp:503 notes 'wraps past INT64_MAX; the digits are the truth'. No guard found: no test pins a wrapped assertion bound or type argument (grep of tests for 20-digit literals finds only pagination, INSERT values, types coercion, key tests), and no known-gaps or docs/pending entry states it. It contradicts a spec sentence: docs/spec/types.md:144-147 'an integer literal that wrapped int64 is refused wherever `int_val` is the value'. Not traced: whether the catalog/type registry rejects any wrapped width later - but a wrapped value inside the legal range (16 for varchar, 18 for a precision, 4 for SPARE) is by construction indistinguishable from a written one. Practical likelihood is low (needs a 20+ digit literal), the effect is an enforced bound/width other than the one written with source_text showing the written one.

### H4. An unterminated string literal is accepted as a literal, so a missing closing quote swallows the WHERE and the UPDATE writes every row

- **Kind**: quiet-wrong; severity low (the finder's: high, confidence high); round 1, `parser` (`parser#1`)
- **Where**: `src/parser/lexer.cpp:135`; origin: pre-3.4 (9cde1b45, initial commit; pinned by tests/lexer_test.cpp:69 UnterminatedStringReadsToEnd)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**; second verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The lexer returns kStrLit for a quote that is never closed, with the rest of the statement as its text and no error. The parser has no check for it, so text the client meant as SQL becomes part of a string value and the statement runs as something other than what was written. No pre-parse quote-balance guard was found in src/server or src/exec (grep; only BACKUP's own argument scanner refuses an unterminated string).

**As narrowed by verification.** The lexer accepts an unterminated string literal as a kStrLit running to end of input (src/parser/lexer.cpp:129-136, pinned by tests/lexer_test.cpp:69 and tests/testdata/parser_corpus.txt:166-169 as "surprising, not right"), and the parser has no check, so a dropped closing quote can swallow a WHERE and make an UPDATE or DELETE write every row. This is NOT reachable over the default KWP port: every statement must pass C_BIND, whose ScanPlaceholders refuses a statement that ends inside a string literal (src/server/kwp_session.cpp:164-173, called from OnBind at :594, the only portal creation at :610). It is reachable only through the newline protocol on debug_text_port (loopback, off unless configured) and through direct CommandDispatcher callers (sim, tests, tools on the text port). The protection on the wire lives in the bind-time placeholder scan, not in the grammar, is stated in no spec, and no test pinning that refusal was found; the lexer fix the finder suggests would make the refusal uniform and carry the opening quote's byte.

**Evidence.** lexer.cpp:132-135: `while (pos_ < src_.size() && src_[pos_] != '\'') ++pos_;` ... `tok.type = TokenType::kStrLit;` `if (pos_ < src_.size() && src_[pos_] == '\'') ++pos_;  // consume closing quote`. parser.cpp:455-458 EndsSetValue accepts kEof after a SET literal; parser.cpp:3675 ParseOptionalWhere returns no conjunct when WHERE is absent. grep for 'unterminated|closing quote' in docs/, manual/, instructions/ returns nothing, so no spec states this as a contract.

**Scenario.** `UPDATE accounts SET note = 'reviewed WHERE id = 5` (closing quote dropped). Lexed as UPDATE accounts SET note = <string "reviewed WHERE id = 5"> <EOF>. ParseSetValue takes the literal (EndsSetValue on kEof), no WHERE is seen, the trailing-token check passes at EOF, and the UPDATE has an empty WHERE: every row of accounts gets note = 'reviewed WHERE id = 5' and the client receives a success tag with the full row count. PostgreSQL refuses the same text as a syntax error. Milder forms of the same defect: `DELETE FROM t WHERE name = 'x AND id = 5` runs as name = 'x AND id = 5'; `SELECT ... WHERE s = 'abc` answers for 'abc'.

**Suggested.** Make the lexer return kError (or a dedicated token) when the closing quote is missing, so every production refuses it at the opening quote's byte; update LexerTest.UnterminatedStringReadsToEnd and add a parser test for the UPDATE case. The fingerprint already drops a statement on kError, so no stored hash moves for a statement that parses.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. I traced the path the claim needs and found no guard. (1) Lexer: src/parser/lexer.cpp:129-137 scans from the opening quote to end of input, sets kStrLit, and consumes a closing quote only if one is there; no kError and no flag is set for the missing quote. kError comes only from the default arm at lexer.cpp:278. (2) Parser, for `UPDATE accounts SET note = 'reviewed WHERE id = 5`: ParseUpdate (src/parser/parser.cpp:3647) -> ParseAssignmentList (3610) -> ParseSetValue (513), whose kStrLit arm reads the literal and returns OK because EndsSetValue (455-459) accepts kEof. Back in ParseUpdate, the next token is kEof, so the FROM test fails, ParseOptionalWhere (1498-1504) returns an empty conjunct list, ParseReturning and ConsumeOptionalSemicolon see EOF. The statement is an UpdateStmt with an empty where, which include/kds/parser/ast.hpp:734 defines as 'no WHERE clause'. (3) No guard elsewhere: every SQL entry point outside src/parser constructs the same parser::Lexer / parser::Parser (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:4036, 4269, 14233-14571 and others; src/exec/query_layer.cpp:880). A grep for unterminated / closing quote / unclosed over src and include finds only BACKUP's own argument scanner (command_dispatcher.cpp:2026). No refusal of a WHERE-less UPDATE exists in src, include, parser-v2.md or known-gaps.md; manual/sql/sql.md:1047 states an empty WHERE marks every row. (4) Not recorded as a gap: grep for 'unterminated' over docs/spec, docs/rules, docs/inflight and docs/pending returns nothing, so neither known-gaps.md nor the F1-F32 / G1-G66 audits carry it. manual/sql/sql.md:875 and :2351 state only 'no quote escaping'. The behaviour is known at test level: tests/testdata/parser_corpus.txt:166-169 pins `SELECT * FROM t WHERE s = 'unterminated` as `ok` with the comment 'Pinned because it is surprising, not because it is right'; tests/lexer_test.cpp:69, tests/parser_fuzz_test.cpp:87 and tests/plan_catalog_test.cpp:61 also pin it. None of these states the UPDATE/DELETE consequence, and a test-data comment is not a spec or gap entry, so this is confirmed rather than already-known. Origin is pre-3.4: `git log v3.4.0..HEAD -- src/parser/lexer.cpp` is empty. Severity is my own: medium rather than the finder's high. The trigger is a malformed client statement (a dropped closing quote on the last literal before the WHERE, into a string-typed column wide enough to take the swallowed text), not a wrong answer to well-formed SQL. I checked the more likely apostrophe-in-value forms (`'it's fine' WHERE ...`, `'boss'' WHERE ...`) by reading the grammar: they leave an identifier or a second literal directly after the first, which is not an EndsSetValue token, so they should fall into the expression chain and be refused; I did not trace that chain to its refusal. When it does trigger, the effect is the worst kind: an autocommit write to every row answered with a success tag, against CLAUDE.md's working rule 'never accept a spelling and enforce something other than what was written'. The fix needs the lexer test, the corpus line and the fuzz seed updated together.

**Second verifier (narrowed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. The lexer half holds: src/parser/lexer.cpp:129-136 returns kStrLit for a quote that is never closed, with no error; EndsSetValue (src/parser/parser.cpp:455-458) accepts kEof after the literal and the UPDATE production takes an absent WHERE (parser.cpp:3674-3676). Tests pin it rather than the opposite: tests/lexer_test.cpp:69-73, tests/testdata/parser_corpus.txt:166-169 ("Pinned because it is surprising, not because it is right"), tests/plan_catalog_test.cpp:61-66, tests/parser_fuzz_test.cpp:87. No spec states it as a contract and docs/inflight/known-gaps.md has no entry for it (only the quote-in-a-bound-parameter gap at known-gaps.md:1338 and protocol.md:84; manual/sql/sql.md:2351 says only that there is no quote escaping), so it is not already-known. The reachability half of the claim is wrong. The finder's statement that no pre-parse quote-balance guard exists in src/server is false: src/server/kwp_session.cpp:152-175 ScanPlaceholders toggles in_string on every quote outside a `--` comment and returns InvalidArgument "C_BIND: the statement ends inside a string literal" (line 171-173) when the text ends inside one. Substitute calls it (kwp_session.cpp:181, 200) and OnBind calls Substitute for every bind, with or without parameters (kwp_session.cpp:594-595). A portal is created at exactly one place, after that check (kwp_session.cpp:610), and OnExecute runs portals only (455, 659); KWP has no simple-query frame (docs/spec/protocol.md:65). The scan's comment and quote rules match the lexer's (lexer.cpp:82-93 skips `--` only between tokens, a string runs to the next quote), so an odd quote count for the lexer is an odd count for the scan. Over the default port, `UPDATE accounts SET note = 'reviewed WHERE id = 5` therefore gets S_PARSE_OK and is then refused at C_BIND; it never executes. The finder's grep for 'unterminated|closing quote' missed this wording. What remains reachable: the newline protocol on debug_text_port (loopback, 0 = off by default, protocol.md:3 and :159) and callers that hand text straight to CommandDispatcher (sim, tests, tools driving the text port), where no quote guard exists (the only other quote scanners in src/server are BACKUP's at command_dispatcher.cpp:2008-2026 and a document scanner at 3873-3897). Not verified: no test under tests/ was found that pins the C_BIND refusal itself (grep for its message in tests/, sim/ returned nothing), so the guard that protects the wire is untested.

### H5. An INSERT's shape ends at its first parenthesised group, so ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE, RETURNING and an INSERT ... SELECT's source hash to the plain INSERT's pattern_id and fetch_id

- **Kind**: quiet-wrong; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `parser` (`parser#3`)
- **Where**: `src/parser/fingerprint.cpp:272`; origin: BI5's rule is pre-3.4 (9fdf3351); the grammar it now silently covers came later: ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING / RETURNING / INSERT ... SELECT in BU-S8, DO UPDATE SET in CG (b7ddff92, 3.4.*)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Once first_group_closed_ is set, Feed folds literals into arg_hash and nothing into shape_ or fetch_. BI5 justified this by 'a storable 1-row statement has nothing but ;/EOF after its group' (fingerprint.hpp:331-333). That premise is no longer true: the tail may now hold `ON CONFLICT (col) DO UPDATE SET <expressions>`, `DO NOTHING`, `RETURNING <items>`, more rows, or - for a column-listed statement, whose first group is the column list - `SELECT ... FROM ... WHERE ...` in full. Statements with different meaning therefore share one pattern_id and fetch_id. bulkinsert.md section 2.4 states the column-list consequence for VALUES rows only; neither it, parser-v2.md, known-gaps.md nor the CG work order states that a conflict arm, RETURNING or a SELECT source is outside the shape.

**As narrowed by verification.** The mechanism holds: src/parser/fingerprint.cpp:272-297 folds only literals into arg_hash once first_group_closed_ is set (set at 299-304), and an INSERT's fetch_id is its shape (fingerprint.cpp:406 `out.fetch_id = select_head_ ? fetch_ : shape_`), so `INSERT INTO t VALUES (..)` and the same statement with `ON CONFLICT ... DO NOTHING / DO UPDATE SET ...` and/or `RETURNING ...` share one pattern_id and fetch_id, and include/kds/parser/fingerprint.hpp:331-333's premise ('a storable 1-row statement has nothing but `;`/EOF after its group') is stale. What it reaches is narrower than written: the statistics key is `(fetch_id, kind, binding)` (docs/spec/sys-views.md:174), so INSERT ... SELECT statements over different source relations are separated by their binding (DI), and only statements with the same binding collide - a plain insert and an upsert/RETURNING insert into one relation, or two column-listed INSERT ... SELECT with the same list and the same source relations but different select text. The effect is one statistics row (calls, cost, conflicts, shape_text of the first seen) for statements of different meaning; no row result, trail or plan is affected (bulkinsert.md §2.4: 'the fingerprint's only consumer here is workload statistics'). This is a statistics-granularity and documentation gap, not a quiet-wrong: bulkinsert.md §2.4 states 'the shape ends at the first parenthesised group' as the deliberate rule for row count and the column list, but no document states that a conflict arm, RETURNING or a SELECT source's text is outside the shape.

**Evidence.** fingerprint.cpp:272-297: `if (first_group_closed_) { switch (tok.type) { case TokenType::kIntLit: ... default: return; } }`. fingerprint.cpp:302-303: `--paren_depth_ == 0) { first_group_closed_ = true; }`. fingerprint.hpp:331-333: 'Safe for every *stored* hash: a storable 1-row statement has nothing but `;`/EOF after its group'. The only INSERT cases in tests/fingerprint_test.cpp are lines 567-568 (1 row vs 3 rows).

**Scenario.** `INSERT INTO t VALUES (1, 2)` and `INSERT INTO t VALUES (1, 2) ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE SET v = v + 1 RETURNING *` produce the same pattern_id and fetch_id; so do `INSERT INTO t (a) SELECT x FROM u` and `INSERT INTO t (a) SELECT y FROM v WHERE y > 3` (the binding's relation oids may still separate the last pair in statistics keyed by binding; not verified). Every consumer keyed by the INSERT's shape (sys.statement_stats / sys.statement_histogram / sys.refusal_stats rows, shape_text taken from whichever statement arrived first) reports an upsert's calls, conflicts and cost under the plain insert's row. No row result is affected: an INSERT has no trail or plan.

**Suggested.** Either keep folding identifier/keyword tokens that follow the VALUES rows at depth 0 (ON, CONFLICT, DO, UPDATE, SET..., RETURNING...) and stop the suppression when the token after the first group is not VALUES / ',' (a SELECT source), with a kFingerprintVersion decision per fingerprint.hpp's bump rule; or state the collision in bulkinsert.md section 2.4 and sys-views.md and correct the fingerprint.hpp comment.

**First verifier (narrowed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. Read fingerprint.cpp:255-315 and 406, fingerprint.hpp:315-345, docs/spec/bulkinsert.md §2.4 (lines 112-131), docs/spec/sys-views.md §6.1 (174-200), workorder-di-shape-identity.md:447 and 676-678 (DI binds an INSERT ... SELECT's source and a conflict arm's subqueries into the binding). tests/fingerprint_test.cpp has only the 1-row vs 3-row INSERT pair (567-568). Grep of sys-views.md, parser-v2.md, known-gaps.md and the CG/DI/DF work orders for a statement that ON CONFLICT/RETURNING share the plain INSERT's shape found none. Not read: the parser's ON CONFLICT/RETURNING productions themselves (taken from CLAUDE.md's I20 row and parser.cpp:2525-2530 for the SELECT source), and no run confirmed the equal hashes. The finder's own example pair for INSERT ... SELECT (sources u and v) is separated by binding, which the finder flagged as unverified; that half falls.

### H6. sys.statement_stats keeps a stale plan_source, plan_name and variant after the plan stops applying to a shape whose compile carries no plan note

- **Kind**: quiet-wrong; severity low (the finder's: medium, confidence high); round 1, `sys-views` (`sys-views#1`)
- **Where**: `src/stats/statement_stats.cpp:136`; origin: CX-S1b (4f12b44d: plan_source and the keep-what-it-had rule), exposed by CX-S3b (single-relation plans)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**; second verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Record overwrites a shape's plan_source and plan_label only when the call hands it a non-empty source. A compile that produced no PlanNote hands an empty one, so the shape keeps its previous value indefinitely. The compiler produces no note for a statement over one relation without a plan, for a join of tuple-key descents alone, and for anything under join_order = written with no plan (the dispatcher then sets "written" only for multi-step chains). Any of those shapes that once ran under an administrator's plan therefore reads plan_source = admin with the dropped plan's name and variant for as long as the instance runs, with calls and last_seen still advancing. sys-views.md section 6.1 says plan_source is the source "at its latest compile" and that NULL is "a block the join planner did not order and no plan chose", including a statement over one relation unless it has an administrator's plan. The same staleness leaves "written" on a descent-only join after SET JOIN_ORDER goes from written back to fixed. Separately, the Snapshot merge at line 179 compares each part against a running max of last_seen that sourceless parts also raise, so which core's source wins depends on part order rather than on "the core that saw the shape last".

**Evidence.** statement_stats.cpp:136 `if (!plan_source.empty() && (shape.plan_source != plan_source || shape.plan_label != plan_label)) {`. step_compiler.cpp (after the admin branch) `if (!plan->search || single) return best;` returns with no plan_note, as does the descent-only return below it. command_dispatcher.cpp:10315-10323 sets statement_plan_source_ only when `compiled.plan_note.has_value()` or `join_order == kWritten && steps.size() > 1`. sys_views.cpp:1424-1426 prints the kept strings. The only test of the after-drop state, tests/admin_plans_test.cpp:1583-1588, uses a join that re-reads `searched`.

**Scenario.** CREATE PLAN p FOR SELECT a FROM t WHERE b = 5 VARIANT v ELSE ORDER (t) ACCESS t INDEX; run SELECT a FROM t WHERE b = 7, and sys.statement_stats reads admin, p, v. DROP PLAN p (or ALTER PLAN p DISABLE). Run the SELECT again: it compiles with no plan and passes an empty source. SELECT plan_source, plan_name, variant FROM sys.statement_stats still answers admin, p, v where the spec says NULL, NULL, NULL. A DBA or agent using the column to find shapes that run without their plan is told the dropped plan is in force.

**Suggested.** Distinguish "this call compiled and no plan chose it" from "this call did not compile". NoteStatementCompiled already marks a compiled call (cost.compiled), so Record can clear plan_source and plan_label when cost.compiled is set and plan_source is empty, and keep them only for a call that compiled nothing. In Snapshot, track the last_seen of the part the kept source came from instead of comparing against the merged max. Add a cell beside StatementStatsNameThePlanAndTheVariantThatRan for a single-relation plan dropped and for written to fixed on a descent-only join.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. I traced the path end to end and found no guard. (1) src/stats/statement_stats.cpp:136-148: Record assigns shape.plan_source/plan_label only when `!plan_source.empty()`; nothing else in the class clears them (the API in include/kds/stats/statement_stats.hpp:145-161 is Record, Snapshot, Dropped, ResetForTest only; no DROP PLAN / ALTER PLAN / SET JOIN_ORDER hook touches the store). The header states the rule as deliberate (statement_stats.hpp:141-143: "`plan_source` is empty for a statement that compiled no join block, and then the shape keeps what it had"), but its premise predates CX-S3b. (2) src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:1268-1269 resets statement_plan_source_/label_ to empty at every statement head; 10311-10323 sets them only when `compiled.plan_note.has_value()` or `join_order == kWritten && compiled.steps.size() > 1`; 1297-1298 and 1510-1511 hand them to Record unchanged. So a compile with no note records an empty source. (3) src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:2610-2617: a one-relation statement enters the planned path only while `plan->admin->any_single()` is true; otherwise it goes straight to CompileBound with no plan_note. With a single plan still enabled for another shape, 2697-2745 emits a note only if `Find(*shape)` hits, and 2749 `if (!plan->search || single) return best;` returns with no note. The descent-only join return at 2759-2761 also carries no note. So after DROP PLAN / ALTER PLAN DISABLE a one-relation shape (and a descent-only join) compiles with no note, Record gets an empty source, and the row keeps `admin`, the plan name and variant while calls/last_seen advance. Same mechanism leaves `written` on a descent-only / SELECT * / subquery-predicate join after SET JOIN_ORDER moves from written to fixed/planned (dispatcher 10321-10322 sets it only under kWritten). (4) Spec contradiction is real: docs/spec/sys-views.md:179-192 says plan_source is the source "at its latest compile" and "NULL is a block the join planner did not order and no plan chose: a statement over one relation under every join_order - unless it has an administrator's plan (CX-S3b)" and a join of tuple key descents alone under planned/fixed. src/server/sys_views.cpp:1424 prints the kept string. (5) Tests: tests/admin_plans_test.cpp:1570-1588 (StatementStatsNameThePlanAndTheVariantThatRan) checks the after-drop state only for a join that re-reads `searched`; the single-relation plan test just above (ending 1567) drops plan p and asserts any_single() false and ANALYZE text, never the view. `grep plan_source tests sim` finds no other cell. (6) Not already known: no mention in docs/inflight/known-gaps.md, docs/pending/*, workorder-cx-managed-plans.md (lines 1035-1039 record only that source and label are replaced as a pair and the NULL wording fix) or the raft-marks. (7) Snapshot sub-claim also holds as read: statement_stats.cpp:179-183 compares each part's last_seen_us against `into.last_seen_us`, which line 183 raises from sourceless parts too, and the first part emplaced seeds the source whatever its age. With parts A(source, t=10), B(no source, t=30), C(source, t=20) the merge keeps A's source; with B first the source is empty until a later part exceeds t=30. Part order is fixed (core index) so the output is deterministic, but it is not "the core that saw the shape last" nor the latest sourced part. Sourceless parts beside sourced ones arise exactly from the keep rule (a core that only ran the shape after the drop). Severity lowered to low: the store is memory-only observability, emptied by a restart; no query result, plan choice or durable state reads it (ANALYZE and sys.plans still tell the truth). It is a diagnostic column answering against its own spec for the life of the instance.

**Second verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture at fba5529f; nothing built or run. I tried to find a guard and found none. Code path: src/stats/statement_stats.cpp:136-148 writes shape.plan_source/plan_label only when the passed source is non-empty; the class API (include/kds/stats/statement_stats.hpp:145-161) has no clear, and the only callers in src are Record at src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:1510 and Snapshot in sys_views.cpp, so no DROP PLAN / ALTER PLAN / SET JOIN_ORDER hook touches a kept source. The dispatcher clears statement_plan_source_ at every statement head (1268-1269) and sets it only at 10315-10323: when the compiled chain has a plan_note, or under kWritten with more than one step. plan_note is referenced nowhere else on the statement path (14676 and 14950 are ANALYZE/ADVISE). In src/exec/step_compiler.cpp a one-relation statement goes straight to CompileBound with no note unless any_single() (2610-2617); with another single plan still enabled and Find missing, 2749 `if (!plan->search || single) return best;` returns no note; the descent-only join return (2759-2761) carries none either. The admin branch emplaces a note for a single statement too (2737-2741), so the row does read admin/p/v first. After DROP PLAN or ALTER PLAN DISABLE the same key (fetch_id, kind, binding unchanged) records an empty source and keeps admin + name/variant; sys_views.cpp:1424-1426 prints them. Contract: docs/spec/sys-views.md:179-192 says plan_source is the source 'at its latest compile' and NULL is 'a statement over one relation under every join_order - unless it has an administrator's plan (CX-S3b)', and a descent-only join under planned/fixed. parser-v2.md I12 repeats 'for the shape's latest compile'. The header's keep rule (statement_stats.hpp:141-143, 'compiled no join block ... keeps what it had') is from 4f12b44d (CX-S1b, blame on lines 134-137), before CX-S3b gave a one-relation shape a source to keep; the spec is the authority and was not amended to admit it. Record: nothing in docs/inflight/known-gaps.md (grep plan_source: no hit; statement_stats entries at 1045, 2093, 2248, 2476, 2829, 2955 are other matters), docs/pending, or workorder-cx-managed-plans.md (1007-1014 treats 'recorded NULL, or what an earlier one left' as a defect that was fixed for the streamed path; 1035-1039 only the pair-replace rule). workorder-di-shape-identity.md:96-99 concerns binding mixing, not this. Tests: tests/admin_plans_test.cpp:1582-1588 pins the wanted behaviour for a join ('With the plan gone the shape reads its next source', expects no 'stats_plan' in the view) - it passes only because a join re-reads 'searched'. The single-relation plan test ending at 1567 drops p and checks any_single() and ANALYZE, never the view. grep of tests and sim finds no other plan_source cell. So the test intent contradicts the single-relation behaviour and nothing pins it as wanted. Reachability: CREATE PLAN ... ORDER (t) ACCESS t INDEX, DROP PLAN, ALTER PLAN DISABLE and SET JOIN_ORDER are all SQL an admin can write; the SELECT is an ordinary one-relation statement. Snapshot sub-claim also holds: statement_stats.cpp:179-183 compares each part against into.last_seen_us, which sourceless parts raise, and the first part emplaced seeds the source; parts A(src,t=10), B(none,t=30), C(src,t=20) keep A's source, B first gives empty. Deterministic by core index, but not 'the core that saw the shape last'. Severity low: memory-only diagnostic column, read by no plan choice or result; ANALYZE and sys.plans stay truthful. It misleads exactly the use the spec names for it (finding shapes that run without a plan) until a restart.

## 6.2 Other logic defects (20)

### H7. Forward FK check refuses TXN_CONFLICT and logs a false lock-protocol warning when the parent's writer committed above the snapshot ceiling

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity medium (the finder's: medium, confidence medium); round 1, `dispatcher-2` (`dispatcher-2#1`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:5778`; origin: CP-S2b (766f6a6d, 2026-11-08, in 3.4); the 2026-11-17 B2 ruling fixed the same cause for the reverse walk and the unique check only
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**; second verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** ResolveForeignKeyParents assumes that, with a lock table, a parent head read busy under a granted S has exactly one cause (an UPDATE holding U). There is a second cause the tree itself documents for the reverse check: a writer that committed and released its row X while another commit's marker still holds the snapshot ceiling below its commit LSN. MintCheckView reads the ceiling, so that committed insert or delete of the parent is invisible to the check view and CheckVisibility answers kBusy. For a head that is delete-marked or has no predecessor the arm keeps kBusy, logs 'a writer that changes a row's existence must hold X', and CheckForeignKeyOnWrite returns TXN_CONFLICT with no wait registered (NoteBlockingWriter is only called when locks_ == nullptr). WaitForRowWriter (6041-6056) waits for the ceiling in exactly this state; the forward path does not.

**Evidence.** 5781-5783: `} else if (!busy.deleted && busy.undo_ptr != txn::kNoUndoPtr) { resolved = exec::FkVerdict::kPass; } else if (logging(LogLevel::kWarn)) {` then the Warn text. 5878-5886 comment: 'Reached only by a writer the lock table does not know ... or, with one, a busy head the witness refused (a verb placed under the wrong mode, logged there)'. 6041-6043 (WaitForRowWriter): 'Committed above the snapshot ceiling: another commit's marker caps every mint below it, for up to one `strict` sync.' visibility.hpp:211: `if (!view.Visible(trx_id)) return CheckVerdict::kBusy;`. foreign-keys.md 2a line 247: 'busy under a granted `S` has one cause, and it reads present'.

**Scenario.** Session C0 commits under `strict` and parks for its sync; its marker holds the snapshot ceiling below its LSN L0. Session A commits `INSERT INTO parent VALUES (7)` at L1 > L0 (window entry in, X on parent row 7 released). Before C0's sync lifts the marker, session B runs `INSERT INTO child VALUES (1, 7)`: the S on (parent, 7) is granted, the check view's snapshot is below L1, the head (A's insert, undo_ptr none) reads busy, the arm keeps it busy and writes the Warn line claiming a writer placed a row under the wrong lock mode, and B is answered `TXN_CONFLICT ... is being written by another transaction that has not decided` although the writer has decided and no wait was taken. The same happens with A = `DELETE FROM parent WHERE id = 7` (head delete-marked): B gets TXN_CONFLICT plus the false warning instead of FK_VIOLATION. No wrong row is written; the defect is a spurious client-visible refusal and a diagnostic that reports a protocol violation that did not occur.

**Suggested.** In the busy arm with a lock table, look the holder up as WaitForRowWriter does (`txn_->visibility().LookupCommit(busy.trx_id)`): if it committed above the ceiling, take the ceiling wait (the statement has written nothing and can re-run) instead of keeping kBusy, and log the wrong-mode warning only when the holder is still in flight. Correct foreign-keys.md 2a's 'one cause' sentence and the comment at 5878-5886. A RowWaitCeilingRigTest-style cell for the forward check would pin it.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing built or run. I traced the path looking for a guard and found none. (1) src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:5754-5757 takes the parent row S; 5763 mints the check view after the grant via CheckView -> TransactionManager::MintCheckView (5369-5380), which sets snapshot_lsn = SnapshotCeiling() (src/txn/manager.cpp:90-94). (2) SnapshotCeiling is the published maximum capped by every core's pending_commit_bound (src/txn/instance_visibility.cpp:138-160). include/kds/txn/instance_visibility.hpp:98-104 and 133-137 state that a strict commit's marker caps commits already acknowledged, and that another session's commit is not covered for up to one sync. (3) FinishCommitBody (src/txn/manager.cpp:293-345) publishes the entry, lifts only its own marker, then releases the borrows last. So A's row X is gone while C0's marker still holds the ceiling below A's LSN. (4) ReadView::Visible (include/kds/txn/read_view.hpp:106-114) is false for A: its commit_lsn is above snapshot_lsn, and the floor cannot pass it because Reclaim is bounded by pending markers. CheckVisibility returns kBusy before it looks at `deleted` (include/kds/txn/visibility.hpp:210-213). CheckParentPresent fills BusyHead from that header (src/exec/fk_check.cpp:116-122) and has no LookupCommit or ceiling test. (5) Back at 5778-5792 with locks_ != nullptr: an insert head (undo_ptr none) or a delete-marked head matches neither the pass arm nor the no-table arm, so it stays kBusy, the Warn text is written, and NoteBlockingWriter is not called. CheckForeignKeyOnWrite 5877-5891 then returns TxnConflict 'is being written by another transaction that has not decided'. In DispatchOutcome assembly (1316-1336) blocking_writer_ is 0, so there is no write_block and no re-run. (6) Every ceiling wait in the dispatcher is either the session's own acknowledged commit (978-980, 1427, 1522) or WaitForRowWriter's grant arm (6051-6055). WaitForRowWriter's callers are the reverse walk (5992) and the unique check (13808) only. Documents: I found none that records this as a gap or a decision. foreign-keys.md 2a lines 247-255 states 'one cause'. The CP work order's proof (instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-cp-lock-model-exploration.md C.1, about lines 1611-1632) rests on 'a released one is visible to a view minted after the release', which is the premise the 2026-11-17 B2 ruling showed false. Commit 941d05e8's message and foreign-keys.md 3a cover the reverse walk and the unique check only. known-gaps.md 779-787 lists the ceiling wait for those probes only. tests/row_wait_ceiling_rig_test.cpp has five cells (parent DELETE x3, unique, the net refusal) and none for a child insert. No docs/pending or F/G audit entry mentions it. Bounds on the finding: it needs a commit marker open on another thread, so more than one core; the window is one strict sync, or the short append-to-publish span of a group commit. The refusal is retryable and no wrong row is written. A likely trigger the finder did not name: B parked on A's X through BorrowOrWait, woken at A's release, and re-running at once under C0's marker; that LockWait carries no ceiling_lsn. The CP work order says this arm keeps the S and poisons the session inside BEGIN; I did not trace that part.

**Second verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing built or run. I looked for a guard on four sides and found none. Code path. src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:5754-5757 takes the parent row S, :5763 mints the check view after the grant, and CheckView (:5369-5380) calls TransactionManager::MintCheckView, whose snapshot_lsn is SnapshotCeiling() (src/txn/manager.cpp:90-94). SnapshotCeiling is the published maximum capped by every core's pending_commit_bound (src/txn/instance_visibility.cpp:138-160). FinishCommitBody (src/txn/manager.cpp:293-345) publishes the entry, lifts only its own marker, then releases the borrows, so a writer's row X is gone while another commit's marker still caps the ceiling below its LSN. ReadView::Visible (include/kds/txn/read_view.hpp:106-114) is then false, CheckVisibility answers kBusy before it looks at `deleted` (include/kds/txn/visibility.hpp:210-213), and CheckParentPresent (src/exec/fk_check.cpp:116-122) only copies the header into BusyHead. At :5778-5792 with a lock table, a delete-marked head or one with no predecessor matches neither arm: it stays kBusy, the Warn line is written, and NoteBlockingWriter is not called. CheckForeignKeyOnWrite (:5877-5891) returns TxnConflict. Guards I tried. (1) The statement-head ceiling wait (:977-982, UncoveredCommit :1424-1430) covers only the session's own acknowledged commit, so it defeats the scenario only when the same session wrote the parent. (2) The structural re-run (:1032, StructuralRerunDue :1149-1154) needs storage::StructuralRefusalNoted(), which this refusal does not set, so the TXN_CONFLICT reaches the client. (3) The write-block re-run (:1316) needs blocking_writer_ != 0, which is never set on this arm with a lock table. (4) WaitForRowWriter's ceiling arm (:6051-6055) is reached only from the reverse walk and the unique check. Reachability. tests/row_wait_ceiling_rig_test.cpp (header lines 1-18) states and drives the premise for the reverse walk: a `relaxed` commit made inside a `strict` marker is published and released yet reads busy to every fresh check view. A child INSERT from another session in that window is plain SQL. None of the file's five cells is a child insert, and no test under tests/ or sim/ contains the forward refusal text. Record. docs/spec/foreign-keys.md:247-255 states the opposite ('busy under a granted S has one cause'). docs/spec/txn.md:560-585 records only the generic remainder (another session's acknowledged commit invisible for up to one sync), not this refusal or the warning. known-gaps.md:779-787 names the ceiling wait for the reverse walk and the unique check only. The deleted bug entry (git show 941d05e8^:docs/inflight/bugs/a-granted-row-probe-re-runs-without-yielding-under-a-capped-ceiling.md) and commit 941d05e8's message cover those two sites and never mention the forward check. The same commit removed the unique check's retryable refusal of exactly this writer, and the operator's B2 ruling rejected the retryable-refusal option there, so the forward check is the one site left with it. No F or G audit entry mentions it. Bounds. It needs more than one core and another commit's marker open: one `strict` sync, or the lag before the marker's core runs its publish. An UPDATE committed above the ceiling passes by coincidence; an INSERT gets a spurious TXN_CONFLICT where the answer is pass, a DELETE gets TXN_CONFLICT where the answer is FK_VIOLATION. No wrong row is written and the refusal is retryable. I keep medium because the Warn line reports a lock-protocol violation that did not happen, the spec sentence is false, and under a steady stream of `strict` commits a child insert on a different pooled connection just after the parent's commit can meet it routinely. I did not trace whether the refusal poisons an explicit transaction.

### H8. A set operation refuses two sides of the same type when one is a stored column and the other is computed

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity medium (the finder's: medium, confidence medium); round 1, `exec-expr-query` (`exec-expr-query#1`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/query_layer.cpp:593`; origin: pre-3.4 (BU-S7, 86765796 / 14f4579b)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The set-operation type check compares `SetType.len` for every non-text type. For anything but char and decimal, `len` is a stored width that nothing else consults. A relation column reaches the layer with its raw `sys.columns` width (8 for int64, 4 for int32 and date, 8 for timestamp, 1 for bool), while every computed value of the same type carries `len` 0. A set operation between the two is therefore refused as a type mismatch that names the same type on both sides. The same raw comparison at lines 837-838 silently drops a join's hash equality when the two columns' `len` differ, for example varchar(32) against varchar(16), or an int64 column against a derived table's COUNT(*).

**Evidence.** query_layer.cpp:593: `const bool same = a.type_val == b.type_val && (IsText(a.type_val) || a.len == b.len);` command_dispatcher.cpp:11185 (`DescribeRelation`): `c.type.len = columns[i].len;`, where rows.hpp:208-211 says of `len` for other types: "the type's width, stored and **never consulted**". set_expr.cpp:53 zeroes it for computed values: `t.len = (type_val == kTypeValChar || IsDecimal(type_val)) ? len : 0;`. The layer's own `Concrete` (query_layer.cpp:56-62) is called with 0 for COUNT and SUM. set_expr's `SameType` (line 65) ignores `len` for non-decimals, so only these two layer sites disagree.

**Scenario.** `CREATE TABLE t (id int64, v int64)`, then `SELECT id FROM t UNION ALL SELECT COUNT(*) FROM t`, or `SELECT id FROM t UNION SELECT 5`, or `SELECT d FROM ev UNION SELECT CURRENT_DATE`. The left column has len 8 (4 for date) and the right has len 0, so the statement is refused with "column 1 of a set operation is int64 on one side and int64 on the other; there is no implicit conversion (BJ-R3), CAST one". parser-v2.md I19 says the sides pair "of one type each", and these are one type. A test cell also appears to pass on this refusal: tests/dml_forms_test.cpp:160-162 runs `INSERT INTO w SELECT id + 400, ... FROM cust UNION ALL SELECT 401, 'dup', 'EU', 1` with `ExpectRefused(..., "")`, and its comment says it checks that a duplicate key unwinds earlier rows. By this reading the statement is refused at compile on the len mismatch (`id + 400` is len 8, `401` is len 0), so the atomicity it names is never exercised. Join case: `a JOIN b ON a.name = b.code` with varchar(32) and varchar(16) gets no hash and runs the nested loop over every pair, with correct rows.

**Suggested.** Normalize `len` once where a `LayerColumn` type is built: have `DescribeRelation` go through set_expr's `Concrete` rule, or export `SameType` from set_expr and use it at lines 593 and 837-838. Add set-operation cells with a literal, COUNT(*) and CURRENT_DATE side, and give the dml_forms_test cell a real needle such as the duplicate-key text.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f, no guard found on the path. (1) src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11183-11185 `DescribeRelation` copies `columns[i].len` raw into the LayerColumn type. For an int64 column that is 8: CREATE TABLE takes `row.len = type_row.value().len` (command_dispatcher.cpp:6128 onward), and sys.types holds int64=8, int32=4, date=4, timestamp=8, bool=1 (src/catalog/catalog.cpp:776-793). (2) The layer's resolver returns that type unchanged (src/exec/query_layer.cpp:447 `ResolvedColumn{hits[0], (*s.columns)[hits[0]].type}`), and set_expr takes it as-is (src/exec/set_expr.cpp:515-521, `n->type = found.value()->type`), so it never passes through set_expr's normalizing `Concrete` (set_expr.cpp:46-55). Nothing after `DescribeRelation` normalizes it (query_layer.cpp:677-697). (3) Computed values carry len 0: `DefaultOf` gives `Concrete(kTypeValInt64, 0)` (set_expr.cpp:614-619), COUNT gives `Concrete(int64, 0)` (query_layer.cpp:965), CURRENT_DATE gives `Concrete(kTypeValDate, 0)` (set_expr.cpp:542).
