Integer arithmetic returns operand `a`'s type (set_expr.cpp:1148), so `id + 400` keeps len 8. (4) query_layer.cpp:593 requires `a.len == b.len` for every non-text type, so a column side against a literal, COUNT(*) or CURRENT_DATE side is refused at 595-600 with the same type name on both sides. set_expr's own `SameType` (set_expr.cpp:65-68) compares len for decimals only, and its comment at 50-52 names this exact hazard. (5) The join site at query_layer.cpp:837-838 skips the hash on a len mismatch the same way, falling to the all-pairs loop in `JoinRows` with correct rows. Test coverage: every set-operation cell in tests/query_layer_test.cpp:483-547 and scripts/bs/difftest.py:197 pairs column with column; scripts/bs/fuzz_seeds.py:54 (`EXCEPT SELECT 2`) is a fuzz seed with no expected result. tests/dml_forms_test.cpp:160-162 uses an empty needle (ExpectRefused at :78-82 only needs an ERR prefix), and column 4 pairs `tier` (raw len) with `1` (len 0), so by this reading it is refused at the set-operation check, not on the duplicate key its comment names. known-gaps.md's BU section (line 1738 onward) lists `SELECT NULL UNION SELECT 1` and a bare `5` into int32, not this. Not reproduced; a single run of `SELECT id FROM t UNION SELECT 5` would settle it.

### H9. The query layer's join and correlated-subquery loops run outside the row-touch budget, with no yield or cancel point

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity medium (the finder's: medium, confidence medium); round 1, `exec-expr-query` (`exec-expr-query#2`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/query_layer.cpp:1330`; origin: pre-3.4 (BU-S4 to BU-S7)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** parser-v2.md section 7 makes `max_rows_touched` (default 100,000,000) the plan guard, because "nothing suspends mid-statement on a cooperative core, so an unbounded correlated scan is a denial of service". The layer evades it. Each relation walk is budgeted on its own inside `WalkForLayer`, but the layer's own work over the rows it holds is charged to nothing. `JoinRows` evaluates ON for every left-right pair when no column-to-column equality is hashable, and a correlated subquery re-executes its block per outer row. `LayerLimits` carries only max_rows, max_groups and max_distinct, and query_layer.cpp has no budget, yield or cancel check. The only bound is `sort_max_rows` per held row set (default 1,048,576), which allows about 1.1e12 pair evaluations inline on the session's reactor.

**As narrowed by verification.** The query layer's own work is charged to no row-touch budget: `JoinRows` evaluates ON for every left-right pair when no same-type column equality is hashable, capped only on output rows, so up to sort_max_rows squared (about 1.1e12 at defaults) evaluations run inline with no ResourceExhausted. A correlated subquery re-filters its whole cached relation per outer row. This is undocumented against parser-v2.md §7's plan guard. The per-outer-row by-value copies and the rows-held bound are accepted costs already in known-gaps.md (BU section) and BU-Q10, and the absence of a yield or cancel point is the documented general contract, not a separate defect.

**Evidence.** query_layer.cpp:1330-1350: `for (Row& l : left) { ... for (std::size_t r : *candidates) { Row combined = Concat(l, right[r]); ... Holds(*j.on, ...)`, where candidates is every right row when `j.eq` is empty. query_layer.hpp:65-71: `LayerLimits` has no row-touch field. A correlated subquery goes `AnswerSubquery` -> `Execute` -> `ExecuteBlock` -> `SourceRows`, which returns the cached relation by value, and `rows = first.value()` copies it again (lines 1280, 1525) before the residual filter runs per outer row.

**Scenario.** Tables a and b each hold about 1,000,000 rows, under the default `sort_max_rows`. `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM a LEFT JOIN b ON a.v + 1 = b.v` is a layer statement because of the outer join, and the expression equality is not hashable, so it evaluates about 1e12 ON conditions, each allocating a concatenated row. The tested shape `SELECT c.id, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ord o WHERE o.cust_id = c.id) FROM cust c` makes two deep copies of all of `ord` and a full residual scan per `cust` row. Neither is refused `ResourceExhausted` by the row-touch budget, as a native correlated scan of that size would be. The loop runs on the reactor with no slice boundary, so every other session on that core is stalled, C_CANCEL is not taken until the statement ends, and the transaction lifetime ceiling does not cut a running statement.

**Suggested.** Give the layer the statement's `exec::Budget`: charge one unit per candidate pair in `JoinRows` and per row filtered in `ExecuteBlock`, and refuse `ResourceExhausted` through `Budget::Exhausted()`. Alternatively state in parser-v2.md I19 and known-gaps.md that the layer is bounded only by sort_max_rows squared. Hashing a correlated equality, and returning held rows by reference from `SourceRows`, would remove the common quadratic case.

**First verifier (narrowed).** [source-read] The facts hold. src/exec/query_layer.cpp has no budget, yield or cancel check: grep finds only `Cap` (line 523, sort_max_rows on rows held) and two ResourceExhausted sites at 1403 and 1427 for groups and distinct. `LayerLimits` (include/kds/exec/query_layer.hpp:65-71) has max_rows, max_groups and max_distinct only. `JoinRows` (query_layer.cpp:1297-1360) builds `all` = every right row when `j.eq` is empty and runs `Concat` + `Holds` per pair; `Cap` is applied to `out.size()` only (1347, 1351), so a selective non-hashable ON does left x right evaluations uncapped. `j.eq` is filled only for column = column of identical type_val and len (826-841), so `a.v + 1 = b.v` is not hashed. `SourceRows` returns the cached walk by value (1281) and `ExecuteBlock` copies it again (`rows = first.value()`, 1525) before the per-row filter, on every outer row of a correlated subquery. Parts are already documented. docs/inflight/known-gaps.md, 'The query layer and the DML forms (BU)', 'Costs measured at BU's close, not cut', states that the layer holds every walked relation's rows, copies each row per stage, returns a cached subquery result by value per outer row and probes IN (subquery) linearly. BU-Q10 (instructions/v3.0.0/workorder-bu-basic-query-surface.md:200) and parser-v2.md I19 'Bounded' bound rows held, by decision. That nothing but a SELECT's outermost walk yields, that C_CANCEL is taken at a slice boundary or the next frame, and that the lifetime ceiling never cuts a running statement are stated contracts (sched.md §3, protocol.md §10, txn.md §1). What no document states is that the layer's pair evaluation and per-outer-row re-filter are outside the row-touch budget that parser-v2.md §7 names as the plan guard for exactly this case.

### H10. The 4 KiB pre-auth inbox cap never fires on a KWP connection: an unauthenticated peer can pin about 16 MiB per connection

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity medium (the finder's: medium, confidence high); round 1, `net-wire` (`net-wire#1`)
- **Where**: `src/server/tcp_server.cpp:526`; origin: pre-3.4 (KW, eecda943, 2026-08-31; the handshake_done comment added at 0733ec9f, 2026-10-13)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** OnClientReadable enforces the pre-auth budget as `conn.inbox.size() + n > kPreAuthInboxCap` (4096). On the KWP path DrainFrames hands the whole inbox to `conn.decoder.Feed` and then calls `conn.inbox.clear()` on every read (tcp_server.cpp:621-624), so `conn.inbox.size()` is 0 at every check. `n` is at most 4096 (the read chunk), so the sum is never above 4096 and the cap cannot fire. The bytes accumulate in FrameDecoder::buffer_, which the check does not consult and which is bounded only by kMaxFrame (16 MiB). The cap still works on the newline debug port, where the inbox does hold the unconsumed bytes. docs/spec/protocol.md section 14 (line 58) states the contract: no pre-auth deadline exists and 'the pre-auth inbox is capped at 4 KiB'.

**Evidence.** tcp_server.cpp:526 `if (pre_auth && conn.inbox.size() + static_cast<std::size_t>(n) > kPreAuthInboxCap) {` tcp_server.cpp:623-624 `Status fed = conn.decoder.Feed(...(p, conn.inbox.size()));  conn.inbox.clear();` frame_codec.cpp:77 `if (header.value().length > kMaxFrame) {` is the only bound on the decoder's buffer.

**Scenario.** Server with auth = scram on the default port. An anonymous peer connects and sends an 8-byte frame header with type C_HELLO and length = 16 MiB, then drips payload bytes without ever completing the frame. Each 4 KiB read passes the check (0 + 4096 is not above 4096) and is appended to the decoder's buffer, so the connection holds up to about 16 MiB before any handshake. Nothing ends it: there is no pre-auth deadline, no connection cap exists in the tree (grep for max_connections finds none), and keepalive does not reap a live peer. The same works after C_HELLO with an oversized C_AUTH frame while the session is in kAwaitAuth. N anonymous connections pin N x 16 MiB, against the documented 4 KiB each. No test covers the cap on the KWP path (grep of tests/).

**Suggested.** Enforce the budget where the bytes live: give FrameDecoder a `buffered()` accessor and close when `pre_auth && decoder.buffered() + n > cap`, or give the decoder a settable frame ceiling of 4 KiB that is raised to kMaxFrame once handshake_done(). Add a KWP cell beside the text-protocol one.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. src/server/tcp_server.cpp:502-503 reads at most 4096 bytes; :526 tests `conn.inbox.size() + n > kPreAuthInboxCap` (4096, :520) before the bytes are appended (:561, or :549 through the TLS channel). On the KWP path every OnClientReadable ends in DrainFrames (:563-564), which hands the whole inbox to `conn.decoder.Feed` and clears it (:621-624) before anything else, so on a KWP connection the inbox is empty at every later entry to :526 (the only early return, `conn.closing` at :616, is not a pre-auth state that keeps reading usefully). 0 + n with n <= 4096 is never > 4096, so the arm cannot fire for `conn.kwp.has_value() && !handshake_done()` (include/kds/server/kwp_session.hpp:244, phase_ == kReady). The bytes accumulate in FrameDecoder::buffer_ (src/wire/frame_codec.cpp:61); the only bound is the first header's length <= kMaxFrame (frame_codec.cpp:77, include/kds/wire/kwp.hpp:59 = 16 MiB), and FrameDecoder exposes no buffered-size accessor (kwp.hpp:295-320) the server could consult. KwpSession::OnFrame sees only complete frames (tcp_server.cpp:650-653), so the kAwaitHello / kAwaitAuth arms (src/server/kwp_session.cpp:418-435) cannot refuse an incomplete oversized C_HELLO or C_AUTH. No other guard found: grep of src/include for a connection cap (max_conn, max_clients, connection limit) finds nothing; tcp_server.cpp:519 itself says the pre-auth deadline is future hardening; keepalive (:50-69) does not reap a live peer. No test exercises the cap on either protocol (grep of tests for the cap constant, 'pre-auth budget', 'overflowed' finds nothing relevant). docs/spec/protocol.md:58 states 'the pre-auth inbox is capped at 4 KiB' as the compensation for the missing deadline; neither docs/inflight/known-gaps.md nor the two docs/pending audit files mention the cap or the decoder buffer. The cap does hold on the newline port, where DrainCommands erases only consumed lines (:861-871). So an unauthenticated KWP peer can pin about 16 MiB (plus one read chunk) per connection instead of 4 KiB, as claimed. Origin is pre-3.4 code; not a 3.4 regression.

### H11. BEGIN's clause refusals carry no byte position although the offending token is in hand; HandleBegin is not even given the line

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `arch-config-errors` (`arch-config-errors#2`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:12843`; origin: CS (adbea55bd, 2026-11-15) for the READ ONLY/WRITE arm; the ISOLATION/DURABILITY arms beside it are pre-3.4
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** CLAUDE.md's working rule is 'Every refusal carries the byte position of the offending token'. CS added `BEGIN ... READ <mode>` parsing whose refusal names the token's text but not its byte, and the same handler's other refusals do the same: 'expected ISOLATION LEVEL, DURABILITY, READ ONLY or READ WRITE after BEGIN, got <word>' (12849), the bare 'ERR expected LEVEL after ISOLATION' (12804), and the ParseIsolationLevel / ParseDurabilityClass statuses returned unlocated (12821, 12831). The handler is called as `HandleBegin(rest, session)` (line 1751) with no `line`, so no byte can be computed there, while every other statement-level handler the 3.4 work added (SET JOIN_ORDER 2792, DROP PLAN 2837, SET ASH 2886, HandleStatistics 3757) takes `line` and locates its refusals. Not in known-gaps or either audit file (grep 'expected ONLY', 'after BEGIN').

**Evidence.** 12843-12844: `return Refused(Status::InvalidArgument("expected ONLY or WRITE after READ, got '" + std::string(mode) + "'"));` / 1751: `return HandleBegin(rest, session);`

**Scenario.** `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL repeatable read READ ONYL` answers "expected ONLY or WRITE after READ, got 'ONYL'" with no byte; over KWP/1 the S_ERROR `position` field is absent for a refusal whose token offset was known.

**Suggested.** Pass `line` to HandleBegin and locate its refusals with the same token-byte helper the neighbouring handlers use (see the duplication finding - one helper, not a sixth lambda).

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:1751 calls `HandleBegin(rest, session)`; the declaration at include/kds/server/command_dispatcher.hpp:835 takes `(std::string_view args, Session&)` only, so the handler has no `line` to measure a token offset against. Inside the handler (12771-12852) I traced every clause refusal: 12804 returns the bare literal `{"ERR expected LEVEL after ISOLATION", false}`; 12821 and 12831 return `Refused(parsed.status())` for ParseIsolationLevel / ParseDurabilityClass with no context added; 12843-12844 (CS's READ arm, pickaxe names adbea55b) and 12849-12851 name the token text only. No caller-side wrapper adds a position: line 1751 returns the outcome directly. The position reaches a client only as message text (`(byte N)` / `at byte N`, e.g. command_dispatcher.cpp:1742, 2792-2797 SET JOIN_ORDER's `refuse` lambda computing `at.data() - line.data()`), so nothing downstream can supply it. The neighbours cited do take `line` (HandleUse at 1763/13099; SET JOIN_ORDER lambda at 2792). tests/read_only_txn_test.cpp:116-119 pin only the text prefix, no byte. Not stated as a gap: known-gaps.md lists other byteless refusals (lines 718, 1600, 2046, 3228) but none for BEGIN; the F27 note (docs/pending/whole-engine-audit-unverified-findings.md:272) says only that HandleBegin's unknown-clause refusal now carries its InvalidArgument via Refused, nothing about position; workorder-cs-read-only-transaction.md requires a byte for the write refusals (line 193), not for the clause parse. Addition the finder did not state: HandleSetIsolation and HandleSetDurability (1766-1767, 13130, 13168) are also called without `line`, so the same shape extends beyond BEGIN (pre-3.4). Impact is diagnostic only - the refusal is still raised and nothing is accepted wrongly - so low.

### H12. A quoted config value containing '#' keeps its quotes and swallows a trailing comment - the one case the sample's header says quoting exists for

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `arch-config-errors` (`arch-config-errors#5`)
- **Where**: `src/server/config_file.cpp:69`; origin: pre-3.4 (b5b0184c, 2026-07-29; no 3.4 commit touches the file)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Parse finds the first '#' on the line and, when the text before it looks like an unterminated quote, declines to strip ANY comment from the line (line 72 strips only `if (!in_quotes)`). It never looks for the closing quote, so a later, real comment stays in the value; Unquote (line 25) then sees a value that starts with a quote and ends with comment text and strips nothing. The value becomes the literal text including both quote characters and the comment, with no parse refusal. kds.conf.sample:5-6 promises 'Quote a value to keep surrounding whitespace or a literal #', and the sample itself puts trailing comments after values (lines 459-460), so the combination is the natural way to write it.

**Evidence.** 69-70: `in_quotes = !val.empty() && (val.front() == '"' || val.front() == '\'') && val.back() != val.front();` / 72: `if (!in_quotes) raw = raw.substr(0, hash);` / 26-27: Unquote strips only when `s.front()` and `s.back()` are the same quote

**Scenario.** `log_file = "kdb#1.log"   # per instance` sets log_file to the 33-character string `"kdb#1.log"   # per instance` (quotes and comment included); the server starts and logs to a file of that name. The same line for `wal_dir` or `data_file` creates a directory/volume under the wrong name rather than refusing; for `users_file` / `tls_cert_file` it fails later at open with a path the operator did not write.

**Suggested.** Scan the value for its closing quote: when the value opens with a quote, take the text up to the matching quote as the value and require that only whitespace or a '#' comment follows, refusing `origin:line` otherwise (the file's own 'guessing is how a config file lies' rule at line 95).

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f, src/server/config_file.cpp:45-105. Traced `log_file = "kdb#1.log"   # per instance`: line 60 finds the first '#' (inside the quotes); `before` is `log_file = "kdb`, `val` is `"kdb`, whose front is a quote and back is 'b', so `in_quotes` is true (69-70) and line 72 strips nothing - the second, real '#' is never looked for. Line 86 then runs Unquote on `"kdb#1.log"   # per instance`: front is '"', back is 'e', so 25-27 strip nothing and the stored value is the whole text with both quotes and the comment. No refusal follows; Parse has no check for text after a closing quote, and the only refusals are a missing '=', an empty key and a duplicate key (79-98). kds.conf.sample:5-6 does promise "Quote a value to keep surrounding whitespace or a literal '#'", and trailing comments after values are the sample's own style (lines 459-460). The only test, tests/config_file_test.cpp:59-62 `QuotesPreserveWhitespaceAndHashes`, despite its name tests no '#' at all, so nothing pins or defeats this. No 3.4 commit touches the file (git log v3.4.0..HEAD empty); not in known-gaps.md or either audit file (grep for config_file / kds.conf finds only an unrelated bench note). Two corrections to the finder's detail, neither changing the verdict: (1) the resulting string is 28 characters, not 33. (2) A second, worse case the finder missed sits in the same heuristic: a quoted value whose first character is '#', e.g. `key = "#foo"`, gives `val` = the single character `"`, so `val.back() == val.front()`, `in_quotes` is false, the line is cut at the '#', and the value becomes the one-character string `"` (Unquote needs size >= 2) - silently, with no refusal. A quoted value with '#' and no trailing comment (`"kdb#1.log"`) does work. Low: startup configuration only, operator-visible in the resulting file or path name, but it is silent and contradicts the sample's stated contract.

### H13. PlanStats' master eviction can evict the keys it is folding in this same step, losing their smoothed value and band and overshooting the bound

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `arch-state-stores` (`arch-state-stores#3`)
- **Where**: `src/stats/plan_stats.cpp:422`; origin: pre-3.4 (CC-S2, f3ced1ad)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The eviction picks victims by `seen_ns` over every master key before the step's observed keys have their `seen_ns` refreshed. A key that is in `merged` and already in `master_`, but was last seen long ago, ranks among the oldest and is erased; the loop just below then re-creates it with `master_[key]` as a first observation. Its posterior value and band hysteresis are reset, `dirty_` is set so a new generation is published, and `master_` ends the step above kMaxMasterKeys by the number of such keys.

**Evidence.** plan_stats.cpp:425: `for (const auto& [key, master] : master_) by_age.emplace_back(master.seen_ns, key);` and :429 `for (std::size_t i = 0; i < drop; ++i) master_.erase(by_age[i].second);`, followed at :433-435 by `Master& master = master_[key]; master.seen_ns = now_ns;` for every merged key. Nothing excludes merged keys from `by_age`. plan-stats.md:104 states the rule as 'past `kMaxMasterKeys` keys the least recently seen go'.

**Scenario.** master_ holds 16,384 keys. A join shape last run an hour ago runs again in the same steward step in which F new shapes arrive. drop = F, and the returning shape is among the F oldest by seen_ns, so it is erased and re-inserted: its posterior restarts from this step's mean alone (no alpha = 1/4 smoothing, band re-derived without hysteresis), a generation is published, and under `planned` the block may switch order on one observation. Only reachable past 16,384 distinct searched join keys.

**Suggested.** Refresh `seen_ns` for the merged keys that already exist before building `by_age`, or skip keys present in `merged` when choosing victims.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. src/stats/plan_stats.cpp:420-431: `fresh` counts merged keys absent from master_; when master_.size() + fresh > kMaxMasterKeys, `by_age` is built from EVERY master_ key (line 425) with its stored seen_ns, `drop = master_.size() + fresh - kMaxMasterKeys`, nth_element picks the `drop` oldest and line 429 erases them. No test of membership in `merged` exists anywhere in that block. The only write of seen_ns in the whole tree is line 435 (`master.seen_ns = now_ns`), which runs in the fold loop AFTER the eviction (grep over src, include, tests, sim: seen_ns appears only at plan_stats.hpp:224 and plan_stats.cpp:425, 435), so a returning key carries its old stamp into the victim selection and nothing else (a compile reading the posterior, for instance) refreshes it. If such a key is among the `drop` oldest it is erased, then line 434 `master_[key]` default-constructs it: `present` is 0 so `banded` is false, line 444 sets value = mean (no alpha = 1/4 step), and Reband (lines 371-380) with banded == false returns moved = true, so dirty_ is set and a generation is published; its `declines`/`declined_ns` are also lost. master_ ends the step at kMaxMasterKeys + (number of such keys), since `drop` was sized assuming only `fresh` insertions; that overshoot is self-correcting on the next step that has any merged entry or not, because the condition at :422 holds with fresh == 0 once size > kMaxMasterKeys. Merge has one caller (plan_stats.cpp:551, the steward step), so no caller-side guard. docs/spec/plan-stats.md:104 (PS4 item 2) states 'the least recently seen go', which a key observed in this very step is not. Not stated in docs/inflight/known-gaps.md or docs/pending (grep for kMaxMasterKeys / 'least recently' finds only the spec line); no test references kMaxMasterKeys. Origin f3ced1ad (CC-S2), pre-3.4 as the finder says. Severity low: reachable only past 16,384 distinct join keys (include/kds/stats/plan_stats.hpp:114), the effect is one posterior restarting from a single step's mean, statistics are advisory to plan choice (no result changes), and under the server default join_order = fixed a captured order is not re-searched; only `planned` (or an uncaptured shape) can switch order on it.

### H14. A capturing sink keeps every row the top-N sort admitted, so a SET subquery with ORDER BY ... LIMIT holds memory by rows walked, not by rows kept

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: medium, confidence medium); round 1, `dispatcher-3` (`dispatcher-3#1`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:10943`; origin: pre-3.4 device (the index-as-text capture in the two sinks), folded into ParkedRows by the audit fix 3f8c499c (F15, F26-F32; first tagged v3.4.10)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**; second verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** ParkedRows::Park appends each encoded row to a vector and returns its index as the row's text; nothing ever removes a row the sorter later evicts. Under a LIMIT the sorter is a bounded heap that admits every row beating its worst retained row (src/exec/sort.cpp:87-95) and SelectRun::Execute encodes each admitted row (command_dispatcher.cpp:7205-7208), so ScalarSubquerySink parks one AstValue per admission for the life of the statement. sort_max_rows does not bound it: the cap is checked only when the heap is not yet full (sort.cpp:98).

**Evidence.** command_dispatcher.cpp:10943-10946: `void Park(Row row, std::string& text) { rows_.push_back(std::move(row)); text = std::to_string(rows_.size() - 1); }`. sort.cpp:104-116: a full heap evicts by swapping the evicted row's text back into the caller's scratch - the parked value that text indexes stays in `rows_`. command_dispatcher.cpp:10995: `rendered_.Park(projection.empty() ? parser::AstValue{} : frame.Get(projection[0]), out);`

**Scenario.** `UPDATE acct SET last_amt = (SELECT amount FROM ledger ORDER BY ts DESC LIMIT 1) WHERE id = 1`, ledger holding N rows inserted in ascending ts with no index on ts (or `indexes = off`), so CV's ordered walk does not apply. The walk meets rows in tuple key order; each has a larger ts than the heap's one retained row, so each is admitted, encoded and parked. The heap stays at one row; ParkedRows grows to N AstValues (two std::string members each) on the dispatcher thread with no refusal - gigabytes for a ledger of tens of millions of rows, where the same SELECT run at top level holds one row. A varchar projection makes each parked value carry its string.

**Suggested.** Give the capturing sinks a way to drop an evicted row: after `scratch.sorter.Take(row_scratch_)` the scratch holds the evicted row's text (its index), so a `ResultSink::Discard(std::string_view)` hook called there lets ParkedRows clear the slot and reuse it from a free list. Alternatively cap ParkedRows at sort_max_rows and refuse ResourceExhausted as the layer's own row sets do.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. I traced the path and found no guard. (1) Entry: CommandDispatcher::RunScalarSubquery (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11067-11075) installs a ScalarSubquerySink through ScopedResultSink and runs HandleSelect(...) to its end via RunToEnd. tests/set_expr_subquery_test.cpp:206 pins that `UPDATE t SET v = (SELECT x FROM u ORDER BY x DESC LIMIT 1) WHERE id = 1` is admitted, so the shape is reachable. (2) HandleSelect sets run->sink = &SinkFor(session, text_sink) (command_dispatcher.cpp:10299-10300), i.e. the capturing sink, and arms the sorter with run->scratch().sorter.Reset(compiled, sort_max_rows_) (10467). No branch there treats a capturing sink differently. (3) SelectRun::Execute's visit (7197-7210): when the sorter is active, Admit(frame) -> render(frame, row_scratch_) -> sink->EncodeProjectedRow -> ScalarSubquerySink::EncodeProjectedRow (10992-10996) -> ParkedRows::Park (10943-10946), which is an unconditional rows_.push_back and writes the index as the text; then sorter.Take(row_scratch_). (4) src/exec/sort.cpp:87-96: in the bounded case with a full heap, Admit returns PendingBefore(buffer_.front()) and never reaches the max_rows_ check at :98, so sort_max_rows does not cap admissions. For ORDER BY ts DESC LIMIT 1 over rows met in ascending ts, every row beats the held one (cmp > 0 with descending), so every row is admitted. sort.cpp:104-116: the eviction only swaps the evicted row's text (an index string) back into the caller's scratch; nothing tells the sink. (5) ParkedRows has only Park and Take (10941-10953); Take moves out one element and never erases. Take is called only from Emit, i.e. for the survivors drained in Finish (7304-7311). So rows_ holds one AstValue per admission until the sink is destroyed at the end of RunScalarSubquery. AstValue (include/kds/parser/ast.hpp:68-) carries two std::string members plus scalars, so roughly 100 bytes each before any varchar payload; evicted entries keep their str_val. (6) No document states it: docs/spec/types.md §3.2b (lines 359-385) describes the SET subquery with no memory bound; docs/inflight/known-gaps.md:1305 covers only the run-once semantics; F29 in docs/pending/whole-engine-audit-unverified-findings.md:278-280 describes ParkedRows as a duplication fold and says nothing of growth. Not already-known. Bounds on the claim, none of which defeat it: growth equals the number of heap admissions, so it is O(N) only when the walk order is correlated with the sort order (each row a new best) - which is exactly the append-ordered ledger with `ORDER BY ts DESC LIMIT 1` and no index on ts (an index, or ORDER BY <tuple key> DESC, takes CV's ordered walk and no sorter); in random order admissions are about k*ln(N/k). Without a LIMIT, or with a LIMIT above sort_max_rows, Admit's cap at sort.cpp:98 does bound the parked rows. The effect is memory held for one statement, not a wrong result. LayerWalkSink (11105-11139) shares the same ParkedRows device and its max_rows_ check is in Emit, not in Park, so it would behave the same if a layer walk carried a sorted LIMIT; I did not trace whether one can.

**Second verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing built or run. The mechanism is as claimed, with one bound neither the finder nor the first verifier named. Code path. RunScalarSubquery installs a ScalarSubquerySink and runs HandleSelect to its end (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11066-11075). HandleSelect takes the session's sink (:10298-10300) and arms the sorter with sort_max_rows_ (:10467), with no branch for a capturing sink. SelectRun::Execute's visit (:7197-7210) calls Admit, then render, then Take. Render reaches ScalarSubquerySink::EncodeProjectedRow (:10992-10996) and ParkedRows::Park (:10943-10946), an unconditional push_back. OutputSort::Admit with a full bounded heap returns PendingBefore(front) and never reaches the max_rows_ check (src/exec/sort.cpp:87-98). Take's eviction only swaps the index text back into the caller's scratch (sort.cpp:104-116). ParkedRows has Park and Take only, and Take is called from Emit for the drained survivors (:7304-7311, :11003-11008), so every admitted row's AstValue lives until the sink dies. Reachability. tests/set_expr_subquery_test.cpp:197-208 pins `UPDATE t SET v = (SELECT x FROM u ORDER BY x DESC LIMIT 1) WHERE id = 1` as admitted and as running the sorted path. That test checks the value returned, not memory. The bound not named: the per-statement row-touch budget. max_rows_touched defaults to 100,000,000 (include/kds/exec/budget.hpp:61, docs/spec/parser-v2.md:233, manual/server/server.md:155) and refuses the walk ResourceExhausted past it. So parked rows are capped at the budget, not unbounded: about 96 bytes per AstValue (include/kds/parser/ast.hpp:69-114: two std::string members plus scalars), which is roughly 10 GB at the default before vector doubling and any varchar payload, and unbounded only at `max_rows_touched = 0`. The finder's 'no refusal' holds for its scenario of tens of millions of rows, which is under the budget. Other limits, none defeating it. Growth equals heap admissions, so it is O(N) only when walk order tracks sort order (an append-ordered ledger with ORDER BY ts DESC LIMIT 1); random order gives about k ln(N/k). An index on the sort column, or ORDER BY the tuple key DESC, takes CV's ordered walk and no sorter. Without a LIMIT, or with one above sort_max_rows, Admit's cap applies. Record. docs/spec/types.md 3.2b states no memory bound for a SET subquery. known-gaps.md has no entry. F29 (docs/pending/whole-engine-audit-unverified-findings.md:278-280) describes ParkedRows as a duplication fold only. No document names it as an accepted cost. Severity low: memory held for one statement, no wrong result, and the trigger is a SET subquery that sorts tens of millions of unindexed rows, which already holds its core for the whole inline walk. The cost is that a top-level SELECT of the same shape holds one row where this holds one value per admission.

### H15. A CREATE the catalog refuses as policy leaves its candidate in BUILDING, charging the page budget for a Cabin that does not exist

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `exec-cabin-transition` (`exec-cabin-transition#3`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:310`; origin: pre-3.4 (PHY controller; not blamed)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Decide moves a confirmed candidate to kBuilding with m.pages = estimate before the CREATE runs. When Catalog::CreateCabin refuses with InvalidArgument, Unsupported, NotImplemented or NotFound, ApplyCreate deliberately does not call NoteBuildFailed, so the entry stays in kBuilding for the life of the process. pages_committed() counts every non-CANDIDATE entry, so the estimate stays charged against cabin_optimizer_page_budget (PO6). known-gaps.md records the parked entry only for a reused dropped column position and calls it performance only; the budget charge is not recorded.

**Evidence.** cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:310-317 skips NoteBuildFailed for the four codes and returns OK. cabin_optimizer.cpp:168 `if (managed.state != State::kCandidate) pages += managed.pages;`. cabin_optimizer.cpp:432-433 `m.state = State::kBuilding; m.pages = estimate;`. catalog.cpp:3548-3555 refuses a NO CABIN column with InvalidArgument.

**Scenario.** With cabin_optimizer on, a hot `SELECT ... WHERE c = ?` filters a large relation on a column declared NO CABIN (RecordOptimizerSignals nominates the lowest equality column whatever its policy). After the confirm snapshots Decide emits CREATE with estimate = max(1, p_rel pages / create_estimate_divisor); CreateCabin refuses; the entry parks in BUILDING. From then on pages_committed() includes the phantom estimate, so later candidates fail budget admission or take the swap path, which drops a real ACTIVE Cabin to make room, until a restart. No query result changes.

**Suggested.** On a policy refusal keep the entry parked but set its pages to 0 (or add a refused state that pages_committed() skips), so a settled refusal still stops retries without holding budget.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f, worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. Traced end to end and found no guard. (1) Nomination consults no policy: src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:10794-10840 (RecordOptimizerSignals) names the first kFilterScan step's access_columns.front() as the candidate; a grep for cabin_policy/CabinPolicy outside src/catalog finds no use in the signal path, the controller (src/stats/cabin_optimizer.cpp) or the executor (src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp). (2) src/stats/cabin_optimizer.cpp:376-438 (Decide, kCandidate): after confirm_snapshots it computes estimate = max(1, (p_rel>>16)/create_estimate_divisor), then sets m.state = kBuilding, m.pages = estimate (432-433) and emits CREATE. case kBuilding (442-443) is 'Execute owns the transition; nothing to decide'. (3) src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:296-317 (ApplyCreate): on a CreateCabin failure with kInvalidArgument, kUnsupported, kNotImplemented or kNotFound it skips NoteBuildFailed and returns OK; the comment says the candidate 'parks in BUILDING' on purpose. src/catalog/catalog.cpp:3553-3558 refuses a NO CABIN column with InvalidArgument (the dropped-position refusal at 3541-3545 is InvalidArgument too). (4) src/stats/cabin_optimizer.cpp:165-171: pages_committed() sums pages of every non-kCandidate entry, so the parked estimate is charged at the admission test, line 389. (5) Nothing ever clears the entry: the only mutators are NoteCreated, NoteBuildFailed (186), NoteExtended and NoteDropped (205, matched by cabin_id, which is 0 here and is only called from ApplyDrop, exec.cpp:469); there is no Reset/Clear and no managed_.clear(). The entry lasts for the process. Consequence as claimed: later confirmed candidates see pages_committed()+estimate > page_budget and either wait or take the swap path (390-430), which emits a DROP of the weakest ACTIVE Cabin. Defaults (include/kds/stats/cabin_optimizer.hpp:123, :167) are page_budget 1024 and divisor 8, so one refused candidate on a relation of about 8,192 pages or more charges the whole budget. Not already-known in this form: docs/inflight/known-gaps.md:1619-1624 (parked BUILDING on a reused dropped position, 'Performance only') and :3222-3227 (park on a NotFound it cannot tell from permanent) record the park but not the budget charge; docs/spec/cabin-controller.md PO5/PO6 do not mention it. docs/spec/cabin.md:628 says of NO CABIN 'auto-creation will never consider it', which the nomination path contradicts (it is considered, confirmed and refused at the catalog door). No test pins the parked entry's pages (tests/cabin_optimizer_test.cpp drives pages_committed only on created/failed/dropped edges). Severity low: no query result changes; the controller is off by default; and under PO11 a controller-made Cabin serves a read only through an administrator's plan naming it, and a Cabin so named is RESTRICTed from the swap's DROP (exec.cpp:86-90), so what the phantom can evict is unnamed Cabins and FK reverse-check acceleration.

### H16. The decimal literal parser overflows a signed integer in its scale-up loop before the precision check that refuses the value

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `exec-expr-query` (`exec-expr-query#3`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/type_literals.cpp:273`; origin: pre-3.4 (types milestone; last touched before v3.4.0)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** `ParseDecimalInto` bounds the digit walk by `digit_cap` (18 for int64, 38 for int128), so `absorb` cannot overflow. The loop that then scales a short fraction up to the column's scale multiplies `unscaled` by 10 with no check. The `digits > precision` refusal comes only after the loop. An over-long literal therefore overflows a signed int64 (or __int128), which is undefined behaviour, before it is refused. In a release build the wrapped value is discarded and the refusal is correct. The BS `asan` build compiles with `-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=undefined` (CMakeLists.txt:21), so by that flag the same literal aborts the server there, and the libFuzzer targets hit the same trap.

**Evidence.** type_literals.cpp:272-275: `for (std::size_t i = frac.size(); i < scale; ++i) { unscaled *= 10; ++digits; }`, followed at 276 by `if (digits > precision) { return Status::OutOfRange(...`. Reachable from the encode and from literal coercion: row_codec.cpp:326 and :773 (`ParseDecimalLiteral(text, precision, scale)`), where an integer literal arrives as `std::to_string(val.int_val)`.

**Scenario.** Column `d decimal(18,2)`. `INSERT INTO t (id, d) VALUES (1, 999999999999999999)`, or `WHERE d = 999999999999999999`. The 18 digits pass `absorb`; the first `unscaled *= 10` gives 9,999,999,999,999,999,990, above INT64_MAX. In release the value wraps, digits becomes 20 and the statement is refused OutOfRange as intended. Under the sanitizer build the process aborts on a statement that should only be refused. The wide path overflows the same way for a 38-digit literal on a decimal128 column with scale above 0.

**Suggested.** Check before multiplying: refuse when `digits + (scale - frac.size()) > precision` ahead of the loop, or use `__builtin_mul_overflow` inside it. Add a cell with an 18-digit integer literal against decimal(18,2).

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] src/exec/type_literals.cpp:250-275. `absorb` range-checks `digits > digit_cap` before each `unscaled * 10 + d`, so the digit walk is safe for 18 digits (int64) and 38 (Int128). The scale-up loop at 272-275 then does `unscaled *= 10; ++digits;` with no check, and the `digits > precision` refusal is at 276, after the loop. For decimal(18,2) and the literal 999999999999999999, the 18 digits pass absorb and the first multiply gives 9,999,999,999,999,999,990, above INT64_MAX: signed overflow, undefined behaviour. A 17-digit literal at scale 2 overflows on the second multiply. The function's header comment (213-217) says the multiply 'can never overflow', which holds for absorb only. No caller guards it: `ParseDecimalLiteral` (288-291) checks precision and scale only; src/exec/row_codec.cpp:326 passes the string through; row_codec.cpp:765-773 refuses only an integer literal with more digits than 18 by its raw text, then passes `std::to_string(val.int_val)`, so an 18-digit integer literal reaches the loop. The wide path (294-297, row_codec.cpp:357 and :812) is the same for a 38-digit literal at scale above 0. The outcome in a default build is as the finder says: any overflow implies digits after the loop is at least 19 (39 wide), above any legal precision, so the wrapped value is always discarded and OutOfRange returned; no wrong value is stored. CMakeLists.txt:19-23 builds KDS_SANITIZE=address with `-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=undefined`, so that build aborts on it. No existing cell hits it: tests/types_contract_test.cpp:281 inserts the 18-digit literal into decimal(18, 0), where the loop runs zero times. Not in known-gaps.md or the docs/pending audits (grep for type_literals and ParseDecimal finds nothing).

### H17. Forward ordered index walk never advances past its first leaf when pages_per_slice is 1

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `exec-vm` (`exec-vm#1`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1884`; origin: CV-S2 (10e7b0a4)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**; second verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** In RunOrderedIndexStep a pass that ends on `slice_due` re-seeks from `from`, which is the last entry of the leaf just read, so the seek lands on that same leaf. With pages_per_slice == 1 that re-read alone makes the slice due again, the pass breaks before `cur = right`, and the walk yields forever without reaching the right sibling. The comment at 1882-1883 says the next seek 'resumes past what this leaf held'; it does not, it re-reads the leaf.

**As narrowed by verification.** Latent only, no shipped path: RunOrderedIndexStep's forward run requires pages_per_slice >= 2 to make progress and nothing states or enforces that minimum (SlicePolicy, step_vm.hpp:346-347); at exactly 1 (or 0) it re-reads its first leaf and yields forever. No SQL statement, wire frame or configuration key can set the value - production passes only 16 or 64 (priority.hpp:44-46 via command_dispatcher.cpp:7072) - so the engine at fba5529f cannot hit it; it would be hit by a test or a future change that lowers PagesPerSlice (marked `[provisional]` in priority.hpp). What is reachable today is only that each result leaf after the first costs two counted leaf reads (the CV-R3 re-seek plus the hop right), so a 64-page slice covers about 32 result leaves - consistent with CV-R4/B3's 'every leaf read counted', an unstated pacing fact rather than a defect - and the comment at step_vm.cpp:1882-1883 does not mention the re-read. No ordered-walk cell runs below 64.

**Evidence.** 1884: `const bool slice_due = outermost && slice_ != nullptr && ++slice_pages_ >= slice_->pages_per_slice;` 1886: `if (end || !batch.empty() || slice_due) break;` 1887: `cur = right;` YieldAtSliceBoundary resets slice_pages_ to 0 (2615) and the next pass starts with IndexSeekLeaf(from) (1838). SlicePolicy states no minimum (step_vm.hpp:347) and tests/exec_chain_test.cpp:622 already drives a relation walk with `pages_per_slice = 1`.

**Scenario.** ExecuteAsync with SlicePolicy{pages_per_slice = 1} over `SELECT ... WHERE a = 1 ORDER BY b LIMIT n` on index (a, b), where the range spans two or more index leaves and the first leaf does not satisfy the LIMIT. Pass 1 reads L0 and emits its rows. Every later pass seeks to L0, skips the entry equal to `from`, counts one page, hits slice_due and yields. The statement never completes and never errors; only a cancel ends it. Latent today: production passes only 64 or 16 (server/priority.hpp PagesPerSlice), so no shipped configuration reaches it. At 2 the walk progresses but yields after every result leaf, and at 64 a slice covers about 32 result leaves because each is counted twice.

**Suggested.** Do not let slice_due end a pass that has not moved: count the slice only for a leaf that contributed to the batch or advanced `from` past the previous pass's value, or evaluate slice_due after stepping to `right`. Add an ordered-walk cell at pages_per_slice = 1 beside the relation walk's.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. /home/louis/ckdbs/.claude/worktrees/audit-v3-4-and-architecture/src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1833-1897 traced with pages_per_slice == 1, outermost, slice_ set. Pass 1: seek(probe.low) -> L0, entries collected, `from` = last entry of L0 (1875-1879), `++slice_pages_ >= 1` true (1884-1885), break at 1886 before `cur = right` (1887); ResolveBatch; 1894 yields and YieldAtSliceBoundary zeroes slice_pages_ (2615). Pass 2: IndexSeekLeaf(from) (1838) lands on L0 again, because a leaf covers [its separator, right sibling's first entry) and the separator is the sibling's first entry (include/kds/storage/index/index_tree.hpp:75-80, 205-211; src/storage/index/index_tree.cpp:561-581 says a seek lands at or left of the key's leaf, never right). LowerBound(from) = n-1, the one entry equals `from` and is skipped (1858-1860), batch stays empty, `start < n` so `from` is reassigned the same value, right != invalid so end stays false, slice_due is true again at count 1, break, empty ResolveBatch, not end, yield. Every later pass is identical: no guard advances `cur` or `from`, PageChainGuard is per pass, and only TakeCancel in YieldAtSliceBoundary (2616, 2628) ends it. A stale descent landing left of L0 does not help (start == n, from unchanged, same break). The comment at 1882-1883 is wrong as the claim says. Latency also confirmed: the only production setters are PagesPerSlice = 16 or 64 (include/kds/server/priority.hpp:44-46; src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:7072, 3567/3585), SlicePolicy states no minimum (include/kds/exec/step_vm.hpp:346-347), and the only pages_per_slice = 1 cell (tests/exec_chain_test.cpp:622) drives a relation walk; the ordered rig cells (tests/walk_slice_rig_test.cpp:338-391) go through the dispatcher at 64. The secondary figures hold too: for >= 2 each pass re-reads the previous leaf and reads one new one, so a slice of 64 covers about 32 result leaves plus one wasted descent when the count falls due on the re-read. Counting every leaf read and letting a due slice end the pass was a deliberate review fix (instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-cv-ordered-index-walk.md:440-442, B3), but neither that entry, index.md IX8c nor known-gaps.md 'Ordered walks (CV)' (2844-2897) states the no-progress case or the halved slice. The reverse run (1913-1980) is not affected: it moves `bound` below the leaf's first entry before counting.

**Second verifier (narrowed).** [source-read] at fba5529f, worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. The mechanics hold: in RunOrderedIndexStep (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1833-1897) with pages_per_slice == 1, pass 1 reads L0, sets `from` to L0's last entry (1875-1879), `++slice_pages_ >= 1` breaks before `cur = right` (1884-1887), and YieldAtSliceBoundary zeroes the count (2615). Pass 2's IndexSeekLeaf(from) lands on L0 again because a separator is the right sibling's first entry (src/storage/index/index_tree.cpp:442-446; index_tree.hpp:205-211), LowerBound(from) = n-1, the equal entry is skipped (1858-1860), `from` is reassigned the same value, the count is 1 again and the pass breaks with an empty batch. Nothing but TakeCancel ends it. I found no clamp on pages_per_slice in step_vm.cpp or SlicePolicy (step_vm.hpp:346-347). BUT the failure scenario is not reachable from SQL or the wire: the one production caller of ExecuteAsync is SelectRun (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:7239), whose policy is set only at 7072 from `constexpr PagesPerSlice` = 16 or 64 (include/kds/server/priority.hpp:44-46); GatherRun's 16 (3567/3585) drives a LeafCursor, not the VM; no config key, SET or wire field reaches the value; sim/ sets none. For any value >= 2 the walk progresses: a pass that falls due on the re-read yields with the count reset, and the next pass re-reads at count 1 and steps right. The only pages_per_slice = 1 cell (tests/exec_chain_test.cpp:621-622) is a relation walk under an EXISTS sub-chain; the ordered rig cells (tests/walk_slice_rig_test.cpp:338-391) run through the dispatcher at 64. The 'halved slice' remainder is reachable but is the design as written, not a defect: CV-R4's comment says the run yields 'counting every leaf it reads' (step_vm.cpp:1814-1816), B3 of the CV work order applied exactly that ('every leaf read counted toward the slice and a due slice ending the pass', workorder-cv-ordered-index-walk.md:439-442), and the re-read is a leaf read CV-R3 requires. The comment at 1882-1883 is imprecise rather than false (the next seek does resume past the leaf's entries, at the price of re-reading the leaf). Not recorded in known-gaps 'Ordered walks (CV)' (2844-2897), index.md IX8c or docs/pending.

### H18. index_entries_filtered counts rejected entries on an ordered step, against its stated meaning of descents avoided per row

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `exec-vm` (`exec-vm#3`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1994`; origin: CV-S2/S3 (CollectEntry, blamed to 7d11ba44)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**; second verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The counter's contract in step_vm.hpp:154-157 is one per row every entry of which the covering filter rejected, and RunIndexStep keeps it that way through filtered_tuple_keys_ (1594-1597, 1635). CollectEntry, used by both ordered runs, increments it once per rejected entry. A row with one stale failing entry and one passing entry is resolved and still counted as filtered, and a row with two failing entries counts twice.

**As narrowed by verification.** On an ordered index step (forward and reverse, via CollectEntry at src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1993-1994) index_entries_filtered is counted per rejected entry, which contradicts the per-row unit written in step_vm.hpp:153-157 and index.md §7 but is mostly the true count of descents avoided there, because an ordered run descends per entry (ResolveBatch claims a tuple key only on a matched resolve). It over-reports only where the rejected entry's descent was never owed: (a) a covered-only update leaves a stale entry under the same (key, tuple key) as an entry that passes - with or without the filter the row costs one descent, yet `index_filtered=1` is printed (index (a, b) COVERING (c), c updated 1 -> 2, `ANALYZE SELECT ... WHERE a = 1 AND c = 2 ORDER BY b LIMIT 10`); (b) a rejected stale entry met after its row was already emitted, which the seen set would have skipped anyway. The fix is not RunIndexStep's per-row set: either do not count a rejected entry whose tuple key is already in `emitted` and take back the count when a same-key sibling entry is resolved and matched, or restate the unit for ordered steps in step_vm.hpp and index.md §7. No test reads index_filtered on an ordered step.

**Evidence.** step_vm.hpp:154-157: `index_entries_filtered counts descents avoided and nothing else - one per row **every** entry of which the filter rejected (AT-S22: a row one entry rejects and another passes is resolved, and counts nothing here)` step_vm.cpp:1993-1994: `if (!kept.value()) { ++step_stats.index_entries_filtered;`

**Scenario.** Index (a, b) COVERING (c); a row's c is updated 1 -> 2, leaving two entries under one key. `ANALYZE SELECT ... WHERE a = 1 AND c = 2 ORDER BY b LIMIT 10` takes the ordered walk; the c = 1 entry is rejected and counted, and the row is then resolved through the c = 2 entry. The plan line (plan_printer.cpp:524-525) prints `index_filtered=1` for a descent that was made, while the same statement without the ORDER BY elision prints 0. The header calls this the only number that prices covering, so it over-reports covering's saving on ordered steps only.

**Suggested.** Either count per row as RunIndexStep does (track the rejected tuple keys per batch and remove one when another entry of it passes), or state in step_vm.hpp that on an ordered step the unit is the entry and give the plan line a distinct label.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. The contract is one per row every entry of which the filter rejected: include/kds/exec/step_vm.hpp:151-157 and docs/spec/index.md:356-371 ('`index_filtered` counts a row once, and only if every one of its entries failed'). RunIndexStep keeps it: a failing entry inserts into filtered_tuple_keys_, a later passing entry erases it, an already-seen key is skipped before the filter, and the counter takes the set's size once (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1588-1598, 1635). CollectEntry (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1984-2000), the only entry filter of both ordered runs (called at 1865 and 1955), does `++step_stats.index_entries_filtered` per rejected entry with no tuple-key tracking; ResolveBatch's `emitted` set (2004-2019) dedups resolves only and never adjusts the counter. So with index (a, b) COVERING (c) and a covered-only update leaving two entries under one (key, tuple key) (IX4b; index.md:360-364), the stale failing entry is counted and the row is still resolved through the passing one; a row with two failing entries counts 2. plan_printer.cpp:524-525 prints the counter unconditionally as `index_filtered=`, so the ordered step's line over-reports descents avoided where the unordered step prints 0 or 1. IX8c (index.md:408-422) confirms an ordered step runs the covering filter ('all filtered by the covering filter'), so the path is reachable. Nothing in the CV work order, IX8c or known-gaps.md 'Ordered walks (CV)' restates the unit as per entry for ordered steps, and the existing index_filtered cells (tests/index_contract_test.cpp:707-747, tests/index_compile_test.cpp:297) are unordered statements. Effect is confined to an ANALYZE meter; no result changes.

**Second verifier (narrowed).** [source-read] at fba5529f, worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. The code does what the claim says: CollectEntry (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1984-2000), the only entry filter of both ordered runs (called at 1865 and 1955), does `++step_stats.index_entries_filtered` per rejected entry with no tuple-key tracking, while RunIndexStep counts filtered_tuple_keys_.size() once and erases a key when a later entry passes (1588-1598, 1635). The stated unit is per row: step_vm.hpp:153-157, docs/spec/index.md §7 ('counts a row once, and only if every one of its entries failed'), pinned for the unordered step by tests/index_contract_test.cpp:741-747 (0 when a second entry passes, '1, not 2' when both fail), and AT-S22's record says a per-rejected-entry mutant was killed 3/3 (instructions/v3.0.0/workorder-at-m3-uniformity.md:503). No ordered-walk test reads index_filtered (tests/ordered_walk_test.cpp has no covering cell; walk_slice_rig_test.cpp:325 uses COVERING with no covered predicate), and neither the CV work order, IX8c nor known-gaps records a per-entry unit. Reachable from SQL: OrderedIndexChoiceOf (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:1397-1437) admits residual conjuncts when the ladder already chose an index prefix, so `WHERE a = 1 AND c = 2 ORDER BY b LIMIT n` over (a, b) COVERING (c) takes the ordered run with the covering filter live, and plan_printer.cpp:524-525 prints the counter. What the claim and the first verifier missed: the ordered run resolves per entry, not per row. ResolveBatch adds a tuple key to `emitted` only when ResolveOrdered returns matched (2012-2018), and ordered_matched_ is set only after the row passed its residual and the key check (AcceptTupleAt, key check at 3104-3108 after the residual; OrderedKeyMatches 2111-2114). So without the covering filter a row whose entries all fail the covered predicate would be descended once per entry, and the per-entry count IS the number of descents avoided there (two failing entries = two descents avoided) - the header's leading rule 'descents avoided and nothing else' holds, only its 'one per row' parenthetical does not. Likewise a stale failing entry under a different key than the visible version's is one descent truly avoided. The genuine over-report is narrower: a rejected entry whose descent would not have been made or would have been the row's only one - a covered-only update's stale entry under the same (key, tuple key) as the passing one (the claim's own scenario: 1 descent with or without the filter, index_filtered=1), or a stale entry met after its row was already emitted. So the claim's 'two failing entries counts twice' is not an over-count on an ordered step, and its first suggested fix (count per row as RunIndexStep does) would under-report there. Effect is one ANALYZE meter; no result changes.

### H19. A peer's idle-block burn exists only while wal_drain_interval_us is above 0; core 0's does not depend on it

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `expeditor-session` (`expeditor-session#3`)
- **Where**: `src/server/core_runtime.cpp:432`; origin: pre-3.4 (59ed9c03, AN-S1b, 2026-09-25)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** MaybeBurnIdleBlock is what stops an idle core's unspent id block from holding the instance's commit-order floor (AN-R13). Core 0 calls it from the fixed 50 ms writeback tick. A peer calls it only from a timer that is registered when wal_drain_interval_ns > 0. wal_drain_interval_us = 0 is a documented value, and with it no peer ever burns. txn.md line 708 states the tick's dependency for the carve-ahead; it does not state that the burn goes with it. Start's only warning for that setting is about relaxed commits.

**Evidence.** core_runtime.cpp:432-437: `if (config_.core_id != 0 && config_.wal_drain_interval_ns > 0) { scheduler_->SubmitEvery(config_.wal_drain_interval_ns, [this] { (void)txn_manager_->MaybeBurnIdleBlock(); txn_manager_->CarveAheadIfLow(); }); }` expeditor.cpp:2201-2221: core 0's call sits in `scheduler.SubmitEvery(kWritebackIntervalNs, ...)`, unconditional. expeditor.cpp:2255: `"drain cadence disabled; relaxed commits stay unsynced until checkpoint or shutdown"`.

**Scenario.** cores = 2, wal_drain_interval_us = 0. A session runs one transaction on core 1, then that core goes idle (the client disconnects, or the balancer or partition moves its sessions to core 0). Core 0 keeps committing. Core 1's issue cursor is now the floor candidate, so PinsFloor(1) holds, but nothing on core 1 ever calls MaybeBurnIdleBlock. By instance_visibility.hpp's description the floor cannot pass core 1's cursor, Reclaim drops nothing, and the commit window gains one entry per commit for the rest of the run (reclaim_at_ doubles at instance_visibility.cpp:418). Every visibility answer for those ids goes through the window instead of the floor. Not traced: whether the window has another release path.

**Suggested.** Give the peer's burn and carve-ahead tick its own fixed interval, as core 0's has, independent of wal_drain_interval_ns. Otherwise refuse or warn about wal_drain_interval_us = 0 above one core, naming the floor.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing built or run. The code path holds as claimed and I found no guard. - `MaybeBurnIdleBlock` has exactly two production callers (grep over src, include, tests, sim, tools): src/server/expeditor.cpp:2218, inside core 0's unconditional `SubmitEvery(kWritebackIntervalNs, ...)` at :2201, and src/server/core_runtime.cpp:435, inside `if (config_.core_id != 0 && config_.wal_drain_interval_ns > 0)` at :432. A peer with the interval at 0 registers no burn timer. - `wal_drain_interval_us = 0` is an admitted value: docs/spec/client-manual.md:122 and manual/server/server.md:152 say "0 disables", kds.conf.sample:242-246 names only relaxed commits as the consequence, and the only boot warning (expeditor.cpp:2255) is about relaxed commits. Nothing refuses it above one core; expeditor.cpp:1955 hands the value to each peer unchanged. - No other release path exists. src/txn/manager.cpp:618-632 says in its own comment that the window shrinks only inside `Reclaim()`. `Reclaim()` (src/txn/instance_visibility.cpp:357-420) bounds `reachable` by `FloorCandidate()`, which is the minimum issue cursor over attached cores (:196-211). An idle peer's cursor moves only in `Begin` or a burn, so once core 0 carves a block above it the floor cannot pass it. `PublishCommit` still calls `Reclaim` at `reclaim_at_`, which drops nothing and doubles the trigger (:418). Two corrections to the claim as written, neither defeating it: 1. The spec does state the dependency in two adjacent paragraphs: docs/spec/txn.md:708 (a peer's tick runs only while the interval is above 0) and :717-719 (the burn rides "a peer's its drain tick"). What no document states is the consequence. docs/inflight/known-gaps.md:498-506 describes the same pinned-for-good floor for a core that stops and calls it "Not reachable today"; this setting reaches it by configuration. 2. The consequence is wider than the window's memory. include/kds/txn/manager.hpp:304-323 hands the undo purge the floor, not the horizon (`undo_.SetHorizonSource` returns `visibility_->Floor()`), so a pinned floor also stops undo page reclamation for the rest of the run. `ResolvedForEveryReader` (manager.cpp:809-813) and read_view.hpp:111 fall from the floor's fast branch to the latched window lookup for every id above it. Precedent that this is an unrecorded residue, not a decision: instructions/v3.0.0/workorder-aw-m1-close.md:780-784 found the lifetime-ceiling sweep going dark under the same `if`, and txn.md:98-99 now gives that sweep a timer of its own; the burn was left on the drain tick. Severity low: the default is 1000 us, the cost is unbounded memory and undo growth and never a wrong answer, and it needs cores >= 2 with a peer that stays idle (for example every session on core 0). Not traced: the undo purge's own code beyond its horizon source, and the exact block arithmetic that puts core 0's later blocks above an idle peer's opening cursor (taken from the comments at instance_visibility.cpp:404-409 and expeditor.cpp:2211-2216).

### H20. An ordered index run counts one `probes`/`ranges` per leaf pass, not per walk between a pair of bounds

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `index-fk-assert-delta` (`index-fk-assert-delta#1`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1840`; origin: CV-S2 10e7b0a4 (forward) and CV-S3 7d11ba44 (reverse), over DF-S3 83aad532's counter
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** `StepStats::index_walks` is the unit of `sys.indexes`' `probes` and `ranges` (DF-Q21 (a), sys-views.md 6.2b: "the times the step walked it between one pair of bounds"). RunIndexStep bumps it once per pair of bounds (step_vm.cpp:1550). CV's ordered runs bump it inside their re-seek loop: the forward run once per pass (each batch-bearing leaf or due slice, line 1840), the reverse run once per leaf it reads (line 1935). One ordered walk between one pair of bounds therefore adds as many `probes`/`ranges` as it read leaves, and RecordIndexUse (command_dispatcher.cpp:10566) folds that number unchanged.

**Evidence.** step_vm.cpp:1838-1840 inside `for (;;)`: `auto first = index::IndexSeekLeaf(...); ... ++step_stats.index_walks;` and step_vm.cpp:1932-1935: `auto held = index::IndexFetchLeafAtOrBelow(...); ... ++step_stats.index_walks;`. command_dispatcher.cpp:10566: `slot.counts->Add(step.kind == exec::AccessKind::kIndexRange, counts.index_walks, ...)`. docs/spec/sys-views.md:390-391: "the times the step walked it between one pair of bounds - `probes` for an equality step, `ranges` for a range step".

**Scenario.** `SELECT * FROM t WHERE user_id = 5 ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 500` over index (user_id, created_at), the user's entries spread over 6 index leaves: one statement, one pair of bounds, and `sys.indexes.probes` for the index rises by 6 (by the number of passes for the ascending form). An operator reading `probes`/`ranges` as uses of the index, or dividing `entries_scanned` by it, gets a figure that depends on leaf fill and LIMIT depth rather than on how often the index served a read. No engine decision reads the counter, so the damage is to the report only.

**Suggested.** Count the walk once per ordered run (bump `index_walks` before the pass loop, after LiveIndexOf succeeded and the first seek returned), or state in sys-views.md 6.2b and index_stats.hpp that an ordered run counts one per re-seek.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture. src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1465-1470: RunIndexStep dispatches an elided ORDER BY to RunReverseOrderedIndexStep / RunOrderedIndexStep before the ordinary path, so the ordinary collect lambda (step_vm.cpp:1545-1550, one `++step_stats.index_walks` per pair of bounds) is never reached for these steps. Forward run: step_vm.cpp:1834-1840, the outer `for (;;)` re-seeks with `IndexSeekLeaf(..., from)` and bumps `++step_stats.index_walks` on every pass; a pass ends at step_vm.cpp:1889 as soon as the batch is non-empty, a slice is due, or the range ends, so a walk over N entry-bearing leaves makes about N passes and N bumps. Reverse run: step_vm.cpp:1929-1935, one `IndexFetchLeafAtOrBelow` per leaf and `++step_stats.index_walks` per leaf read (the terminating re-descent that returns `previous` returns at 1934 before the bump, so it is exactly one per leaf read). No guard resets or clamps the counter: StepStats merge is a plain sum (step_vm.cpp:3469), and CommandDispatcher::RecordIndexUse (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:10558-10568) passes `counts.index_walks` unchanged into IndexUse::Add as probes or ranges. The declared unit contradicts this: include/kds/exec/step_vm.hpp:164-167 ('the times the step walked its index between one pair of bounds: one an opening of an equality or a range step, one a value of a key set'), include/kds/stats/index_stats.hpp:19-21, docs/spec/sys-views.md:388-391 (6.2b), and DF-Q21 (a) in instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-df-statistics-coverage.md:386. Nothing documents a per-leaf unit for ordered runs: sys-views.md 6.2b has no mention of the ordered or reverse walk, grep for `index_walks` across docs/, instructions/, tests/, sim/, bench/ finds only the two DF work order lines, tests/index_stats_test.cpp has no ORDER BY case, and the CV entries in docs/inflight/known-gaps.md (around 2853-2896) speak of descents per leaf as a cost, not of the usage counters. git log -L attributes line 1840 to CV-S2 10e7b0a4. Bounds on the damage, which keep it low: an ordered run exists only under a LIMIT (the elision), so the over-count is the number of leaves read before the LIMIT stops the walk; `entries_scanned` and `rows_resolved` are counted correctly (CollectEntry, step_vm.cpp:1987); and nothing in the engine reads the count (index_stats.hpp:13, DF-R3), so only the operator-facing `sys.indexes.probes`/`ranges` report is off. Small precision note on the finder's wording: the forward run counts per re-seek pass (a leaf that adds nothing to the batch is followed by its sibling within the same pass, step_vm.cpp:1890), not strictly per leaf, which the claim's body already states.

### H21. A cancel observed at the next frame swallows a C_SYNC: S_ERROR is sent, no S_READY follows

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `net-wire` (`net-wire#2`)
- **Where**: `src/server/kwp_session.cpp:401`; origin: pre-3.4 (BA-S15, 0064d8bd, 2026-10-10)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The cancel arm in KwpSession::OnFrame runs before the frame-type switch and before the skip-to-sync arm. It refuses whatever frame comes next, and Refuse arms skipping_to_sync_. When that frame is itself a C_SYNC, the session emits S_ERROR(CANCELLED), consumes the sync and returns. No S_READY is sent, and the session now discards frames until another C_SYNC. protocol.md section 3 item 4 says S_READY is 'also sent after every C_SYNC', and section 5 says frames are discarded 'until the next C_SYNC, then answers S_READY'. The frame that carried the barrier gets no answer. known-gaps.md records that a cancel 'refuses the session's next frame' but not that the refused frame can be the barrier itself. The only cells that exercise this path use C_PING as the next frame and then a separate C_SYNC (tests/kwp_session_test.cpp:455-461, 1081).

**Evidence.** kwp_session.cpp:401-403 `if (phase_ == Phase::kReady && session_.TakeCancel()) { session_.Poison(); return Refuse(out, ...Cancelled("cancelled by request"))); }` kwp_session.cpp:438-442 the skip arm that would have answered the sync with SendReady is below it and is not reached for this frame.

**Scenario.** A client pipelines [C_EXECUTE(UPDATE ...), C_SYNC] in one write and, while the UPDATE waits on a lock, sends C_CANCEL from a second connection. A write does not observe a cancel (protocol.md section 10), so it runs to its end and S_COMPLETE is sent. TcpServer::OnStatementComplete then calls DrainFrames, which pops the C_SYNC; the cancel arm answers S_ERROR(CANCELLED) and nothing else. The client has already sent its barrier and waits for an S_READY that never comes; the session stays in skip mode until an extra C_SYNC arrives. The reference client tools/kwp.py is not affected, because it always puts a C_CLOSE between EXECUTE and SYNC and the C_CLOSE absorbs the refusal. A driver that sends EXECUTE then SYNC, as section 5 describes, hangs.

**Suggested.** In the cancel arm, when the frame is C_SYNC, send the S_ERROR and then S_READY and leave skipping_to_sync_ clear; or let C_SYNC through and take the cancel at the next non-barrier frame. Add a cell whose next frame is C_SYNC.

**First verifier (confirmed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. src/server/kwp_session.cpp:382-404: OnFrame handles only C_TERMINATE before the cancel arm; at :401 `phase_ == kReady && session_.TakeCancel()` poisons the session and returns Refuse(...Cancelled) for whatever frame it is, with no test of the frame type. Refuse (:371-380) emits S_ERROR and sets skipping_to_sync_ = true, and returns without SendReady. The skip-to-sync arm (:438-443) and the kSync case (:446-448) that would answer S_READY sit below and are not reached for that frame, so a C_SYNC that meets a pending cancel is consumed with S_ERROR(CANCELLED) only, and the session then discards frames until a further C_SYNC. The flag is cleared nowhere but TakeCancel (include/kds/server/session.hpp:243-244; other uses in tcp_server.cpp:728 and command_dispatcher.cpp:766 only load it, and the walk's slice_.TakeCancel at command_dispatcher.cpp:3137-3176/3369 is the SELECT-walk and build path), so a cancel that lands during a write, a point read or between statements survives to the next frame, and TcpServer::OnStatementComplete re-enters DrainFrames (tcp_server.cpp:1007) where a pipelined C_SYNC is the next frame popped. docs/spec/protocol.md:59 says S_READY is 'also sent after every C_SYNC' and :87 says frames are discarded 'until the next C_SYNC, then answers S_READY'; §10 (:135) and known-gaps.md:1121-1123 say only that the cancel 'refuses the session's next frame' and do not state that the refused frame may be the barrier or that it then goes unanswered, so this is not already recorded. The cells that reach the arm use C_PING as the next frame and a separate C_SYNC (tests/kwp_session_test.cpp:455-461, and the registry cell around :1081); none sends C_SYNC as the refused frame. Impact is narrow: it needs a cancel that no walk took and a client whose next frame is the C_SYNC itself; such a client waits for an S_READY that needs a second C_SYNC to arrive. No data is affected. Pre-3.4 code (BA-S15).

### H22. Four parser refusals carry no byte position

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `parser` (`parser#4`)
- **Where**: `src/parser/parser.cpp:3233`; origin: pre-3.4 (blame: boundary commit 1a34afbe for 3233)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** CLAUDE.md's working rule ('Every refusal carries the byte position of the offending token') and parser-v2.md's lexer paragraph ('every error position ... is exact') are not met at four sites: the native join's missing ON (3233), CREATE TABLE with no column (1762), a statement whose first token is not a word (3734) and an unknown leading keyword (3881). Every other InvalidArgument/Unsupported/NotImplemented return in the file (about 150, scanned by script) names a byte. ExpectKeyword/ExpectToken were fixed once for this reason (comments at 99-100, 111-114).

**As narrowed by verification.** Three reachable parser refusals carry no byte position, not four: the native join's missing ON (src/parser/parser.cpp:3232-3234), a first token that is not a word (3733-3736) and an unknown leading word (3879-3882). The fourth, 'CREATE TABLE requires at least one column' (parser.cpp:1761-1763), is unreachable rather than unpositioned: the column loop at 1748-1758 calls ParseColumnDef and pushes a column before it can break, and returns ParseColumnDef's own status on failure, so `stmt.columns.empty()` is never true there - it is a dead check. The missing-ON refusal does reach a client: TryQueryLayer (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11405-11411) returns nullopt when ParseQuery also fails (unless a lock clause was met), so the native, byte-less message is the reply even though the query grammar's own refusal at parser.cpp:4376-4378 carries At(cond.byte_offset).

**Evidence.** parser.cpp:3233-3234: `return Status::InvalidArgument("expected ON after JOIN, got '" + std::string(Describe(on)) + "'");`. parser.cpp:1762: `return Status::InvalidArgument("CREATE TABLE requires at least one column");`. parser.cpp:3880-3882: `"unknown SQL keyword '" + std::string(tok.text) + "' (supported: CREATE, DROP, ..."`.

**Scenario.** `SELECT a.x FROM a JOIN b WHERE a.x = 1` is refused 'expected ON after JOIN, got 'WHERE'' with no byte, where the same mistake in the query grammar (parser.cpp:4377) names it; a client or the difftest harness that reads the position from the message gets none.

**Suggested.** Append At(tok.byte_offset) at the four sites (the token is already in hand at each); 1762 can name the byte of the closing ')'. Note 3881's list of supported words is also stale (it omits REINDEX's siblings handled earlier in Parse).

**First verifier (narrowed).** [source-read] at fba5529f. Read each cited site with its surroundings. 3233: message is built from Describe(on) with no byte_offset. 3734 and 3881: built from tok.text only; manual/sql/sql.md:2414 documents the 'unknown SQL keyword' reply in exactly that byte-less form, which records the behaviour but is not a ruling that exempts it from CLAUDE.md's 'every refusal carries the byte position' rule or token.hpp:171 ('This is what lets every refusal carry an exact position (J2)'). No test, sim or script matches these three message texts (grep over src, tests, sim, scripts outside parser.cpp: none), so adding a position breaks no pinned string in the tree apart from the manual line. known-gaps.md lists other byte-less refusals (lines 718, 1600, 2046, 3228) but none of these parser sites. Not checked: the finder's 'about 150 other returns all name a byte' count, and whether 3881's supported-word list is stale.

### H23. ALTER PLAN answers a refusal for a change that is already in force and durable; sys.plans then lists the superseded row as live

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `plans-cx` (`plans-cx#2`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/plan_catalog.cpp:800`; origin: CX-S1b (ReplaceAdminPlan); the view's missing rule is CX-S1b / DI-S4
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**; second verifier: **narrowed**
- **Recorded in**: Partly: docs/inflight/known-gaps.md 'What CX-S1b's cells do not reach' (lines 2627-2629); include/kds/exec/plan_catalog.hpp:104-107; docs/spec/parser-v2.md I12 - the leftover two-row state only, not the reply or the view

**Claim (the finder's).** ReplaceAdminPlan inserts the new row, installs it in the instance table, and only then deletes the old row; if that delete fails it returns the failure, so the client is told the ALTER was refused although the new plan is serving and its row is logged. The 'newest row of a name is the plan' rule that covers the leftover is written separately in LoadPlans (plan_catalog.cpp:671-682), RowsNamed/FindPlan (737-759) and DROP TABLE's `superseded` lambda (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:4921-4926), and is not applied at all in the sys.plans view (src/server/sys_views.cpp:999-1032), which prints every row with its own flag.

**As narrowed by verification.** When ReplaceAdminPlan's removal of the old row fails after the new row is inserted and installed (src/exec/plan_catalog.cpp:798-803), ALTER PLAN answers a refusal although the change is in force in the instance's table on every core and its row is logged (not awaited durable). Until the next mount or a DROP PLAN, sys.plans (src/server/sys_views.cpp:992-1050) lists both rows of the name, the superseded one with its old state, because the view does not apply the newest-row-of-a-name rule that LoadPlans, RowsNamed/FindPlan and DROP TABLE's RESTRICT each spell separately; repeating the same ENABLE/DISABLE answers ALTERED PLAN and writes nothing, leaving the stale row. The leftover state itself is documented design; the misleading reply and the view's stale row are not. The trigger is rare: a chain-page fetch refused or failed between two walks of the same statement on a sys.plans chain grown past its resident root, or a log failure that fail-stops the instance anyway.

**Evidence.** plan_catalog.cpp:796-803: `// From here the new row is the plan, whatever becomes of the old one` / `admin.Install(ToAdminPlan(next), next.enabled);` / `if (auto removed = DeleteDeclarationsById(...); !removed.ok()) { return removed.status(); }`. command_dispatcher.cpp:14838-14839: `auto altered = alter_row(); if (!altered.ok()) return Refused(altered.status());`.

**Scenario.** `ALTER PLAN p DISABLE` while DeleteDeclarationsById fails (an I/O error, or a full pool's ResourceExhausted). The client receives an error. The plan is nevertheless disabled at once on every core, and both rows stay in sys.plans until the next mount: `SELECT * FROM sys.plans` shows p twice, the old id `enabled` and the new id `disabled`. A retry answers `ALTERED PLAN ... state=disabled` and writes nothing (row.enabled == enable), so the stale `enabled` row remains. An operator or agent reading the view or the first error concludes the plan is still in force.
