The stated hazard is overstated for the 3.4 channels. key_set_leaf_ is advisory by contract: a stale or wrong leaf changes only which OR leaf is evaluated first, never an answer, and an out-of-range leaf is ignored. The ordered channels do not inherit a previous caller's value: ordered_key_ and ordered_matched_ are reset around every call and ordered_collect_ is cleared after the walk, so an ordered step that reached AcceptTupleAt without them would match no row (fail closed, visibly empty) rather than emit a wrong row. What remains is a maintainability and readability point with no defect and no quiet-wrong path found.

**Evidence.** 690: `// Set per value, not once: a deeper step's own set overwrites it.` 1652-1655: `**Moved out of the member for phase 2, a correctness property.** The descent below re-enters RunStep for the next step, which may be another index step on this runner whose phase 1 clears this vector` 3394-3395: `set around one AcceptTupleAt and read only by the driving step, whose emit_order no inner step carries`

**Scenario.** No defect today. The risk is structural: ordered_collect_ silently turns AcceptTupleAt from emit into collect-and-withhold for any step whose emit_order is an index order, and key_set_leaf_ is correct only because every key-set caller reassigns it per row. A fourth access path that reaches AcceptTupleAt without setting or restoring one of these inherits the previous caller's value with no compile-time signal, on the function the spec makes the one place visibility and the residual are applied.

**Suggested.** Pass a small per-call context struct to AcceptTupleAt (first leaf, entry key to check or collector, out-flag for matched) instead of members, leaving on the runner only what is genuinely statement- or walk-scoped (recording_, building_). The seam is clean: all three 3.4 channels are set and read within one call.

### H74. Core 0 is not a CoreRuntime, so each per-core service is wired twice, and the two copies have drifted

- **Kind**: architecture; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `expeditor-session` (`expeditor-session#4`)
- **Where**: `src/server/expeditor.cpp:2273`; origin: pre-3.4 structure; the page-reader split is DE-S1 (0d3ee8fe). The F audit's fold at 3f8c499c added WireInstanceServices for six setters only
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**
- **Recorded in**: docs/pending/whole-engine-audit-unverified-findings.md F32 (the duplicated wiring and its fold)

**Claim (the finder's).** Expeditor is core 0's runtime ('core 0's runtime is this object') and CoreRuntime is every peer's, so everything per-core is written in both. WireInstanceServices covers six setters; the rest is still paired. The copies have drifted in at least three places: (a) the burn tick's cadence and gating (finding 3); (b) the page reader, which core 0 gets by a separate call in Start because WireInstanceServices hands it null at the mount; (c) CoreRuntime::CloseListener has no CurrentCoreGuard, though ~CoreRuntime, Sync, Checkpoint, ShutdownCheckpoint and ListenAndAttach each take one. So the shutdown tail rolls back a peer's open explicit transactions as core 0: page latch owner, contention slot and the WAL gate choice are all core 0's. No failure was found from (c), because every reactor is joined by then.

**As narrowed by verification.** The general statement (core 0 and peer wiring written twice) is the F audit's F32, applied at 3f8c499c as WireInstanceServices, with its remainder stated there. What remains at fba5529f: (1) the per-core cadences are still written in both Expeditor::Start and CoreRuntime::Run - drain lambda + post-task hook (expeditor.cpp:2228-2241, core_runtime.cpp:399-425), checkpoint tick (expeditor.cpp:2118-2121, core_runtime.cpp:451-455), burn/carve tick, suspend audit and strict wait scopes (core_runtime.cpp:463-467). (2) Two of the three 'drifts' are deliberate and commented, not accidental: core 0's burn/carve rides the kWritebackIntervalNs tick ungated (expeditor.cpp:2200-2221, comment 'riding this tick rather than taking one of its own') while a peer's rides the drain tick and is off when wal_drain_interval_ns == 0 (core_runtime.cpp:432-437) - a real gating asymmetry; and the page reader reaches core 0 by its own call (expeditor.cpp:1859) because WireInstanceServices hands null at the mount (expeditor.cpp:2273-2276, stated in its comment). (3) New and real: CoreRuntime::CloseListener (include/kds/server/core_runtime.hpp:348) resets the listener with no CurrentCoreGuard, and its one caller (expeditor.cpp:2370-2372, core 0's thread; expeditor.cpp contains no CurrentCoreGuard at all) supplies none, while ~CoreRuntime's identical listener_.reset() takes one and says why (core_runtime.cpp:30-34). So a peer's open explicit transactions are rolled back with CurrentCore() == 0: page latch owner (device_page_store.cpp:2945, 3010), contention slot (contention.hpp:256), leaf stats part (leaf_stats.cpp:14) and lock-table waiter core (lock_table.cpp:604) are core 0's. No failure established: workers are joined first (expeditor.cpp:2352), latches are acquired and released under the same value, and I did not trace whether the rollback can reach WalManager's debug-only owner check (src/wal/manager.cpp:220-228, aborts when CurrentCore() != core_id_ in the owning sync arm).

**Evidence.** Paired sites: - dispatcher wiring: expeditor.cpp:1186-1214 and core_runtime.cpp:274-306; - the drain lambda with its comment: expeditor.cpp:2228-2241 and core_runtime.cpp:399-415; - checkpoint cadence: expeditor.cpp:2118-2121 and core_runtime.cpp:451-455; - burn and carve tick: expeditor.cpp:2217-2221 and core_runtime.cpp:432-437; - suspend audit and strict wait scopes: expeditor.cpp:1847, 2340-2341 and core_runtime.cpp:463-467; - page reader: expeditor.cpp:1859 and 2276. core_runtime.hpp:348: `void CloseListener() noexcept { listener_.reset(); }`, called at expeditor.cpp:2371 from core 0's thread; core_runtime.cpp:30-34 says of the destructor's same reset 'This runs as this core, wherever it is called from'.

**Suggested.** Move the per-core cadences (drain hook and timer, checkpoint tick, burn and carve tick, audit and strict-scope install) into one function both Expeditor::Start and CoreRuntime::Run call, and extend WireInstanceServices to the remaining shared setters. Give CloseListener the CurrentCoreGuard its siblings have. Folding core 0 into a CoreRuntime is the larger form of the same change and is the operator's to decide.

### H75. shape_text stores the first statement of each shape verbatim, bind values included, while the 3.4 header comment says no value enters a shape

- **Kind**: architecture; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `sys-views` (`sys-views#3`)
- **Where**: `src/stats/statement_stats.cpp:105`; origin: pre-3.4 (BO-S5) for shape_text; the contradicting comment is DF-S1's (44dddd27, include/kds/stats/statement_stats.hpp:67); DI-S1 (1ffbf607) multiplies the rows per binding
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The engine holds two opposite rules on statement values in its statistics. CJ's WD11 says bind values are never recorded under any setting, CJ left arg_hash unpublished because it covers substituted binds, and DF-R6 says "no value enters a statistic". Yet StatementStats::Record copies the first 256 bytes of the dispatched line into shape_text, and that line is the KWP statement after C_BIND has substituted the parameters into the SQL text. sys.statement_stats is an engine-state view with no session filter, and sys_views.cpp applies no role check, so a session that can run a SELECT reads the literal and bind values of the first call of every shape other sessions ran (an INSERT's VALUES, an UPDATE's SET value, a WHERE key). DF-R6 was worded to step around this ("no column DF adds holds a statement's text"), but the header comment DF-S1 wrote states it without that qualifier and is false for the struct it describes. I did not trace where the statement-class roles gate a sys view, and they are off by default.

**As narrowed by verification.** The facts hold but the 'two opposite rules' framing does not. shape_text is the dispatched line's first 256 bytes verbatim (src/stats/statement_stats.cpp:100-105), and for KWP that line is the text after C_BIND substituted the parameters as SQL literals, so the first call's literals and bind values of every shape are readable through sys.statement_stats by any session that may SELECT (readonly role included; no role or session filter in sys_views.cpp). This is the documented BO design, not a contradiction of a binding rule: sys-views.md section 6.1 and workorder-bo line 379 define shape_text as 'the first statement seen, cut to 256 bytes'; WD11 is scoped to ASH records, and DF-R6 is scoped to what DF adds ('no column DF adds holds a statement's text'). What remains is (a) the unqualified header comment at include/kds/stats/statement_stats.hpp:67 ('No value of a statement enters a shape (DF-R6)') is false for the struct it sits above, whose field at :82 is shape_text, and (b) no spec, manual or known-gaps entry says in so many words that shape_text carries literal and bind values to every reader, while known-gaps.md:2291-2295 withholds arg_hash on exactly that ground (it covers bind values), so the engine is stricter about a hash of the values than about the values themselves.

**Evidence.** statement_stats.cpp:105 `shape.shape_text = std::string(line.substr(0, cut));`. statement_stats.hpp:67 `// counts. No value of a statement enters a shape (DF-R6), and nothing in the`. kwp_session.hpp:293 `std::string sql;        // that statement's text with parameters substituted`. workorder-cj-wait-events-ash.md:77 has WD11: "Bind values are never recorded under any setting".

**Suggested.** Decide one rule and state it in sys-views.md section 6.1. If values must not leave a session, store the text with its literals written `?`, as sys.plans' statement and plan_advice's proposal already do (the lexer has the literal positions), or keep the pre-substitution statement text for KWP calls. If the exposure is accepted, correct the comment at statement_stats.hpp:67 and record in known-gaps.md that shape_text carries the first call's values to every reader of the view.

### H76. The statement boundary is a per-core dispatcher flag that every other statement's head clears: a resumed statement is safe only where a site re-sets it by hand, and for READ ONLY the re-mint would lift the lease off a running walk

- **Kind**: architecture; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `txn-locks-delta` (`txn-locks-delta#3`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:8989`; origin: the flag is pre-3.4; CS (adbea55b) added the READ ONLY branch whose re-mint also re-points the reader lease
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** `statement_boundary_taken_` (command_dispatcher.hpp:1411) is a fact about one statement kept in a member of the core's dispatcher. StampStatement clears it at every statement head on the core (command_dispatcher.cpp:1652), and other statements' heads run inside a suspended one (a SELECT walk yields every 64 pages). A statement that resumes therefore sees whatever the last head left. Two resume sites restore it by hand (the build tail at :3189, whose comment names the hazard, and the parked write's resume at :10880); a SelectRun's resume does not. If any code on a resumed statement of an explicit READ COMMITTED transaction reaches EnsureStatementBoundary (SnapshotFor, BeginWrite, or ResolveNames -> ViewFor -> MintResolveView while a DDL is open anywhere), the transaction's view is re-minted mid-statement: for a read-write transaction StartStatement replaces txn.view_ and republishes the bound; for a READ ONLY one RefreshReader re-points the session's only lease at the newer LSN, while the running walk still reads at its lease-less copy of the older view (SnapshotFor returns `{snap, no lease}` inside a transaction). Nothing would then hold ReadHorizon at the walk's snapshot, which is the condition CLAUDE.md marks [quiet-wrong] for PURGE's horizon gate. I found no reachable path today: ExecuteAsync and SelectRun::Finish resolve nothing through the session and the query layer's walks run to their end without yielding. The safety is an unstated contract between every suspension point and every resolver, not a property of the state.

**Evidence.** command_dispatcher.cpp:1649-1652 `// ... A plain member, cleared by every statement before it runs: another statement of this core runs inside a suspended one` / `statement_boundary_taken_ = false;`; :3187-3189 `// The head took the statement's boundary; a second would re-mint an explicit transaction's view mid-statement.` / `d_.statement_boundary_taken_ = true;`; :8996-8998 `ro->view = txn_->MintReadView(txn::kNoTrxId); txn_->RefreshReader(ro->lease, ro->view, statement_head_ns_);`.

**Suggested.** Move the flag to where the statement's other facts live: the session (cleared in StampStatement for that session only) or the SelectRun/WriteScope, so a head on the same core cannot clear another statement's boundary and the two hand-written restores go away. Alternatively have a transaction-bound SelectRun refuse a re-mint while it is live (an assert in EnsureStatementBoundary that no walk of this session is suspended).

## 6.6 Duplication (15)

### H77. The 'byte of this token, or the line's end' refusal helper is re-written as a local lambda in six dispatcher handlers, with three different guards

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `arch-config-errors` (`arch-config-errors#3`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:2792`; origin: 3.4.*: CX (SET JOIN_ORDER 2792-2797, DROP PLAN 2837-2842), CJ (SET ASH 2887-2891), CY (HandleStatistics 3757-3762); pre-3.4 copies at 13191 (SET PRIORITY) and 14150-14152 (HandleWaystone), plus RefuseWhole at 175
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Each handler that parses a raw line builds its own lambda computing `at.data() - line.data()` and appending ' (byte N)'. The copies have drifted: SET JOIN_ORDER, DROP PLAN and HandleStatistics treat a null OR empty token as the line's end; SET ASH treats only a null data pointer so; HandleWaystone's `at` has no guard at all (a default-constructed token would give `nullptr - line.data()`). A file-level `ByteSuffix(std::size_t)` already exists at 14476 but only the plan code uses it. The missing shared helper is also why HandleBegin, HandleSetCabinOptimizer (2765, 2772), HandleSetIsolation (13133) and HandleSetDurability carry no byte at all.

**As narrowed by verification.** The 'byte of this token, or the line's end' computation is written out six times in the dispatcher (five local lambdas in SET JOIN_ORDER, DROP PLAN, SET ASH, HandleStatistics and HandleWaystone, one inline expression in SET PRIORITY) with four guard variants; four of the copies are 3.4 work (CX, CJ, CY). The variants produce the same byte on every path reachable today, so this is duplication only. Separately, SET CABIN_OPTIMIZER (2765, 2772) and SET ISOLATION (13133) refusals carry no byte and known-gaps.md does not list them.

**Evidence.** 2793-2795: `const std::size_t byte = at.data() == nullptr || at.empty() ? line.size() : static_cast<std::size_t>(at.data() - line.data());` / 2888: `const std::size_t byte = at.data() == nullptr ? line.size() : static_cast<std::size_t>(at.data() - line.data());` / 14151-14152: `return static_cast<std::size_t>(word.data() - line.data());`

**Suggested.** One free function in the dispatcher's anonymous namespace - `Status LocatedAt(Status, std::string_view line, std::string_view token)` with the null-or-empty rule - used by all seven sites and by the handlers that today answer with no byte.

### H78. The write walk's head and its arm ladder (point, key set, collect, scan, park) are written three times: UPDATE, DELETE and FOR UPDATE

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: medium, confidence high); round 1, `arch-dispatcher-shape` (`arch-dispatcher-shape#2`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:12668`; origin: CU-S3 84bf6828 (key-set arm copied into UPDATE and DELETE, after F26's WriteWalk at 3f8c499c) and DC-S2 f6a12f5d (third copy for the locking read)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**
- **Recorded in**: instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-cu-key-set-access.md, CU-S3b review, 'Not taken: folding the single-key arm and the list arm of both statements into one WriteWalk member' (lines 810-816)

**Claim (the finder's).** F26 moved Qualify/Claim/CollectTargets/ParkedOutcome into WriteWalk, but the driver around them stayed with each statement, and CU-S3 and DC-S2 then each added to all copies. Three sites hand-keep the same sequence: the `*_at` lambda over LocateByTupleKey with the kAbsent/kAt/kScan mapping, the WriteKeyOf point arm and its four-way switch, the WriteKeySetOf arm, CollectTargets, `cursor.targets = walk.targets`, VisitRelation with the parked-row stop, and the parked epilogue. The head before it (ResolveNames/ResolveRelation, ReadBorrow with the AT-R1 namespace argument, InitTableAccess, CompileWhere, NoteStatementCompiled, WriteWalk, DeclaredWriteBorrow, BorrowOrWait kFutile) is likewise three copies.

**As narrowed by verification.** The arm ladder around WriteWalk (point descent, key set, collect, scan, parked epilogue) and the statement head before it are hand-kept at three sites (UPDATE, DELETE, SELECT ... FOR UPDATE). For UPDATE and DELETE the fold of the point and list arms was considered and declined in the CU work order because the arms reply differently on purpose; what no document decides is DC-S2's third copy and the triplicated head. No behavioural defect follows from it at HEAD.

**Evidence.** Ladders: UPDATE cpp:12440-12575 (`write_at`), FOR UPDATE cpp:12671-12715 (`claim_at`), DELETE cpp:15604-15672 (`mark_at`); e.g. the identical three lines `if (found.kind == TupleKeyLookup::Kind::kAbsent) return PointWrite::kAbsent; if (found.kind != TupleKeyLookup::Kind::kAt) return PointWrite::kScan;` at 12453-12454, 12674-12675, 15612-15613, and `exec::WriteKeySetOf(predicates.value())` + `walk.WriteKeySet(...)` at 12515-12517, 12691-12693, 15643-15645. Heads: cpp:11838-11860, 12597-12625, 15391-15431. The copies already differ in ways a reader must re-derive each time: UPDATE loops for `unique_pending`, the locking read answers a park with `Refused(walk.blocked_verdict)` where the writes return `ParkedOutcome`, and only UPDATE traces.

**Suggested.** Give WriteWalk the driver: `Drive(per_row, page_access, cursor)` returning {kAbsentPoint, kDone, kParkedPoint, kParkedMidWalk} with the Locate/point/key-set/collect/scan order inside it, and `per_row(page_id, page, slot)` the only statement-specific part (UPDATE's apply + clear_unique hook, DELETE's mark, the locking read's claim with kRead). A `WriteTarget` helper for the head (resolve, borrow, access, CompileWhere, declared borrow) returns {ta, predicates, declared}. The next arm (docs/pending/keyed-write-beside-a-subquery.md asks for one) then lands once.

### H79. The lock-wait protocol is hand-kept in three coroutine drivers with ten DropWake sites and differing rules

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `arch-dispatcher-shape` (`arch-dispatcher-shape#4`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:3302`; origin: AwaitWriteBlock/AwaitRelationLock pre-3.4 (AO/AX); the third copy, IndexBuildRun::Drain, by CI-S2 614864c4 (3.4.*)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**
- **Recorded in**: docs/inflight/known-gaps.md, 'lock_deadlocks is not every deadlock' (the drain's missing sys.lock_waits row only)

**Claim (the finder's).** AwaitWriteBlock (cpp:392-644), AwaitRelationLock (cpp:792-938) and IndexBuildRun::Drain (cpp:3302-3376) each spell out the same wait: arm the fault-net deadline, NoteWaitFor and on a cycle record a kDeadlock LockWaitRow and refuse, open a PendingLockWait, park in a WaitScope on a WaitUntil predicate over LockWaitReady(slot) or the deadline, End(granted | fault net), DropWake, ClearWaitFor, then re-run. The wake registration's contract is '`DropWake` on every exit' (lock_table.hpp:694) and is met by ten separate calls (cpp:542, 855, 903, 1333, 3261, 3372, 13297, 13399, 13902 and the reset at 544). The copies have drifted in rules a reader cannot tell are intended: only AwaitRelationLock extends the deadline on the holder's progress count (cpp:831-838), only Drain's predicate reads the cancel flag (cpp:3361-3363), and Drain writes no sys.lock_waits row (the last is recorded in known-gaps as a counting gap, not as a cause).

**As narrowed by verification.** Three coroutine drivers (AwaitWriteBlock, AwaitRelationLock, IndexBuildRun::Drain) hand-keep the same lock-wait skeleton, and DropWake's 'on every exit' contract is met by nine separate calls. Their rule differences (progress extension, cancel in the predicate, no sys.lock_waits row for the drain) are each stated in a comment, a spec or known-gaps, so they are intended; the remainder is duplication only, with no leak found at HEAD.

**Evidence.** cpp:431 `locks_->NoteWaitFor(waiter_id, block.trx_id)` ... `row.outcome = stats::LockWaitOutcome::kDeadlock;` and the same pair at cpp:806-811; cpp:3322 `if (locks.NoteWaitFor(self, waiting.front())) { drained = DeadlockVictim(...` with no row. cpp:540-544 `if (block.slot != nullptr) { locks_->DropWake(block.key, block.slot); out->write_block->slot.reset(); }` against cpp:903 `locks_->DropWake(wait.key, slot);` and cpp:3372.

**Scenario.** None shown at HEAD: every exit I traced drops its wake (AwaitWriteBlock's deadlock and deadline breaks go through RefuseParkedWrite, cpp:13296-13298). The cost is that a fourth wait or a new early co_return must rediscover all of it.

**Suggested.** One `LockWaitPark` value type owning {key, slot, mode, waiter, holder}: its destructor does DropWake + ClearWaitFor, `NoteOrDeadlock()` records the kDeadlock row, `Park(event, deadline, cancel, progress)` is the single WaitScope + WaitUntil + PendingLockWait::End. The three drivers keep only what differs (ceiling waits, the re-run, the drain's holder list), and the cancel/progress rules become arguments that are visibly passed or not.

### H80. Four hand-copied 'byte of the offending token, or the line's end' refusal lambdas in the dispatcher-verb handlers, already drifting

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `dispatcher-1` (`dispatcher-1#4`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:2792`; origin: CJ (HandleSetAsh), CX-S0 (HandleSetJoinOrder), CX-S1a (HandleDropPlan), CY-S1b (HandleStatistics) - three of the four are 3.4.* work
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Each statement that is parsed by hand in the dispatcher re-declares the same local lambda that turns a token view into a byte offset and appends ' (byte N)' to a refusal: HandleSetJoinOrder 2792-2798, HandleDropPlan 2837-2842, HandleSetAsh 2887-2891, HandleStatistics 3757-3763. The copies are not identical: three treat an empty view as the line's end (`at.data() == nullptr || at.empty()`), HandleSetAsh tests only `at.data() == nullptr` and compensates at its call site by passing a default-constructed view. The trailing-semicolon strip is likewise repeated at 2848, 3798 and (as an `if`, one semicolon only) 13103. RefuseWhole (175) is a fifth spelling of the same byte rule for the Unsupported case.

**Evidence.** 2793: `const std::size_t byte = at.data() == nullptr || at.empty()`; 2888: `const std::size_t byte = at.data() == nullptr ? line.size() : static_cast<std::size_t>(at.data() - line.data());`; 3758: `return at.data() == nullptr || at.empty() ? line.size()`

**Suggested.** One file-local helper beside RefuseWhole - e.g. `Status LocatedAt(Status s, std::string_view line, std::string_view at)` with the empty-view rule decided once - and one `StripTrailingSemicolons`; the four handlers and RefuseWhole call them. CLAUDE.md's rule that every refusal carries the offending token's byte is then implemented in one place for the hand-parsed verbs.

### H81. The point / key-set / collect / walk ladder is written three times; the locking read added the third copy after F26 folded the first two halves

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `dispatcher-4` (`dispatcher-4#2`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:12684`; origin: DC-S2 (f6a12f5d, v3.4.14), over CU-S3 (84bf6828)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** F26 moved a row's Qualify, Claim, CollectTargets and ParkedOutcome into WriteWalk, and CU-S3 added WriteKeySet there, but the sequence that drives them - single tuple key via exec::WriteKeyOf, then a list via exec::WriteKeySetOf, then CollectTargets, then VisitRelation with the cursor's targets, each with its own parked check - stayed in the callers. It now stands at three sites: UpdateParsed (:12477-12570), RunLockingRead (:12684-12716) and DeleteInner (:15621-15672). RunLockingRead's own comment says the pass is 'UPDATE's, minus the write ... its arms in its order'. The three differ only in the per-row functor, the page access (kWrite / kRead) and what a park returns; the rule that every arm must be taken in the same order so the row units are asked in key order is kept by hand in three places.

**As narrowed by verification.** The driver sequence (exec::WriteKeyOf point arm -> exec::WriteKeySetOf via WriteWalk::WriteKeySet -> WriteWalk::CollectTargets -> VisitRelation with cursor.targets, each with its parked check) is written at three sites: UpdateParsed (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:12477-12570), RunLockingRead (:12684-12716) and DeleteInner (:15621-15672). F26 folded only the per-row judge (Qualify, Claim, CollectTargets, ParkedOutcome) and CU-S3 added WriteKeySet; DC added the third driver. The three do not differ only in the per-row functor, the page access and the park's return: UPDATE additionally carries the ON CONFLICT arm_target override and its no-walk refusal (:12477-12503), a retry loop in its point arm and around VisitRelation for a moved unique key (unique_pending / clear_unique, :12449-12464, :12533-12558), and a resume cursor (resume_from) with ParkedOutcome; DELETE has the resume cursor and ParkedOutcome but no unique loop; the locking read has no resume cursor, always answers a park with Refused(blocked_verdict), uses an 'answered' flag instead of early returns, and continues to an emit pass. A single driver is possible but needs those as parameters; it is a maintainability item, no behaviour is wrong.

**Evidence.** command_dispatcher.cpp:12684 `if (std::optional<std::uint64_t> key = exec::WriteKeyOf(predicates.value()); key.has_value()) {` ; :12691 `if (const std::optional<exec::KeySet> set = exec::WriteKeySetOf(predicates.value());` ; :15643-15657 the same two tests followed by `auto any = walk.CollectTargets(); ... walk_cursor.targets = walk.targets;` and :12515-12531 the same again.

**Suggested.** Give WriteWalk one driver, e.g. `StatusOr<Arms> Run(PageAccess, WalkCursor&, RowFn row_at_slot, AfterStop on_stop)`, that owns LocateByTupleKey, the kAbsent/kScan/kParked mapping, WriteKeySet, CollectTargets and the VisitRelation loop; UPDATE passes `apply` plus its unique-key hook (the one real difference, :12460-12464 and :12553-12558), DELETE passes `mark`, the locking read passes `claim` with kRead. Each caller keeps only its reply. No behaviour change intended; the walk_write, on-conflict, key-set (CU) and locking-read (DC) cells cover it.

### H82. The plan statements' example prologue is written twice, and the blank-the-prefix and '?' scan a third time in HandleWaystone

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `dispatcher-4` (`dispatcher-4#3`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:14860`; origin: CX-S7 (ADVISE PLAN, v3.4.13) copying CX-S1b's HandleCreatePlan
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** HandleAdvisePlan (:14860-14902, :14935-14940) repeats HandleCreatePlan (:14567-14625, :14642-14646) step for step: PlanExampleText, Parser::Parse, the 'takes one SELECT' test on the variant and the fingerprint, the bindings vector from stmt.from and stmt.joins, the kMaxPlannedRelations refusal with the same text, StatementContextFor, a ReadBorrow and the same `compile` lambda over ResolveNames/exec::Compile, the written-order compile, the CapturedKey filled from written.steps[w].rel_oid, and the PlanOptions fields (join_build_max_rows, sort_max_rows, statistics, fetch_id, statement). PlanExampleText (:14518-14536) is itself a second copy of HandleWaystone's padding and kParam lexer scan (:14230-14266), as its comment says (`HandleWaystone`'s shape). A change to what a plan's shape key is - DI just changed what a shape binds - has to be made in each copy.

**Evidence.** command_dispatcher.cpp:14598-14600 `"a plan orders at most " + std::to_string(exec::kMaxPlannedRelations) + " relations" + ByteSuffix(create.statement_at)` and :14881-14883 the same refusal; :14621-14625 and :14898-14902 both fill `key.fetch_id`, `key.n` and `key.oids[w] = written.value().steps[w].rel_oid`.

**Suggested.** One helper, e.g. `StatusOr<PlanExample> ReadPlanExample(line, at, size, head, session)`, returning the padded text, the parsed SelectStmt, the fingerprint, the bindings, the written chain and its CapturedKey, plus a `PlanOptions BaseOptions(fingerprint, padded)`; both handlers start from it. HandleWaystone can take the padded text and first '?' position from the same lexer pass. Covered by the plan DDL and advice cells and the Waystone opt-in cells.

### H83. CX spells 'run a non-written order through the ladder, fall back to the written chain unless AddedSortCannotRefuse' at three sites, and the planned-block stamp and the shape key twice each

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `exec-compiler` (`exec-compiler#3`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:2713`; origin: CX-S0 (63ce2c93, dbe3ddb5), CX-S1b (4f12b44d), CX-S7 (435336c9)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** CompileBlock carries three copies of one decline rule: the administrator's variant (2711-2728), the captured order (run_captured, 2810-2821) and ADVISE's plan in force (3036-3044) each compile a permutation with compile_order, test AddedSortCannotRefuse(chain, written_sorted, plan->sort_max_rows) and otherwise fall back to the written chain or its cost. The stamp a planned block's chain carries is written twice (2734-2739 in the admin arm; the `stamp` lambda at 2769-2776), and the block's identity is built twice from the same fetch id and oids (CapturedKey at 2657-2665, stats::PlanKey in statistics_key at 2670-2679). The copies agree at HEAD (the admin arm's missing `refs.size() <= kMaxPlannedRelations` test is implied by shape.has_value()), but CC-Q10's rule is a correctness rule for a refusal ('a captured or administrator's order never refuses a statement its written order answers') and a fourth source of an order would have to remember it by hand - the memo hit at 2904 already takes a different route to it.

**As narrowed by verification.** CompileBlock repeats the pattern 'compile_order, then AddedSortCannotRefuse, else fall back to the written chain' at three sites (2713-2723, 2814-2820, 3040-3044), with a fourth application of the same predicate inside EstimateChain (join_order.cpp:313-316) and a memo hit (2904) that relies on its key instead. The planned-block stamp is written twice with equivalent effect (2734-2739 and 2769-2776). The predicate itself is already one function and the fallbacks differ by design. CapturedKey and stats::PlanKey are both filled from the fetch id and the oids in written order, but they are distinct types with ceilings of 16 and 4 relations, so that part is two keys rather than one key built twice.

**Evidence.** 2715: `} else if (!AddedSortCannotRefuse(ordered.value(), written_sorted, plan->sort_max_rows)) {` 2816: `AddedSortCannotRefuse(chain.value(), written_sorted, plan->sort_max_rows)) {` 3042: `AddedSortCannotRefuse(in_force.value(), written_sorted, plan->sort_max_rows)) {`

**Suggested.** One local helper, e.g. `std::optional<StepChain> admitted(perm, pins, std::string* why)`, that compiles the order and applies CC-Q10's rule, used by the three arms (and by the memo hit, which then no longer rests on arg_hash covering LIMIT); hoist `stamp` above section 1a' and use it in the admin arm; derive stats::PlanKey from the CapturedKey (or one from the other) in one place.

### H84. The query layer carries a second aggregate fold and private copies of set_expr helpers, one of which has already diverged

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `exec-expr-query` (`exec-expr-query#4`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/query_layer.cpp:1374`; origin: pre-3.4 (BU-S5 and BU-S7)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** `Runner::GroupRows` re-implements what `exec::Aggregator` already does, as a parallel body. aggregate.hpp says of its value encoder: "One encoder for both, deliberately ... two encoders would be two answers to what 'the same value' means"; the layer adds a second one. The file also re-declares helpers that set_expr.cpp owns. The copy of `Concrete` does not zero `len` for non-char, non-decimal types as set_expr's does, which is one half of the mismatch in finding 1. No answer was found to differ between the two folds today.

**As narrowed by verification.** The query layer (src/exec/query_layer.cpp, pre-3.4, BU-S5/S7) carries its own aggregate fold (`Runner::GroupRows`, 1374-1516) and its own value-identity encoder (`AppendKey`, 75-91) beside `exec::Aggregator` (aggregate.cpp:203-485, `EncodeValue` at 116). It also carries file-local copies of set_expr.cpp helpers that set_expr.hpp does not export (`At`, `IsText`, `Concrete`, `NumberOf`, plus `IEq`/`FoldName` against `IEquals`/`Lower`), and its relation-walk cap text is repeated at command_dispatcher.cpp:11124-11129. The two folds are kept in agreement by rule (BU work order S5), and no differing answer was found. The `Concrete` copy's missing `len` zeroing is inert at its four call sites (varchar/0, decimal, int64/0) and is not shown to contribute to any type mismatch. `AggregateValue` instead of `ValueOf` is a recorded BU-S5 review decision. The missing uint64 arm in `NumberOf` is unreachable because SUM over uint64 is refused at compile (969-973). This is a maintenance-drift risk only, with no wrong answer today.

**Evidence.** Fold and group handling: query_layer.cpp:1374-1516, against aggregate.cpp:203-335 (`FoldInto`), 337-379 (`Accumulate`) and 381-485 (`Finish`). AVG half-to-even divide: query_layer.cpp:1500-1506, against aggregate.cpp:451-465. SUM overflow discipline: query_layer.cpp:1445-1452, against aggregate.cpp:281-307. Value-identity encoder: query_layer.cpp:75-91 (`AppendKey` over `OrderKeyOf`), against aggregate.cpp:116-186 (`EncodeValue`). Cap refusals: query_layer.cpp:1402-1405 and 1426-1430, against aggregate.cpp:245-250 and 363-368; the relation-walk cap text at query_layer.cpp:522-529 is repeated at command_dispatcher.cpp:11124-11129. Helpers: `Concrete` (query_layer.cpp:56 vs set_expr.cpp:46), `IsText` (64 vs 43), `At` (54 vs 77), `NumberOf` (104 vs set_expr.cpp:1310, the layer's without the uint64 arm), `AggregateValue` (118) vs `ValueOf` (set_expr.cpp:1326), and `IEq`/`FoldName` (182, 395) vs `Lower` (set_expr.cpp:214) and `IEquals` (functions.cpp:70).

**Suggested.** Export `Concrete`, `SameType`, `IsText`, `NumberOf` and `ValueOf` from set_expr.hpp and delete the layer's copies. Give `Aggregator` an entry that folds a row of values (it currently takes a `ChainFrame`), so `GroupRows` drives the one fold with the one `EncodeValue`; or at least share the AVG divide and the SUM accumulate as free functions in aggregate.hpp.

### H85. The bounded per-core keyed part is written four times, and the splitmix64 finalizer three times

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `stats-stores` (`stats-stores#2`)
- **Where**: `include/kds/stats/oid_parts.hpp:41`; origin: DF-S3 83aad532 added OidParts ('written once so a store cannot bound, drop or merge differently from another') without moving StatementStats onto it; DA added a second open-addressed table beside CC's; DI-S1 1ffbf607 added the third hash fold
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** One pattern (a per-core part under a kStatsRing latch, find-or-admit under a bound, drop and count past it, a `Dropped()` loop over 64 latches, a reset, a merge-by-key snapshot) has four separate implementations: (1) `OidParts::With/Snapshot/Dropped/Reset` (oid_parts.hpp:41-88), used by RelationStats, LeafStats and IndexStats; (2) `StatementStats::Record/Snapshot/Dropped/ResetForTest` (statement_stats.cpp:80-111, 150-220), the same unordered_map part with bound, bad_alloc drop and count, differing only in key type; (3) `AccessStats::Record/Snapshot/Dropped` (access_stats.cpp:47-80, 122-164), a half-full linear-probed flat table with drop-and-count; (4) `PlanStats::Fold` (plan_stats.cpp:332-359), a second half-full linear-probed flat table with drop-and-count. The two flat tables already differ: AccessStats allocates lazily and deletes by backward shift, PlanStats allocates eagerly and is emptied by the steward, and one probes with `%` while the other wraps by compare. The splitmix64 finalizer is also written three times with the same constants: `HomeOf` (access_stats.cpp:17-29), `Mix` (plan_stats.cpp:21-26) and the `fold` lambda in `StatementStats::KeyHash` (statement_stats.cpp:55-58). DL has to change every one of these record paths (rules.md section 3a (b)); with four copies it has four places to get the drop-and-count rule right, and the cache-line layout issue in the previous finding has to be fixed in each.

**As narrowed by verification.** The bounded per-core keyed part is implemented four times (OidParts, StatementStats, AccessStats' flat table, PlanStats::Fold's flat table), and the two flat tables already differ in allocation, wrap and drop accounting. The splitmix64 finalizer is written three times in stats (access_stats.cpp HomeOf, plan_stats.cpp Mix, optimizer_signals.cpp SignalKeyHash) and three more times elsewhere (lock_table.cpp, memory_page_device.cpp, tcp_server.cpp). StatementStats::KeyHash shares only the constants; it is a different mix, not a fourth copy of the finalizer.

**Evidence.** oid_parts.hpp:13-15 `the shape RelationStats and IndexStats share (BO-Q5 (b), BO-Q7 (a)), written once so a store cannot bound, drop or merge differently from another`; statement_stats.cpp:85-87 `if (part.shapes.size() >= kShapesPerCore) { ++part.dropped; return; }` against oid_parts.hpp:46-48 `if (part.counts.size() >= kBound) { ++part.dropped; return; }`; access_stats.cpp:66 `if (part.live >= slots_ / 2) {` against plan_stats.cpp:344 `if (part.used >= kKeysPerPart / 2) break;`

**Suggested.** Generalise OidParts over its key and hash (a `KeyedParts<K, V, Hash, Bound>`) and put StatementStats on it. Extract one fixed-capacity half-full open-addressed table for AccessStats and PlanStats::Fold, and one `Mix64` helper. Do this as the first step of DL so the latch-free record path is written once.

### H86. KwpLoadServer carries a second copy of TcpServer's connection transport, and the copy has drifted (no CLOEXEC, unbounded accept drain, EINTR closes a load)

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: medium, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dup` (`sweep-dup#1`)
- **Where**: `src/server/kwp_load_server.cpp:211`; origin: pre-3.4 (the v0 load endpoint; 3.4 touched it only for F3/F25 and DF-S2's rollback cause)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** About 130 lines of src/server/kwp_load_server.cpp re-implement what src/server/tcp_server.cpp already has: SetNonBlocking (kwp 28-37 = tcp 25-34, byte-identical), Listen's socket/bind/listen (kwp 118-144 vs tcp 84-135), the accept loop (kwp 211-235 vs tcp 321-333), OnClientEvent (kwp 237-245 vs tcp 485-496), the read head of OnClientReadable (kwp 247-262 vs tcp 498-512), FlushOutbox (kwp 604-617 vs tcp 809-834), SyncWriteInterest (kwp 619-632 vs tcp 836-848) and the best-effort-send-then-rollback teardown of CloseClient (kwp 634 on vs tcp 1080 on). The two copies no longer agree: TcpServer accepts with accept4(SOCK_NONBLOCK | SOCK_CLOEXEC) and at most kMaxAcceptsPerEvent a wake, treats EINTR on read as 'try again' and retries it on send; KwpLoadServer accepts with plain accept() in an unbounded for(;;), and both its read and its send close the connection on any errno but EAGAIN.

**Evidence.** kwp_load_server.cpp:213 `int client_fd = ::accept(listen_fd_, nullptr, nullptr);` against tcp_server.cpp:325 `::accept4(listen_fd_, nullptr, nullptr, SOCK_NONBLOCK | SOCK_CLOEXEC)`; kwp_load_server.cpp:257 `if (errno == EAGAIN || errno == EWOULDBLOCK) return;` then CloseClient, against tcp_server.cpp:509 `errno == EAGAIN || errno == EWOULDBLOCK || errno == EINTR`; wal_archive.cpp:204 spawns `/bin/sh` with a null file-actions argument, so a descriptor without CLOEXEC is inherited.

**Scenario.** Consequences of the drift, both narrow: (1) a load connection open while an archive_command child is spawned (wal_archive.cpp:204) is inherited by the child, so when the server closes that connection the peer sees no FIN until the command exits; (2) a read or send that returned EINTR would take CloseClient, which mid-load dispatches ROLLBACK (kwp_load_server.cpp:634 on) - on a nonblocking socket that errno is close to unreachable, so this is drift, not an observed defect. TcpServer's comment at 321-324 says the bounded accept is what keeps a burst from starving timers; the load port has no such bound.

**Suggested.** Keep TcpServer's copy. Lift the socket plumbing into one unit both servers use (the listener open, the accepted-socket setup beside the already shared ConfigureAcceptedSocket, and an outbox with Flush/SyncWriteInterest over a byte buffer), leaving KwpLoadServer with its frame decoder and the three LOAD handlers. Short of that, bring the copy level: accept4 with SOCK_CLOEXEC and the per-event bound, and EINTR handled as TcpServer does.

### H87. The pread/pwrite/fsync retry loop and the errno-to-Status mapping are written out per file: eleven loops and four mappers, which disagree on ENOSPC and on EINTR

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dup` (`sweep-dup#2`)
- **Where**: `src/wal/file_log_device.cpp:521`; origin: pre-3.4 (devices), widened by the backup and restore work; G30/G41/G42 touched the files without folding
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The 'loop until every byte is read or written, continue on EINTR, map errno' block (10 to 20 lines) is hand-written at: src/storage/file_page_device.cpp:102-121 (pread), 138-149 (pwrite), 165-175 (preadv2); src/wal/file_log_device.cpp:113-121 (header pwrite), 141-152 (prewrite), 519-533 (WriteAt), 549-568 (ReadAt); src/server/backup_manifest.cpp:102-114 (PwriteAll, the one site that is already a helper), 135-145 and 168-178 (read loops); src/server/restore.cpp:238-245 (ReadPage). The EINTR-retried sync loop is written five more times (file_log_device.cpp:122, 156, 622, 718; file_page_device.cpp:284). ErrnoStatus is byte-identical in file_page_device.cpp:19-30 and file_log_device.cpp:27-38, while backup_manifest.cpp:26 (`Errno`) and stop_signal.cpp:15 are different mappers. About 110 duplicated lines, and the copies answer differently.

**Evidence.** file_page_device.cpp:19-30 and file_log_device.cpp:27-38 both map `case ENOSPC: case EDQUOT: return Status::OutOfSpace(detail);`; backup_manifest.cpp:26-27 is `return Status::IoError(what + ": " + std::strerror(err));` for every errno; restore.cpp:243 `if (n <= 0) return Status::IoError("read page " + std::to_string(id));` drops the errno text, and backup_manifest.cpp:129, 186, 207 and restore.cpp:103 call fsync once with no EINTR retry where the devices loop.

**Scenario.** A BACKUP COPY that fills its target is refused IO_ERROR where the same ENOSPC on the data file or the log is OUT_OF_SPACE (two wire tokens for one condition); a restore's short read reports no cause. Neither changes a result.

**Suggested.** One copy in include/kds/base/ beside file_descriptor.hpp: `Status PreadAll(int fd, std::span<std::byte>, std::uint64_t offset, what)`, `PwriteAll(...)`, `SyncRetrying(int fd, bool data_only, what)` and the one `ErrnoStatus`. backup_manifest.cpp's PwriteAll is the shape to keep, with the devices' ErrnoStatus as its mapper; the devices keep only their range checks, the write fence and their EOF-is-Corruption rule (a callback or a distinct return for n == 0).

### H88. The staged leaf copy-out (fetch leaf, read every slot, skip retired, classify by CheckVisibility, stage payload and key, take next_page_id) is written four times under the one shared LeafCursor

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dup` (`sweep-dup#3`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/assertion_build.cpp:194`; origin: pre-3.4; A1 stage 2b (cca7415b, in 3.4.*) moved the outer walk into exec::LeafCursor and left each per-leaf body in place
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** LeafCursor (include/kds/exec/leaf_cursor.hpp) owns the chain walk and its cycle guard, but its `per_leaf` body's first phase is a separate copy in each caller: src/exec/assertion_build.cpp:192-232, src/exec/transition.cpp:309-337, src/exec/index_ddl.cpp:473-512, and the Cabin controller's tick at src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:190-231 (the same block over a ScanFetcher ring). Each declares its own StagedRow (assertion_build.cpp:33, index_ddl.cpp:65, cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:172; transition stages bare payloads), each repeats the retired-slot rule and the kBusy / kAbsent / kLive switch, and each re-states the reason for the two phases (no var-heap fetch under the leaf's span). index_ddl.cpp:531-549 (HighWaterKey) is a fifth, simpler slot loop. About 35 lines a site, 105 beyond one copy. The exact-match pass flags assertion_build.cpp:199-208 and cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:199-208 as identical line for line.

**As narrowed by verification.** The staged copy-out (fetch leaf, ReadTuple per slot, skip a NotFound retired slot, KeystoneIdOfPayload, copy the payload, take next_page_id, all under the page span before any var-heap fetch) is written four times: src/exec/assertion_build.cpp:193-232, src/exec/transition.cpp:309-337, src/exec/index_ddl.cpp:474-512 and src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:190-231. Only the first three are LeafCursor per-leaf bodies; the Cabin controller's rebuild is its own PageChainGuard while-loop over a ScanFetcher ring (cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:179-196) and does not use LeafCursor. The CheckVisibility kBusy/kAbsent/kLive switch is in three of the four (assertion build, transition, Cabin controller), not in each: the index build stages every version with trx_id, undo_ptr, deleted and head_in_flight and has no switch, and it adds an owner-oid and high-water test that the others lack. The three StagedRow structs differ (index_ddl's carries the version fields), and transition stages bare payloads. HighWaterKey (index_ddl.cpp:531-549) is a fifth, simpler slot loop as stated.

**Evidence.** assertion_build.cpp:202-204 `auto tuple = page.ReadTuple(i); if (tuple.status().code() == StatusCode::kNotFound) continue;  // retired slot` followed by `switch (txn::CheckVisibility(check_view, tuple.value().trx_id, tuple.value().deleted))`; the same three statements at transition.cpp:316-320 and cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:202-207, and the same ReadTuple/KeystoneIdOfPayload/payload.assign staging at index_ddl.cpp:493-509.

**Suggested.** One `StageLeaf` beside LeafCursor in include/kds/exec/leaf_cursor.hpp: it takes the page bytes (so the ring's fetch and the store's both fit), returns `{std::vector<StagedRow>, PageId next}` with StagedRow carrying tuple_key, slot, trx_id, undo_ptr, deleted and the payload, and takes a per-tuple verdict callback (the three settled-state builds pass the CheckVisibility switch with their own kBusy answer; the index build passes 'take every version'). The four callers keep only their phase 2.

### H89. Fixed-width scalar load/store helpers are defined in ten places although storage/page_bytes.hpp exists to be the one pair

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dup` (`sweep-dup#4`)
- **Where**: `include/kds/storage/page_bytes.hpp:16`; origin: pre-3.4; CJ WE05-WE06 (db2696fe, in 3.4.*) added the ash_record.hpp pair
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The same two primitives - read a T at an offset, write a T at an offset - are private to each codec file. Host-order memcpy pair: src/wal/payload.cpp:15-25 and src/wal/record.cpp:15-25 (identical text, same namespace kds::wal), src/stats/waystone_page.cpp:17-27, include/kds/storage/page_bytes.hpp:15-25 (the shared one, used by page_header.cpp and anchor_page.cpp). Explicit little-endian shift loop: src/storage/cabin_bound_page.cpp:35-71 (Store64/Load64/Store32/Load32/Store16/Load16), src/storage/tagged_cell.cpp:10-22 (PutLE/GetLE), src/exec/row_codec.cpp:57-74 (StoreLe64, PutLE, GetLE), src/wire/row_codec.cpp:28-40 (PutLE/LoadLE), include/kds/stats/ash_record.hpp:77-89 (PutLe/GetLe), src/storage/keystone.cpp:9-15 (LoadLe64), src/exec/bound_cabin.cpp:19-21 (AppendU64). About 100 lines. page_bytes.hpp's own header already records one of them as outstanding.

**Evidence.** page_bytes.hpp:10-11: "Used by page_header.cpp and anchor_page.cpp; `stats/waystone_page.cpp` still keeps a private pair." wal/payload.cpp:15-19 and wal/record.cpp:15-19 are both `template <typename T> T Load(std::span<const std::byte> bytes, std::size_t offset) { T value{}; std::memcpy(&value, bytes.data() + offset, sizeof(T)); return value; }`. cabin_bound_page.cpp:33-34 says its format "has to read the same on every architecture" while the header of the same page is written by the memcpy pair.

**Suggested.** Two pairs in one header under include/kds/base/ (or widen page_bytes.hpp to a dynamic-extent span): `LoadField/StoreField` (host order, what the WAL, page header, anchor and Waystone formats use) and `LoadLe/StoreLe(width)` (what the cell, row, wire, ASH and Bound Cabin formats use). Delete the private copies. Whether a persisted format may be host-order at all is a separate question the specs own; folding the helpers makes the two conventions visible in one place instead of ten.

### H90. 'Verify the hinted location, else descend by tuple key, NotFound is a skip' is written at six sites, five of them outside tuple_verify

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `sweep-dup` (`sweep-dup#5`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1416`; origin: pre-3.4 (BD-R3 E3, AT-S7); the Cabin-serve phase 2 copy came with the key-set work
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** exec::VerifyTupleAt is the one verifier, but the protocol around it - call it, on a miss call btree::BtreeLookup for the tuple key, treat kNotFound as a dangling key to skip, take the leaf the lookup holds, and (for a Cabin entry) stamp the heal with the epoch read off that held leaf - is hand-written at each consumer: src/exec/step_vm.cpp:759-788 (trail replay and point lookup), 1416-1439 (statement-local build probe), 2152-2199 (Cabin serve phase 1, with the heal), 2237-2260 (Cabin serve phase 2), src/exec/fk_check.cpp:175-200 (reverse check over a Cabin set, with the heal), src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:412-432 (set rebuild, with the heal). About 15 to 25 lines a site, roughly 90 beyond one copy. The correctness-bearing parts are the ones repeated: the kNotFound skip and the 'epoch from the held leaf, never 0 and never a re-fetch' rule are each re-argued in comments at three sites.

**Evidence.** step_vm.cpp:1427-1432 `auto found = btree::BtreeLookup(store_, access.desc_page_id, entry.tuple_key); if (!found.ok()) { if (found.status().code() == StatusCode::kNotFound) continue; co_return found.status(); }`; the same at step_vm.cpp:2251-2255, fk_check.cpp:186-196 and cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:419-425; the heal's epoch rule at step_vm.cpp:2192-2194 and again at cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:428-432 (`entry.page_epoch = CurrentPageEpoch(heap::PageView(found.value().leaf.bytes()));`).

**Suggested.** One function in include/kds/exec/tuple_verify.hpp beside VerifyTupleAt: `StatusOr<std::optional<ResolvedTuple>> ResolveTuple(store, desc_page_id, owner_oid, tuple_key, hint)` returning the held PageRef, page id, slot, the epoch read under the hold and whether the hint was good (nullopt for a dangling key). The six sites keep their own counters and their own Heal/NoteHint calls on the returned flag; the skip rule and the epoch rule are then stated once. Confidence is medium because the sites differ in what they count and in coroutine versus plain return, so the fold is a helper plus six small call sites, not a mechanical deletion.

### H91. sys_views.cpp carries duplicate helpers from the CX/CY merge: StatisticsLabel repeats RelationLabels::Qualified, plus three copies of the histogram bound and three of the relation-list join

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sys-views` (`sys-views#4`)
- **Where**: `src/server/sys_views.cpp:1098`; origin: CY-S1b (61f5dddd) beside CX-S1a (4f62dd2a), both kept by merge e942ff74; DF-S1/DF-S2 and DI-S1 for the rest
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** (1) StatisticsLabel (sys_views.cpp:1098-1103) produces the same string as RelationLabels::Qualified (181-187): `ns.name`, or `oid <n>` for a relation the catalog no longer lists. The merge commit says each conflict was resolved by keeping both sides. (2) The log2 bucket lower bound `k == 0 ? 0 : std::uint64_t{1} << k` is written out at 1475 (statement_histogram), 1636 (wait_histogram) and 1661 (txn_histogram), each with its own copy of the comment describing the bucket rule. (3) The loop that joins a key's oids with `, ` through labels.Qualified is written at 218-226 (BindingRelations), 1039-1046 (PlansView) and 1077-1084 (PlanAdviceView). (4) The case-insensitive IEquals at sys_views.cpp:51 is one of eight identical free functions: lexer.cpp:36, parser.cpp:67, step_compiler.cpp:30, functions.cpp:72, declaration_relation.cpp:36, statement_stats.cpp:22, command_dispatcher.cpp:135 and here, with IEqualsAscii at priority.cpp:16 as a ninth.

**As narrowed by verification.** Items (1)-(3) hold as written. (1) StatisticsLabel (src/server/sys_views.cpp:1098-1103) returns exactly what RelationLabels::Qualified (181-187) returns - 'oid <n>' when the relation is not in the label map, else the namespace spelling plus '.' (omitted when the namespace has no spelling) plus the name - and has one caller (:1144). (2) The bucket lower bound `k == 0 ? 0 : std::uint64_t{1} << k` is written three times (:1475, :1636, :1661 with b.bucket). (3) The ', '-join over labels.Qualified is written at 218-226, 1039-1046 and 1077-1084 (the latter two build two strings in one loop, relations and an order permutation, so a shared helper needs an index mapping). (4) is overstated: there are eight file-local IEquals definitions with the same behaviour for ASCII but three different bodies, not eight identical ones - std::tolower via std::equal at parser.cpp:67, step_compiler.cpp:30, functions.cpp:72, sys_views.cpp:51, command_dispatcher.cpp:135; std::toupper at statement_stats.cpp:22; a FoldAscii loop at lexer.cpp:36 and declaration_relation.cpp:36 (locale-independent, unlike the tolower/toupper copies). priority.cpp:16's IEqualsAscii folds only its first argument against an already-lowercase second, so it is a different function, not a ninth copy.

**Evidence.** sys_views.cpp:1100 `if (name.type == parser::ValueType::kNull) return "oid " + std::to_string(oid);` against :183 `if (it == relations_.end()) return "oid " + std::to_string(rel_oid);`. Both then return the namespace spelling, a dot and the name. grep for `StatisticsLabel` finds one caller, :1144.

**Suggested.** Delete StatisticsLabel and call labels.Qualified(oid) at :1144. Add one `BucketLowerUs(k)` beside sched::WaitBucketOf and one `JoinQualified(labels, oids, n, order)` helper used by the three list builders. Move IEquals to one header under base/.

## 6.7 Dead code and stale statements (18)

### H92. kds.conf.sample disagrees with the parser and the specs in three places: 'every key is optional', no archive keys, and an isolation rationale that says writers do not wait

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `arch-config-errors` (`arch-config-errors#4`)
- **Where**: `kds.conf.sample:11`; origin: header pre-3.4 (buffer_pool_frames became required at BE); archive keys BR-S4 (pre-3.4); the isolation comment went stale at CP (v3.4.8, the lock family's waiting writers)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** (a) Line 11 says 'Every key below is optional - an omitted key keeps its default', but line 85 of the same file and Config::ApplyFile (expeditor.cpp:302-304) make buffer_pool_frames required and refuse a file without it. (b) `archive_dir` and `archive_command` are in KnownConfigKeys (expeditor.cpp:70), read at 292-298 and documented in manual/server/backup-restore.md:95-96, but the sample - 'the commented template' per manual/server/server.md:137 - mentions neither (grep -c archive = 0); they are the only live known keys absent from it (scripted comm of sample keys vs KnownConfigKeys). (c) Line 721 justifies the READ COMMITTED default by 'first-updater-wins with no waiting'; since CP a writer meeting an undecided holder waits for its decide (txn.md section 5, CLAUDE.md's Transactions row), so the stated reason describes an engine that no longer exists. Every default value in the sample was checked against expeditor.hpp and agrees; the manual's key table covers every known key.

**As narrowed by verification.** kds.conf.sample has three documentation drifts: its header (line 11) calls every key optional while buffer_pool_frames is required (line 85, expeditor.cpp:299-304); it omits the two live WAL-archive keys archive_dir and archive_command, the only live known keys absent from it; and line 721's phrase 'with no waiting' contradicts txn.md section 5's waiting writers (AO-S3, pre-3.4), though the READ COMMITTED rationale itself still matches txn.md:122. Documentation only; none is 3.4 work except that 3.4 edits to the sample left them in place.

**Evidence.** kds.conf.sample:11: `# Every key below is optional — an omitted key keeps its default. An` / :85: `# pool every core shares (docs/spec/eviction.md section 6). **Required** -` / :721: `# than convention: under first-updater-wins with no waiting, REPEATABLE READ`

**Suggested.** Reword the header ('every key but buffer_pool_frames'), add a commented WAL-archive section naming archive_dir / archive_command and their mutual exclusion, and restate the isolation rationale from txn.md section 1 as it reads today.

### H93. known-gaps.md records a `TRACE ON` gap on `CommandDispatcher::tracing_`, a member and a command the tree no longer has

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `arch-dispatcher-shape` (`arch-dispatcher-shape#5`)

- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

By the file's own rule an entry older than its subsystem's last change is a statement about an engine that no longer exists.

**Evidence.** `grep -rn -i '"TRACE"\|TRACE ON\|tracing' src include` returns only base/log.cpp's level name and two comments; `git log -S"tracing_" -- include/kds/server/command_dispatcher.hpp` names a8d1425e 'BO-S2: every SHOW target is a SELECT over one sys view; the handlers and the trace sink deleted' as the last commit to touch it. known-gaps.md:1205 '- **`TRACE ON` is per core, not per session** (`CommandDispatcher::tracing_`).'

**Suggested.** Delete the entry (a line TRACE now reaches the dispatcher as an unknown command, refused admin-classed). hpp:2660-2662's 'Enabling trace on a busy server costs a write() per tuple' is about the log level and can stay.

### H94. Four empty source directories from the initial commit: src/access, src/query-optimizer, src/physical-optimizer, src/transaction

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `arch-layering` (`arch-layering#5`)

- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

Two of the names now contradict what is built: the transaction code is src/txn, and the physical optimizer (the mover) was deleted and its spec split by owner (CLAUDE.md's page-epoch row: 'No mover exists'; raft-marks-2026-10-22 section 1), so src/physical-optimizer names a subsystem the engine states it does not have.

**Evidence.** git ls-files for the four paths returns exactly src/access/.gitkeep, src/physical-optimizer/.gitkeep, src/query-optimizer/.gitkeep, src/transaction/.gitkeep. grep over *.md, *.cmake, *.sh, *.py outside instructions/ and bench/ for 'src/access', 'query-optimizer', 'physical-optimizer', 'src/transaction' finds no reference to the directories (the instructions/ hits are to the former docs/spec/physical-optimizer.md, not the directory). git log for the four files shows the single commit 9cde1b45.

### H95. Three comments still call page 18 an unused root id after CX-S1a gave it to sys.plans

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `catalog-delta` (`catalog-delta#2`)
- **Where**: `src/catalog/catalog.cpp:38`; origin: CX-S1a (4f62dd2a), superblock 37
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The static_assert message that tells a developer where to add a new fixed catalog root, and two comments in superblock.hpp, were updated for sys.statistics (page 17) but not for sys.plans (page 18). They state the unused range as 18..127 and the fixed roots as ids 4-17, while well_known.hpp:404-407 says the highest root is 18 and 19..127 are unused.

**Evidence.** catalog.cpp:38 `"add a new root past the highest, in the unused ids 18..127");`; superblock.hpp:43-44 `ids 18..127 are unused, left for a future bootstrap root`; superblock.hpp:48 `the catalog's fixed roots (kds::catalog::kCatalogPage*, ids 4-17)`; against well_known.hpp:408 `inline constexpr PageId kCatalogPagePlans = 18;`.

**Scenario.** A developer following the assert's message picks 18 for the next root. The same static_assert's distinctness check catches it at compile time, so the cost is a misleading instruction, not a runtime defect.

**Suggested.** Change the three texts to 19..127 and ids 4-18, or derive the message from the highest entry of kCatalogRoots so it cannot go stale at the next format event.

### H96. A gather's per-slice re-resolve stops nothing: a relation dropped mid-gather is walked to its last leaf before the tail refuses, and the class comment says otherwise

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `dispatcher-1` (`dispatcher-1#3`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:3597`; origin: CY-S1b (61f5dddd)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** GatherRun::Step calls catalog_.InitTableAccess(oid) on every slice, but the result is used only on the first one (`if (!gather_.started())`), and the function's own comment says the pinned memo keeps resolving a relation dropped since the head. So the per-slice lookup is cost with no effect after slice 1, and the class comment at 3560-3561 ('Each slice re-resolves the relation first; a relation dropped since the head stops the gather with nothing published') misstates the code: the drop is noticed only by ManagedStatistics::Store in Tail, after every remaining leaf has been walked at the gather's pace - while the run's epoch hold keeps that dropped relation's own pages from being reclaimed (finding 1).

**As narrowed by verification.** GatherRun::Step's per-slice InitTableAccess (command_dispatcher.cpp:3597) uses the returned access only on the first slice, but its failure branch is live: it stops a gather of a dropped relation on any slice after this core's catalog memo has been invalidated (the drop ran on this core, or any later statement head on this core revalidated past the drop). Only on a core where nothing revalidated since the drop does the lookup keep hitting the old memo, and then the gather walks to its last leaf and is refused by ManagedStatistics::Store in Tail (managed_statistics.cpp:56-57). The inaccurate comment is the one inside Step (3595-3596, 'The pinned memo still resolves a relation dropped since the head'), which overstates what a pin does; the class comment (3560-3561) is true in the invalidated case and false in the idle-core case.

**Evidence.** 3595-3597: `// The pinned memo still resolves a relation dropped since the head;` / `// what refuses that gather's result is the store (`Forget`).` / `auto access = d_.catalog_.InitTableAccess(gather_.oid());`; 3602-3603: `if (!gather_.started()) { if (Status s = gather_.Start(d_.page_store_, *access.value()); ...`; 3605: `return gather_.Step(d_.page_store_, leaves);`

**Scenario.** GATHER STATISTICS ON big starts; DROP TABLE big commits a second later. The gather keeps reading big's leaves through the buffer pool for the rest of the walk (minutes for a large relation), then answers NotFound 'was dropped while its statistics were made'. No wrong result; wasted page reads and a delayed reclaim.

**Suggested.** Resolve the access once (first slice) and, per slice, ask the managed store whether the oid was forgotten (the `forgotten_` set Store already consults) and stop there; or drop the per-slice InitTableAccess and correct the class comment to say the tail is what refuses.

### H97. HandleDropColumn's comment says RENAME COLUMN takes no relation lock; since AL4a it takes the relation X

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `dispatcher-2` (`dispatcher-2#4`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:4817`; origin: pre-3.4: comment from 46d723ba (2026-10-15), made stale by 7f0fa91d (2026-10-21, 'A rename takes the relation X (alter.md AL4a)')
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The comment giving the reason DROP COLUMN writes by position misstates the lock protocol. HandleAlter takes the relation X for both renames (4408-4435, TakeDdlBuildLock), HandleTransition's comment at 5315 says so ('a rename takes the `X` too, AL4a'), and Catalog::DropColumn's own comment (catalog.cpp:2056) was updated to say it. The dispatcher's copy still states the pre-AL4a protocol, in a file whose comments are the stated record of who holds what.

**Evidence.** 4817-4819: `// By the position the RESTRICT was asked of: a RENAME COLUMN takes` / `// no relation lock, so a second resolution by name could reach` / `// another column (the door refuses a renamed one).`  catalog.cpp:2056: 'RENAME COLUMN takes the relation `X` since `alter.md` AL4a'.

**Suggested.** Reword the comment to the current reason (the position is the one the RESTRICT was asked of under this statement's X; the name is passed only for the door's own guard), matching catalog.cpp:2055-2059.

### H98. CabinStore::Commit's un-announced arm is unreachable from the engine while a Cabin lives, and its "heal path" comment describes a caller that no longer exists

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `exec-cabin-transition` (`exec-cabin-transition#5`)
- **Where**: `src/stats/cabin_store.cpp:323`; origin: pre-3.4 (left behind by AT-S7's announce and AY-S6's Rebuild; not blamed)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Both engine callers of Commit (step_vm.cpp:1160, cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:290) call BeginRecording first, and BeginRecording refuses an observed key, so Commit always finds a building mark unless Forget erased it for a dropped cabin_id. The branch that replaces an already-observed set wholesale is therefore not reached for a live Cabin. Its comment calls it the heal path, but healing now goes through Rebuild, and ApplyHeal's own comment says a wholesale Commit would drop the write hook's appends, which is a C1 break. The arm is a latent hazard for any future caller that commits without announcing.

**As narrowed by verification.** The arm is not dead code, but its 'heal path' comment is stale and its replace-wholesale sub-arm is unreachable for a live Cabin. Both engine callers of CabinStore::Commit announce first (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1041 then :1160; src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:119 then :290), so Commit finds a building mark unless Forget (cabin_store.cpp:507-519) erased it for a dropped cabin_id. In that case the un-announced arm IS taken, deliberately: Forget's own comment says 'Commit on a key with no mark and no set banks a fresh one, which is the same answer a probe compiled before the DROP already gets'. The replace sub-arm (cabin_store.cpp:316-325, 'That is the heal path') is reached in the engine only by a dropped Cabin's race (walk A's mark erased by Forget, a stale probe B announces and commits the same key, then A commits), and directly by tests/cabin_store_test.cpp (24 Commit calls, 7 BeginRecording calls; line 226 pins the replace behaviour). The comment describes no existing caller: a heal goes through Rebuild (cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:446), BeginRecording's comment (cabin_store.cpp:245-249) says the heal un-observes before it re-records, and ApplyHeal's comment (cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:397-399) says a wholesale Commit would drop the write hook's appends, a C1 break. What stands is a stale comment and an un-guarded entry point for a future caller that commits without announcing, not removable dead code; a Commit that refused without a mark would also have to change Forget's documented post-DROP answer and the store's unit tests.

**Evidence.** cabin_store.cpp:316-325 `// Re-observing a value that is already observed replaces its set. // That is the **heal** path` followed by `RemoveSetLocked(...); part.observed.erase(existing);`. cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:397-399 `A wholesale `Commit` would drop those appends, and a set missing a live tuple key is the C1 break`. A grep for a direct store Commit call under tests/ matches tests/cabin_store_test.cpp only; that file was not opened.

**Suggested.** Make Commit refuse (return false) when the key has no building mark, or at least when it is already observed without one, and move tests to BeginRecording plus Commit. Otherwise correct the comment to say the arm is reachable only after Forget and must never serve as a heal.

### H99. parser-v2.md I12 states two opposite contracts for a renamed plan's state and its DROP TABLE RESTRICT

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `plans-cx` (`plans-cx#3`)
- **Where**: `docs/spec/parser-v2.md:143`; origin: DI-S4 (871845e4) added the new sentence and left CX-S1b's (eb467da9) in the same paragraph
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The I12 paragraph says a plan a rename put out of reach 'reads `invalid` too, and restricts no drop' (DI-S4), and a few sentences later still says 'A rename orphans a plan without marking it: ... which still reads `enabled` ... and still RESTRICTs the renamed relation's drop until its `DROP PLAN` or `DISABLE`'. The code implements the first: the sys.plans view sets `invalid` and the DROP TABLE RESTRICT skips the plan when PlanRowCanMatch is false. The second sentence is true now only for the unestablished case (a column written unqualified), which known-gaps.md states correctly. CLAUDE.md makes the spec the authority, so the owning spec currently states a RESTRICT the engine no longer applies.

**Evidence.** src/server/sys_views.cpp:1032: `if (!exec::PlanRowCanMatch(catalog_, plan, tables.value())) state = "invalid";`. src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:4927: `if (superseded || !exec::PlanRowCanMatch(catalog_, plan, tables.value())) { continue; }`. `git log -S"A rename orphans a plan without marking it"` gives eb467da9 only; `-S"A plan a rename put out of reach reads"` gives 871845e4.

**Suggested.** Delete the 'A rename orphans a plan without marking it' sentence from parser-v2.md I12, or narrow it to the unestablished cases (an unqualified renamed column, a statement stored cut short), matching the known-gaps.md entry. Check drop-table.md DT3 for the same leftover; I did not read it.
