### H101. Six of CabinStore::Stats' eight counters are counted under the instance-wide stats_latch_ and read by no production code

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: medium, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dead-lower` (`sweep-dead-lower#1`)
- **Where**: `src/stats/cabin_store.cpp:138`; origin: pre-3.4 (struct from fa9a7ed1, the Cabin store; no 3.4 commit changed the counters)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** CabinStore::Stats has eight fields (include/kds/stats/cabin_store.hpp:297-311). Production reads two, unbankable_views and cap_refusals, at src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:2265-2266 (sys.meta's cabins section). The other six - hits, misses, recordings, appends, unobserved, sighting_clears - are incremented under the one-per-instance stats_latch_ and read only by tests. Per-cabin hits and misses are kept separately in part.info under the partition latch, so the instance-wide copies are redundant as well as unread. known-gaps.md:3062 records the latch cost of a Cabin probe counting itself and gives it to DL; it does not say the counters have no consumer, so for these six the fix is deletion, not a per-core rewrite.

**Evidence.** cabin_store.cpp:137-140 `{ LatchGuard hold(Armed(stats_latch_)); ++stats_.hits; }`, the same shape at :153-156 (misses) and :561-562 (appends, on the write path). `grep -rn "cabins_->stats()" src include tools sim` returns only command_dispatcher.cpp:2265 (.unbankable_views) and :2266 (.cap_refusals). Test readers by grep over tests and sim: stats().hits x11, recordings x2, appends x1, unobserved x1, sighting_clears x1.

**Suggested.** Delete the six fields and their increments, which removes the stats_latch_ acquisition from NoteHit, NoteMiss, the recording commit and the write-hook append. Keep unbankable_views and cap_refusals. Tests that assert on stats().hits can read the per-cabin CabinInfo counters. Any gain would show as server CPU per statement on a Cabin-served probe and on an INSERT into a relation with a Cabin, at cores > 1 in build-release; not measured here.

### H102. The crash-point facility has no engine call site: 151 library lines and a 73-line test for a protocol that was retired

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dead-lower` (`sweep-dead-lower#2`)
- **Where**: `src/base/crash_point.cpp:61`; origin: pre-3.4 (built for the 2PC gate, RP7; call sites retired at AT-S6; last touched 0733ec9f and 1e86db61)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** kds::base::CrashPointHit, ParseCrashPointArm and ArmedCrashPoint (src/base/crash_point.cpp, 92 lines; include/kds/base/crash_point.hpp, 59 lines) are compiled into the kds library (CMakeLists.txt:50) and called by nothing in src, include, sim, fuzz, tools or scripts. The only caller is tests/crash_point_test.cpp, which exercises 2PC point names ("coordinator.before_prepare", "participant.prepare_logged_predurable") for a protocol the engine now refuses (TXN_PREPARE is refused Corruption). The header says so itself; no audit, known-gaps entry or work order records it.

**Evidence.** crash_point.hpp:12-14: "**No engine call site exists**: the cross-owner commit path's points retired with 2PC (AT-S6; history at cf889b63). The facility and `tests/crash_point_test.cpp` remain for the next protocol point." The reference census gives CrashPointHit two production occurrences (declaration and definition) and six in tests; crash_point.hpp's only includers are crash_point.cpp and tests/crash_point_test.cpp.

**Suggested.** Delete crash_point.cpp, crash_point.hpp, tests/crash_point_test.cpp and the two CMake lines; the facility is recoverable from git (cf889b63) if a later protocol wants it. The sim harness's seed-chosen crash op and the DB torn-write sweeps are what crash testing uses today.

### H103. SimWakerTable, a two-core test rig, is compiled into the engine library and referenced only by tests

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dead-lower` (`sweep-dead-lower#3`)
- **Where**: `src/sched/sim_waker_table.cpp:7`; origin: pre-3.4 (AV-R1, 2026-09-07 per waker_table.hpp:30-31)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** src/sched/sim_waker_table.cpp (80 lines) and include/kds/sched/sim_waker_table.hpp (131 lines) are in the kds library's source list (CMakeLists.txt:204, between add_library at :47 and add_executable at :241). No file under src, include, sim, fuzz or tools names SimWakerTable outside its own unit, apart from one comment. This is the same class of defect as G47 (BufferPool) for a different unit, and G47 does not name it. The sim/ harness does not use it either; it is the tests' rig.

**Evidence.** `grep -rnw SimWakerTable src include tools sim fuzz` returns only sim_waker_table.cpp's own definitions and the comment at include/kds/sched/waker_table.hpp:28. Test users: tests/two_core_rig.hpp, two_core_rig_test.cpp, lock_wake_rig_test.cpp, row_wait_wake_rig_test.cpp, sim_waker_table_test.cpp. waker_table.hpp:27-28: "The interface exists for exactly one other implementation, `SimWakerTable`".

**Suggested.** Move the two files into a test-support target alongside tests/two_core_rig.hpp (211 lines out of the engine library). The WakeRegistry seam and its one indirect call per Kick stay, since the rig needs them. If G47's narrow form is taken up, do both in the same change.

### H104. CabinStore::Unobserve, the per-value drop, has no production caller; comments still name it as the heal path that clears the entry-cap mark

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `sweep-dead-lower` (`sweep-dead-lower#5`)
- **Where**: `src/stats/cabin_store.cpp:403`; origin: pre-3.4 (defined at fa9a7ed1; `git log -S".Unobserve(" -- src` last hits 0205444b, BZ-S1, which is an ancestor of v3.4.0 - whether that commit removed the last caller was not read)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** CabinStore::Unobserve(const CabinKey&) (cabin_store.cpp:403-432, 30 lines) is called only by tests/cabin_store_test.cpp. Production drops Cabin state wholesale through Discard(cabin_id) (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:10688, 10773). Three consequences. (1) The "droppable per value" half of cabin.md C1, as CLAUDE.md states it, has no production path. (2) The sticky entry_capped mark is cleared in production only by Discard and by the sighting table's wholesale reset, not by the heal path that include/kds/stats/cabin_store.hpp:414 names. (3) Unobserve's arm that marks an announced build false (cabin_store.cpp:416-418, the AT-S7 rule) is exercised by no production interleaving.

**As narrowed by verification.** CabinStore::Unobserve(const CabinKey&) (src/stats/cabin_store.cpp:403-432) has no production caller - only tests/cabin_store_test.cpp:121 and :434 - and the comment at include/kds/stats/cabin_store.hpp:414 therefore names a clearing path for the sticky entry_capped mark that production never takes: in production the mark is cleared only by the sighting table's wholesale reset (cabin_store.cpp:227) and by Discard (:485-486). The per-value drop itself is NOT absent from production: NoteWrite's cap arm (cabin_store.cpp:534-548) un-observes one value's set on the write path, and it carries the same AT-S7 rule of marking an announced build false (:543-546), so cabin.md C1's per-value droppability and the building-mark rule both have a live production path; only the explicit API and its entry_capped/sighting erase are test-only.

**Evidence.** `grep -rnw Unobserve src include tools sim` returns the definition at cabin_store.cpp:403, the declaration at cabin_store.hpp:495, and comments only; command_dispatcher.cpp:10685 reads "`Discard`, not `Unobserve` of the uncoerced key". cabin_store.hpp:414: "What clears it: `Unobserve` (the heal path - the world provably changed)".

**Suggested.** Delete Unobserve and its test cell, and reword cabin_store.hpp:414 and the comments at cabin_store.cpp:299, 365 and 374 to name Discard and the wholesale reset as what clears the mark. If a per-value drop is meant to exist, cabin.md C1 should say which production event calls it. Medium confidence only because I did not read cabin.md C1's text or the fk and step-VM heal sites end to end; those call CabinSet::Heal, a different function.

### H105. KWP, SCRAM and TLS client halves are compiled into the server and called only by tests

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `sweep-dead-upper` (`sweep-dead-upper#1`)
- **Where**: `src/server/scram.cpp:468`; origin: pre-3.4
- **Verification**: first verifier: **narrowed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The client side of the wire protocol lives in the engine's own translation units and has no caller in src, include or tools. The sites are: `scram::Client` (src/server/scram.cpp:468-551, class at include/kds/server/scram.hpp:151); `EncodeClientHello`, `EncodeCancelRequest`, `DecodeServerHello` and `EncodeLoadBegin` (src/wire/kwp_types.cpp:11, 45, 73, 92); `EncodeBindParams`, `DecodeRowDescription` and `DecodeText` (src/wire/row_codec.cpp:463, 353, 447); `DecodeError` (src/wire/error_registry.cpp:140); and `TlsContext::NewClient` (src/server/tls_channel.cpp:172). The only shipped client, tools/kwp.py, is Python and reimplements all of it. This is the largest removable block in the slice, roughly 200-250 lines.

**As narrowed by verification.** The KWP/SCRAM/TLS client-side encoders and decoders (scram::Client, EncodeClientHello, EncodeCancelRequest, DecodeServerHello, EncodeLoadBegin, EncodeBindParams, DecodeRowDescription, DecodeText, DecodeError, TlsContext::NewClient) sit in the `kds` library the server links and have test callers only; they are test support, not dead code, and for SCRAM, TLS and cancel they are the only client implementation in the tree (tools/kwp.py implements none of the three). The scram and tls headers already say the tests are the consumer; what is stale is the promise of a C++ CLI or conformance harness, and the wire headers (kwp_types.hpp, row_codec.hpp, error_registry.hpp) do not say the client halves are test-only. Moving them to a test-support target is a packaging choice, not a defect.

**Evidence.** A word grep of each symbol over src and include returns only its declaration and definition (plus one comment mention at include/kds/wire/kwp.hpp:265); callers are in tests only, e.g. tests/tcp_server_test.cpp:449 `scram::Client viewer_client("viewer", "pencil");`. include/kds/server/scram.hpp:149-150 says: "The client half - what the tests drive both ends with, and what the CLI and the future KWP conformance harness will reuse." No C++ CLI or harness exists in the tree; tools/ckdbs_cli.py is Python.

**Suggested.** Move the client halves into a test-support library linked by the test targets, so the server binary carries only what it runs and the header comment stops promising a consumer that does not exist. Alternatively, keep them and state in the headers that they are test-only.

### H106. RowBatchWriter::RollbackLastRow has no caller anywhere, tests included

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dead-upper` (`sweep-dead-upper#3`)
- **Where**: `src/wire/row_codec.cpp:331`; origin: pre-3.4 (beec2602, 2026-08-27, "pipeline: seal a batch exactly, not by predicting the next row")
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** `RowBatchWriter::RollbackLastRow` (src/wire/row_codec.cpp:331-340, declared at include/kds/wire/row_codec.hpp:199 under a 13-line rationale comment) is referenced nowhere in src, include, tests, sim, fuzz or tools. The typed-row path no longer goes through `RowBatchWriter`: it bounds a batch by encoding each row into its own buffer first (src/server/kwp_session.cpp:272). `RowBatchWriter` itself has one remaining production user, the diagnostic-line reply at kwp_session.cpp:880, which never rolls back. About 25 lines including the comment.

**Evidence.** A grep for RollbackLastRow across the tree returns two lines only: include/kds/wire/row_codec.hpp:199 and src/wire/row_codec.cpp:331. The header comment still says it is "exposed for a caller that has to decide *after* seeing a row's encoded width - which is the only way to bound a batch exactly". kwp_session.cpp:272 reads `(open_.size() + row.size() > wire::kRowBatchTargetBytes ||`.

**Suggested.** Delete the method and its comment. The comment describes a sealing rule the session no longer implements this way.

### H107. Four small unreferenced leftovers: KeywordText, S_NOTICE's frame kind, varheap_slots_swept

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dead-upper` (`sweep-dead-upper#5`)
- **Where**: `src/parser/lexer.cpp:74`; origin: pre-3.4 (4b76cef3, f53b26ec, b5807c9c)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** Three leftovers no code, test or tool reads. (a) `parser::KeywordText` (src/parser/lexer.cpp:74-79, include/kds/parser/token.hpp:195-196) is declared "for error messages" and called by nothing. (b) `ServerFrameType::kNotice` (include/kds/wire/kwp.hpp:148) is never sent by the server, although docs/spec/protocol.md:67 lists `S_NOTICE` among the server's frames and tools/kwp.py:450 handles it. (c) `MountRecoveryReport::varheap_slots_swept` (include/kds/server/mount_recovery.hpp:152, under an 11-line comment) is assigned at src/server/expeditor.cpp:1094 and read by no sys view, log line or test.

**Evidence.** A grep for KeywordText returns only token.hpp:196 and lexer.cpp:74. The qualified-enumerator sweep gives `ServerFrameType::kNotice` zero references in src, include, tests, sim and fuzz. A grep for varheap_slots_swept over src, include, tests, sim, tools and docs returns only mount_recovery.hpp:152 and expeditor.cpp:1094: `expeditor->recovery_.varheap_slots_swept = swept.value().released;`.

**Suggested.** Delete `KeywordText`. For `kNotice`, keep the enumerator (it pins the wire numbering before `kAuth = 13`) and add the word "reserved, never sent" beside it and in protocol.md, as is done for `COMPRESSION`. For `varheap_slots_swept`, either surface it in sys.meta's recovery section next to the other recovery_* fields or drop the field.

### H108. superblock.hpp says ids 18..127 are unused and the fixed roots are 4-17, but page 18 is sys.plans' fixed root

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `wal-delta` (`wal-delta#2`)
- **Where**: `include/kds/server/superblock.hpp:43`; origin: CX-S1a (4f62dd2a) added page 18; the comment was last moved by CY-S1a (e13efd80)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** The comment that defines the reserved page-id range misstates it. It says ids 18..127 are "unused, left for a future bootstrap root" and that the catalog's fixed roots are ids 4-17. CX-S1a made page 18 the fixed root of sys.plans (superblock 37), and the same file's version 37 comment says so. Since superblock 38, page 11 is no longer a root either.

**Evidence.** superblock.hpp:43-44: `root is handed out here like a user page (superblock 31); ids 18..127` / `are unused, left for a future bootstrap root.` and :48 `(kds::catalog::kCatalogPage*, ids 4-17)`. well_known.hpp:408: `inline constexpr PageId kCatalogPagePlans = 18;`.

**Suggested.** Correct both lines to 4-18 and 19..127 (noting page 11 as reserved and unused since 38), or replace the literals with a reference to well_known.hpp's last fixed root so the next bootstrap relation does not pick 18 from this comment.

### H109. EncodeFullPageImage is referenced only by tests; both production writers of kind 10 append the page bytes directly

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `wal-delta` (`wal-delta#4`)
- **Where**: `src/wal/payload.cpp:478`; origin: pre-3.4 encoder; DH's WalManager::LogPageImage is the second production site that bypasses it
- **Verification**: first verifier: **confirmed**

**Claim (the finder's).** No file under src, include, sim, fuzz or tools calls EncodeFullPageImage. The two production writers of FULL_PAGE_IMAGE pass the page span straight to Append: storage::LogFullPageImage (log_page_image.hpp:40) and WalManager::LogPageImage's verbatim arm (manager.cpp:471-476). The encoder, with its 8 KiB memcpy, is exercised only by tests/wal_redo_test.cpp:247, :741 and tests/wal_payload_test.cpp:367, so those tests pin a function the engine does not run.

**Evidence.** grep of the tree: `src/wal/payload.cpp:478: StatusOr<std::size_t> EncodeFullPageImage(...)`, `include/kds/wal/payload.hpp:412` (declaration), and three test call sites; no other reference. log_page_image.hpp:37-38: "The payload is the page itself (`payload.hpp`: no prefix), so the held bytes are appended as they stand."

**Suggested.** Remove the encoder and have the tests build the record the way production does (the page span as the payload), or keep it and say in payload.hpp that it is a test helper. DecodeFullPageImage stays: redo uses it.

## 7. Findings not carried: refuted, disputed, already recorded, undetermined

### H110. Ordered index runs allocate a hash node per matched row for a dedup that one remembered tuple key gives

- **Kind**: inefficiency; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `exec-vm` (`exec-vm#2`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1827`; origin: CV-S2 (10e7b0a4), reused by CV-S3 (7d11ba44)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **already-known**
- **Recorded in**: instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-cv-ordered-index-walk.md, CV-S2 record, Review, "Not applied" (lines 449-452); the set itself is mandated by CV-R2 (same file, line 178) and docs/spec/index.md IX8c "The seen set" (line 446)

**Claim (the finder's).** RunOrderedIndexStep and RunReverseOrderedIndexStep each keep a statement-local `std::unordered_set<uint64_t> emitted`, probed for every batch entry and inserted into for every matched row. By the code's own statement the only duplicates it exists for are entries a covered column's move leaves side by side under one key. Those are adjacent in entry order (key, tuple key, covered bytes), and both runs process entries strictly monotonically, so remembering the last emitted tuple key is enough. For an index with no covered columns no two entries share (key, tuple key) and the key check alone already gives at-most-once, so the set buys nothing.

**Why it is not carried.** [source-read] at fba5529f. The facts hold: src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1827 and :1923 each declare a statement-local std::unordered_set<uint64_t> emitted; ResolveBatch probes it per batch entry (:2012) and inserts per matched row (:2016). ordered_matched_ is set in OrderedKeyMatches (:2111), called at :3104-3108 before the sub-chains and deeper steps, so a matched driver row is inserted even when an inner step rejects it. The adjacency argument is also sound on its face: entries sort by key, tuple key, covered bytes, and both runs go monotonically. But the exact change proposed was raised and declined at CV-S2's critics-developer review. The work order records: "Not applied: replacing the seen set by an adjacency test - CV-R2 names the set, it holds only the rows emitted, and the adjacency argument would rest on the walk never repeating an entry, which B1 just showed is a property to defend, not assume". B1 was the defect where `from` moved back and entries were re-collected, and the seen set is what kept the reply right. The no-covered-columns shortcut rests on the same never-repeats premise, so the recorded decision covers it too. CV-R2 is marked [quiet-wrong] and requires the set by name. This is a deliberate, documented decision, not a new finding. I did not read the index insert path for identical-entry uniqueness; it is not needed for this verdict.

### H111. WalkBtreeLeaves builds a heap-allocated std::function wrapper once per leaf, and every slot goes through two type-erased calls

- **Kind**: performance; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `dispatcher-3` (`dispatcher-3#4`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:8769`; origin: pre-3.4 (AO-S3b, 07c94c7f, in v3.1.0)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **already-known**
- **Recorded in**: docs/pending/whole-engine-audit-unverified-findings.md, the readers' notes, `dispatcher-3` bullet (line 100): 'Smaller items not reported: ... WalkBtreeLeaves rebuilds a std::function per leaf' - noted by the F audit as an unreported smaller item, never taken up and not decided

**Claim (the finder's).** Inside the `while (leaf != kInvalidPageId)` loop a `const std::function<...> guarded = [&](...)` is constructed for each leaf. The closure captures five variables by reference (resuming, resume_tuple_key, fn, cut, cut_tuple_key), which is 40 bytes - past the small-object buffer of libstdc++ (16 bytes) and libc++ (24) - so each leaf costs one allocation and one free, and nothing in the closure is per-leaf. Each slot is then called as BtreeVisitLeafPage -> guarded -> fn, two std::function dispatches. Every UPDATE and DELETE walk, the subquery collection pass (WriteWalk::CollectTargets) and the other VisitRelation callers (seven call sites in src/server) go through it.

**As narrowed by verification.** As claimed, except the walk has four call sites (UPDATE walk, DELETE walk, FOR UPDATE's RunLockingRead, the write's subquery collection pass), not seven; and the per-leaf std::function was already noted, unreported, by the F audit's dispatcher-3 reader.

**Why it is not carried.** [source-read] at fba5529f. The facts hold: src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:8765-8822 constructs `const std::function<...> guarded = [&](...)` inside `while (leaf != kInvalidPageId)`; the closure odr-uses resuming, resume_tuple_key (both const locals with non-constant initialisers, 8747-8748), fn, cut and cut_tuple_key - five reference captures, no `this` (KeystoneIdOfPayload is the free kds:: function) - 40 bytes, over libstdc++'s 16-byte local buffer, so a heap allocation and a free per leaf; nothing captured is per-leaf (key_at captures only the lambda's own `page` parameter). BtreeVisitLeafPage takes `const std::function<...>&` in another translation unit (src/storage/btree/btree.cpp:1339-1342), so the allocation cannot be elided, and each slot goes BtreeVisitLeafPage -> guarded -> fn, two type-erased calls. One stated fact is wrong: the callers are four, not seven - VisitRelation at command_dispatcher.cpp:11679 (the subquery collection pass), 12534 (UPDATE walk), 12704 (RunLockingRead, DC's FOR UPDATE) and 15658 (DELETE walk); WalkBtreeLeaves's only caller is VisitRelation (8849). A SELECT's walk does not go through it (step_vm.cpp:2439 has its own slot loop). The F1-F32 audit's dispatcher-3 reader already wrote this down as a smaller item it did not report; the heap-allocation size argument and the double dispatch are this finder's additions. Cost is one malloc/free per leaf beside that leaf's fetch, latch and per-row write work - small; unmeasured.

### H112. A hot join shape serialises every core's compile on one exclusive mutex under the default join_order = fixed

- **Kind**: performance; severity low (the finder's: medium, confidence medium); round 1, `plans-cx` (`plans-cx#1`)
- **Where**: `src/exec/captured_plans.cpp:37`; origin: CX-S0 (captured table), CX-S1b (admin table); CX-Q22 (b) built against the adopted (a)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **already-known**
- **Recorded in**: instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-cx-managed-plans.md:707-712 (CX-S0 stage log, 'CX-Q22: (b) is built, not the adopted (a) ... Its cost is one partition latch on a searched block's compile'); include/kds/exec/captured_plans.hpp:40-47 and include/kds/exec/admin_plans.hpp:71-74 (latch protocol headers); docs/inflight/known-gaps.md:2674-2685 ('No cell runs two reactors' statements under fixed'); instructions/v3.2.0/raft-marks-2026-11-18.md:254 (the deviation is listed as not yet ruled on)

**Claim (the finder's).** CapturedPlans::Find takes an exclusive latch (a std::mutex, include/kds/base/latch.hpp:81) on the partition chosen by the shape's key. Every compile of a non-descent join block under `fixed` calls it (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:2831), so all cores running the same statement shape take the same mutex once a statement; the 64 partitions spread different shapes only. AdminPlans::Find (src/exec/admin_plans.cpp:59) has the same shape with 16 partitions and adds a shared_ptr copy under the latch, i.e. a refcount increment and decrement on one shared cache line per compile. The data is read-mostly: an installed key's order never changes until DROP PLAN or a committed DROP TABLE erases it.

**Why it is not carried.** [source-read] The stated facts hold at fba5529f. src/exec/captured_plans.cpp:37-43: Find takes LatchGuard on the key's partition; include/kds/base/latch.hpp:68-76: Latch is a std::mutex (try_lock, then lock and a contention charge), kind kStatsRing (captured_plans.hpp:185, admin_plans.hpp:193). src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:2792-2831: with plan->captured set (only under kFixed, src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:10238), every compile that survives the all-descent skip (step_compiler.cpp:2757-2759) and has a shape calls captured->Find(captured_key); no memo or per-core cache precedes it, and CLAUDE.md/CX-S6 state no compiled-chain cache exists, so it is once per statement compile. The partition is chosen by the key hash (captured_plans.cpp:32-35), so one shape on all cores is one mutex. src/exec/admin_plans.cpp:59-65: same shape, 16 partitions (admin_plans.hpp:198), returns a shared_ptr copy under the latch; it is gated by the relaxed any() load (step_compiler.cpp:2697), so it costs only while some plan is enabled. No guard defeats the claim. But it is a recorded deliberate decision with its cost named: the work order's stage log and the header both say (b) was built against the adopted (a) to give first-install-wins (CX-R7), cost 'one partition latch on a searched block's compile', nothing on a point read or tuple key chain. What is not recorded is a measurement: no cell runs two cores under fixed (known-gaps.md:2683) and the operator has not ruled on the (b)-for-(a) deviation (raft-marks-2026-11-18.md:254). The critical section is one unordered_map find on a block that walks (non-descent), so the per-statement cost is small against the block's compile and run; the cross-core cache-line cost is plausible and unmeasured. Severity low until measured.

### H113. The cadence checkpoint's try-then-park-then-block loop is written three times

- **Kind**: duplication; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `wal-delta` (`wal-delta#3`)
- **Where**: `src/wal/checkpointer.cpp:507`; origin: CZ-S3
- **Verification**: first verifier: **already-known**
- **Recorded in**: instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-cz-checkpoint-roll-wait.md, section 'CZ-S3 - the build, 2026-11-18', the critics-developer review's 'Rejected' list (lines 804-808)

**Claim (the finder's).** The same control shape - try the latch entry, count a busy answer, park through ParkOnBusy, re-check the generation, and fall to the blocking entry with ++site.bound and blocking_ns once kLatchTries is reached - is hand-copied at the begin record (lines 507-533), the end record (587-611) and the log park's flush (768-798). The copies already differ in what bounds them: the begin and end loops count a loop-local `answers`, while the flush loop counts the run's `site.busy`. That is intended today, but it is the kind of divergence a fourth site or a change to the bound would get wrong in one copy.

**Why it is not carried.** [source-read] at fba5529f. The facts hold: src/wal/checkpointer.cpp:507-532 (begin), :585-612 (end) and :766-799 (flush, in ParkOnLog) are three hand-written copies of try / ++site.busy / skip the park at kLatchTries / ParkOnBusy / generation re-check / blocking entry with ++site.bound and blocking_ns, and the line 'if (site.busy >= static_cast<std::uint64_t>(kLatchTries)) continue;' is at :527, :606 and :787; begin and end gate on a loop-local 'answers', the flush on the run's site.busy (:774, with the comment at :767-770 saying why). But the fold into one helper was proposed and rejected in the stage's own review, recorded in the work order: 'folding the three sites' loops into one helper coroutine: the begin site's roll-flag test, the flush site's durable exit and its DurableWait differ, and a helper over callables would carry them as parameters; the three loops stay, each about twenty lines'. The same review also fixed the flush bound to per-run, so the answers/site.busy difference is a recorded decision, not drift. A deliberate, documented choice.

### H114. The `maintenance` scheduling group has no task: nothing in production submits to it, yet it has a share, a ready queue and three sys.meta fields

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence medium); round 1, `sweep-dead-lower` (`sweep-dead-lower#4`)
- **Where**: `include/kds/sched/task.hpp:16`; origin: pre-3.4 (the scheduler's original three groups; sched.md section 4)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **already-known**
- **Recorded in**: instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-ca-background-advisory-optimizer.md §1.4 (line 211: '`kMaintenance` is defined, and nothing submits to it. It is CA's group.'); the index row for CA (instructions/v3.2.0/index.md:16) says its structures are 'built by `kMaintenance` tasks'

**Claim (the finder's).** SchedulingGroup::kMaintenance ("statistics, hint-index upkeep") is one of three engine-defined groups, with a share of 100 in SchedulerConfig::group_shares (include/kds/sched/scheduler.hpp:61) and its own ready queue. Production creates tasks at three sites, all kSystem or kForeground. The statistics steward, the Cabin controller and the index jobs therefore do not run as maintenance tasks. sys.meta still prints sched_maintenance_polled_us, _polls and _consumed_us (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:2507-2516), which can only be 0. docs/spec/sched.md:54 lists the group with no note that it is empty, so the spec's claim that background engine work shares a core through this group describes nothing that is built.

**Why it is not carried.** [source-read] at fba5529f. The facts hold: `SchedulingGroup::k*` in src/include/tools/sim gives kSystem at src/wal/checkpointer.cpp:478 and src/server/tcp_server.cpp:455, kForeground at tcp_server.cpp:733 and scheduler.cpp:514, kMaintenance only at its declaration (include/kds/sched/task.hpp:16); FunctionTask is constructed nowhere under src, sim, tools or fuzz, and CoroTask (coro.hpp:308) is the only other Task subclass. But the emptiness is a recorded, deliberate reservation: the CA work order (opened 2026-10-17, exploratory) states it in as many words and names the group as CA's, so removing the group contradicts an open work order rather than cleaning up dead code. Two details of the claim are off: sys.meta does not carry 'three fields' for the group - command_dispatcher.cpp:2507-2518 prints every per-group field by a loop over kNumSchedulingGroups (polled_us, polls, consumed_us, longest_poll_us and more), so nothing there is maintenance-specific code; and the group's ready queue and share are array slots, not separate code. What remains new is only that docs/spec/sched.md:52-54 lists the group without saying no task is in it, a one-sentence spec omission.

**Claim (the finder's).** `BoundCabin::VerifyAgainstEntries` (src/exec/bound_cabin.cpp:479-515) and its `EntryReader` alias (include/kds/exec/bound_cabin.hpp:333-335) are called by four test files and by no engine path: not mount, not recovery, not a checkpoint, not a sys view. Engine comments at src/exec/assertion_recover.cpp:73, :189 and :381 and src/exec/assertion_check.cpp:922 justify their handling by what this function would report, which reads as though something in the engine runs it. About 45 lines.

**Why it is not carried.** [source-read] at fba5529f. The facts hold: a word grep over src, include, sim, fuzz, tools, scripts and bench finds the definition at src/exec/bound_cabin.cpp:479, the declaration at include/kds/exec/bound_cabin.hpp:335 and comments only (src/exec/assertion_check.cpp:922, src/exec/assertion_recover.cpp:73, :189, :381, include/kds/storage/cabin_bound_page.hpp:107); every call is in tests (bound_cabin_test, assertion_recover_test, assertion_wal_test, assertion_build_test). EntryReader has no other user. But the G audit already recorded that no production path calls it, both in G38's claim and in its verified row, and the spec treats it as the proof the tests run (docs/spec/assertion.md:270, :684, :713) while CK's work order uses it as a test oracle (instructions/v3.2.0/workorder-ck-assertion-commit-settle.md:455, :488-489). It is a test oracle the suite depends on, not removable code; what remains new is only the wording point that bound_cabin.hpp:326-327 calls it a 'verification hook' with nothing hooked.

### H116. VerifyOutcome's five failure enumerators are written and never read

- **Kind**: dead-code; severity low (the finder's: low, confidence high); round 1, `sweep-dead-upper` (`sweep-dead-upper#4`)
- **Where**: `include/kds/exec/tuple_verify.hpp:74`; origin: pre-3.4
- **Verification**: first verifier: **already-known**
- **Recorded in**: docs/pending/whole-engine-audit-second-run-twelve-slices.md, the `exec-rows-index` slice note (:113), under SEEN AND DELIBERATELY NOT REPORTED: 'VerifyOutcome's five miss values are never distinguished by any caller.'

**Claim (the finder's).** `VerifyTupleAt` distinguishes `kPageGone`, `kNotThisRelation`, `kEpochMismatch`, `kSlotGone` and `kTupleKeyMismatch` (src/exec/tuple_verify.cpp:17, 27, 39, 45, 56), but `VerifiedTuple::outcome` is read by nothing except `ok()`. No caller in step_vm.cpp or fk_check.cpp, and no test, branches on or counts the reason. The enum therefore carries a diagnosis that no statistic, ANALYZE line or test consumes.

**Why it is not carried.** [source-read] at fba5529f. The facts hold: VerifyOutcome appears only in the six assignments at src/exec/tuple_verify.cpp:17, 27, 39, 45, 56, 60, the enum at include/kds/exec/tuple_verify.hpp:74, the field default at :98 and ok() at :108; no `.outcome` reader of a VerifiedTuple exists in src, include, tests or sim (the `outcome` hits in fk_check.cpp are FkReverseOutcome, step_vm.cpp:607 is the sink's). All callers (step_vm.cpp:714, 759, 1416, 2153, 2239; fk_check.cpp:176; cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:413; tests/drop_table_reclaim_test.cpp:667; tests/cabin_optimizer_exec_test.cpp:298) use ok() only. The G audit's exec-rows-index reader saw exactly this and chose not to report it. It is a self-documenting return reason with no cost on the path (one byte store), so I would not rate it above low.

### H117. ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE binds EXCLUDED.<col> as an untyped literal, so the arm refuses decimal, date and timestamp values the insert arm accepts - only on rows that conflict - and accepts ill-typed uses

- **Kind**: logic-defect; severity medium (the finder's: medium, confidence medium); round 2, `x-insert-conflict` (`x-insert-conflict#1`)
- **Where**: `src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:7926`; origin: CG-S1, commit b7ddff92 (2026-10-23, first tagged in v3.4.4)
- **Verification**: first verifier: **not-verified**

**Claim (the finder's).** BindExcluded replaces EXCLUDED.<col> with a bare kLiteral holding the proposed row's AstValue and drops the column's type. CG-Q5 (a) says EXCLUDED is 'typed as the relation's columns'. The SET typer then re-types the literal from its AstValue kind alone. Three results: (1) a proposed value in storage form - any decimal column of an INSERT ... SELECT source or of a VALUES expression (kDecimal) - is refused 'this literal cannot appear in an expression'; (2) a date or timestamp in storage form (kInt: INSERT ... SELECT, NOW(), CURRENT_DATE) is refused as 'integer cannot be used as timestamp/date', although the insert arm stores the same value; (3) the type check the column would have had is gone, so an EXCLUDED of one type is silently accepted into a column of another. (1) and (2) fire only when a row conflicts, so the same statement succeeds or fails by data, after earlier rows are written.

**Why it is not carried.** verifier died

## 8. Coverage: what each round-1 reader read

### `dispatcher-1` - read, 4 findings

Read in full: src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:375-1991 (LockWaitRow, AwaitWriteBlock, AwaitStatementWaits, MissRefusalAdmitted, BeginMissRerun, AwaitPageRead, AwaitRelationLock, DispatchAsync, StructuralRerunDue, StatementEpochScope, DispatchAndStage, StageStatement, LogStatement, deferred commits, Dispatch, BeginStatement, RecordStatement, DispatchInternal, RequiredRole, StampStatement, DispatchInner); src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:2755-2895 (HandleSetCabinOptimizer, HandleSetJoinOrder, HandleDropPlan, head of HandleSetAsh); src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:2996-4001 (SuspensionHold, BuildRun, IndexBuildRun, DeclarationBuildRun, AssertionBuildRun/TransitionBuildRun Publish, GatherRun, ParseStatisticsDocument, StatisticsDocument, EndOptInsNaming, ForgetRelationStatistics, HandleStatistics up to its EXPORT arm); include/kds/server/read_borrow.hpp

Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] on worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture at fba5529f; nothing was built or run. NOT READ in the slice: lines 1-180 and 237-374 apart from the helpers named above, HandleBackup/HandleSync (1987-2139), most of MetaSections (2140-2754), the rest of HandleSetAsh and HandleCreateTable (2896-2995). The header was consulted for a few members only, not read. Checked and found clean (not findings): - AwaitWriteBlock's early exits (victim, net at loop top) leave a wake registered, but RefuseParkedWrite drops it (13296). The F audit dismissed the same. - The thread-local structural-refusal note across suspensions: I re-derived the F audit's dismissal for the paths new since ce5347e6 (AwaitPageRead always re-stages; a build's tail runs in its own StageStatement, which clears the note). No interleaving found that reaches StructuralRerunDue with a foreign note and a live deadline. - DE's discarded run: an armed run is a descent run inline, so a page_wait outcome carrying a write_block or lock_wait slot (which AwaitPageRead's re-stage would overwrite without DropWake) looks unreachable. It is a latent contract, not a defect today. - The lifetime sweep cannot roll back a transaction under a suspended BuildRun/SelectRun (conn.in_flight). - HandleDropPlan dereferences admin_plans_/captured_plans_ unguarded, but both default to the dispatcher's own tables. - IMPORT STATISTICS' document_bytes[bad_at] is in bounds (bad_at <= doc.size(), vector has size+1 entries). - ManagedStatistics::Store refuses a forgotten oid, so a gather that outlives its relation publishes nothing. Observations not raised to findings: - A DispatchAsync statement reads the clock about 7-9 times (began_ns 952, FlushOnCpu in BeginStatement, started_ns 1204 when a logger exists, statement_head_ns_ 1270, the compile's system_clock read and compile_ns read, then NowNs 1467, system_clock 1469 and FlushOnCpu 1496). Three of the head reads are of the same instant. DF's work order chose the head stamp and "one more clock read a run" knowingly and its close cost was accepted, so I did not report it. - RecordStatement (1468) and PURGE's verdict (15082) test response.rfind("ERR") without the trailing space that kErrorPrefix and LogStatement use. The F audit recorded bare-ERR refusals as accepted; I did not establish a SELECT reply that can begin with "ERR". - DeclarationBuildRun's tail and HandleDropPlan sync the log synchronously on the reactor (AwaitDdlDurability -> SyncAll); ddl-transactional.md §7 states it. - DispatchInner's statistics route has a redundant third clause (`IEquals(cmd, "GATHER")` after the STATISTICS-second test already covers GATHER); harmless. - ManagedStatistics::forgotten_ grows by one oid per dropped relation for the run (outside the slice, not examined further).

### `dispatcher-2` - read, 4 findings

Read in full: src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:3950-7868 (the whole slice 3950-7850, plus TupleKeyPlaced's tail to 7868)

Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture at fba5529f (v3.4.17); nothing was built or run. The slice was read end to end; most of it is DDL (statistics verbs' tail, HandleIndex, HandleCabin, HandleAlter, ADD/DROP COLUMN, DROP TABLE, namespace, assertion, transition, CREATE TABLE), plus the FK checks, WAL logging helpers, SelectRun and the INSERT head. CU key-set writes, InsertOnConflict's body, the UPDATE/DELETE row walks and DC's locking read lie past line 7850 and were not read (only the few call sites listed under consulted). Checked and found sound, not reported: (a) the forward FK hoist's S-before-descent order, the check view minted after the grant, and the 'busy reads present' arm for a U holder (no quiet-wrong path found: every head that passes is non-deleted with a predecessor, which is a present row whichever way the writer ends); (b) CheckForeignKeyOnWrite's ReleaseIf of absent parents' S (mode- and verdict-filtered, an own X is untouched); (c) WaitForRowWriter's three arms and the precedence of the ceiling wait over NoteBlockingWriter's poll; (d) NormalizeInsertRows / NormalizeListedInsertRows arity and heap rules; (e) the tuple-key ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING pre-pass: KeystoneIdOf is called on r.front() with no type test, but a non-kInt key value appears to carry int_val 0 on every path I could trace (decimal literals reach the codec as text, INSERT...SELECT and VALUES expressions are typed against the int key column), so I could not build a wrong-skip scenario and did not report it; a type guard there would make that independent of parser behaviour; (f) HandleDropTable's double RESTRICT ask and the DI-S4 plan exemption; (g) rename paths (AL4a X, re-resolution under the grant), with rename-versus-plan/opt-in effects already in known-gaps.md. Considered, not reported: DI's head compile of an ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE arm's subqueries on every execution (command_dispatcher.cpp:7567-7577) is a second compile of each subquery, but it was applied deliberately by the DI-S1 review (workorder-di-shape-identity.md, review item 3) and reaches only statements with a subquery in the conflict arm. An ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING statement that mixes an issued key with a later row naming that same id fails AlreadyExists rather than skipping (the pre-pass decides before any row is placed); a refusal, not a wrong answer. DROP INDEX's and DROP CABIN's plan RESTRICT do not take DROP TABLE's 'plan out of reach' exemption; conservative, a refusal only. Finding 1's premise (a commit that has released its row X can still be above the snapshot ceiling because another commit's marker holds it down) is taken from the tree's own text: WaitForRowWriter's comment at 6041-6046 and foreign-keys.md section 3a; I did not trace the marker code beyond FinishCommitBody's comments. The std::function small-buffer claims in findings 2 and 3 assume libstdc++ (16-byte local storage); no -stdlib=libc++ was found in CMakeLists.txt or scripts.

WHERE refused by the parser); the catalog view's skipped key conjunct (only sys.columns and sys.page_slots are keyed); index_use_cache_ pointers (index_stats.hpp removes no row); EndDdlScopeById's unguarded reclaim_queue_ at 9190 (NoteOwedIndex gates on the same pointer, so the list is empty when it is null); RecordPlanStats' mask shifts (key.n is bounded by kMaxPosteriorRelations at step_compiler.cpp:2671). (2) WithReturning (11241-11242) parses the whole statement a second time and resolves the relation again for every write whose text contains the word RETURNING, including inside a string literal; pre-3.4, not weighed further. Not read: src/exec/plan_printer.cpp and its header, include/kds/exec/plan_ddl.hpp, the body of src/exec/join_order.cpp outside the 3.4 diff, the Choice functions the ladder calls (LiteralIndexChoiceOf, CabinProbeOf and the rest), InsertDeclaration/DeleteDeclarationsById, tests/plan_*. Checked against the slice's focus list and found sound (not findings): - Key identity: both tables key on fetch id + relation count + every oid (CapturedKey, defaulted ==). A drop/recreate misses on the oid; a rename is DI-S4's PlanRowCanMatch, which errs only towards "can match" (it returns true on every unestablished case). - A captured or administrator's order goes through compile_order -> CompileBound, the full ladder, and is declined to the written chain on a failed compile or AddedSortCannotRefuse. A decoded order is checked as a permutation of the key's relations (IsPermutation) before it can index anything. - ARG(k): CREATE PLAN stores fingerprint->literal_count, the compile reads ArgumentsOf(line); the select list is outside the fetch id and holds no literal, and a wrong variant is another order of the same inner block. I did not verify literal_count == ArgumentsOf().size() for every token class (typed literals). - ACCESS pins: every keyed rank in step_compiler.cpp:3252-3472 is guarded by pin.Admits(class), the named index by `only`, the named Cabin by CabinGate; an unmet pin refuses the variant's compile, which the caller declines. I saw no forcing at this level; the Choice functions were not read. - Crash order: ReplaceAdminPlan logs the insert before the delete, so a log prefix never holds the delete alone; LoadPlans and FindPlan both take the highest id of a name. - A capture for a relation whose drop has committed: closed in practice. An open DDL mints a view (may_capture false), and a compile that began before it holds the borrow the drop's X waits for. - The one-relation plan's probe cost (CX-S3b) was measured at CX-S6 and is recorded, so it is left out. - FOR UPDATE never reaches a chain compile (step_compiler.cpp:2293), so no plan answers it. Smaller observations, not reported as findings: PlanAdvices keeps rows for dropped relations until a restart; PersistCapturedPlans writes at most 64 rows a second, so a burst of new shapes has a long crash-loss window (the loss itself is documented); the plan tables' latches are constructed as LatchKind::kStatsRing, so any wait on them is charged to that kind in the contention accounting.

### `stats-stores` - read, 3 findings

Read in full: src/stats/access_stats.cpp; include/kds/stats/access_stats.hpp; src/stats/statement_stats.cpp; include/kds/stats/statement_stats.hpp; include/kds/stats/statement_binding.hpp; src/stats/relation_stats.cpp; include/kds/stats/relation_stats.hpp; src/stats/refusal_stats.cpp; include/kds/stats/refusal_stats.hpp; src/stats/txn_stats.cpp; include/kds/stats/txn_stats.hpp; src/stats/leaf_stats.cpp; include/kds/stats/leaf_stats.hpp; src/stats/lock_wait_log.cpp; include/kds/stats/lock_wait_log.hpp; src/stats/checkpoint_log.cpp; include/kds/stats/checkpoint_log.hpp; src/stats/plan_stats.cpp; include/kds/stats/plan_stats.hpp; include/kds/stats/plan_key.hpp; src/stats/managed_statistics.cpp; include/kds/stats/managed_statistics.hpp; include/kds/stats/oid_parts.hpp; include/kds/stats/per_core_ring.hpp; include/kds/stats/index_stats.hpp; include/kds/stats/instance_key.hpp

Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] at fba5529f in the worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing was built, run or measured. The struct sizes in finding 1 are derived from the source assuming glibc x86-64 (sizeof(std::mutex) == 40), not printed by a compiler. Not read: cabin_optimizer, cabin_store, optimizer_signals, trail_*, waystone_*, ash_*, decay, wait_event (outside the slice). The dispatcher, expeditor and catalog were read only at the call sites listed under consulted. sys_views.cpp's builders of these views were not read, so whether a view filters rows of dropped relations or shows a binding correctly is not covered. Checked and found sound by reading: - AccessStats::Forget's backward-shift deletion (start past an empty slot, no wrapped run, the refilled slot re-examined) and the half-full load bound. - StatementStats' key: the kind is hashed by pointer and compared by content, consistent because every kind is static storage from StatementKindOf. StatementBinding keeps oids past n zero (Rewind zeroes, an overflow resets), so the defaulted == and the n-bounded hash agree. - ManagedStatistics' published/pending/history/lock/version state machine (Store, PublishPending, Restore, Delete, Forget, Load). I found no transition error. A pending store over an existing pending replaces it without history, which reads as intended. - sys.statistics across a crash: TakeStatisticsAtMount believes rows only on exactly one closing mark whose count equals the row count, and empties the chain at every mount before the completion checkpoint. The comment 'No closing mark' on PlanStats::ExportStatistics means only that the export does not include it; the catalog write adds it. Left out because already recorded: - A record waiting for a reader's copy of its part, Snapshot allocating under the latch, Dropped() taking 64 latches, Merge allocating under the latch, publish_latch_ on the first compile: known-gaps 'Statistics that can queue a statement (DL)'. - The BO stores and LeafStats being process-wide statics while AccessStats and IndexStats are instance-owned: known-gaps.md:2062 and :2207. - The cost of DI's 144-byte binding in the StatementStats key (copy, hash fold, 16-oid equality per statement), as inside DI's measured close. Observations below the bar, not reported as findings: - StatementStats::Snapshot's plan_source merge depends on part order: a later-seen part with an empty source keeps the earlier part's source, but an earlier-iterated part with an empty source and a later last_seen blanks another core's source, where the per-core rule is 'the shape keeps what it had'. I could not establish a reachable case where one key has a source on one core and none on another. - ManagedStatistics::forgotten_ gains one std::set node per committed DROP TABLE for the life of the run, whether or not the relation was ever gathered. Unbounded but tiny. - PlanStats::Merge walks every part's whole 1,024-entry table (about 250 bytes an entry) under the part latch each second even when part.used == 0. An early-out on used == 0 && nwanted == 0 would skip it; the latch-and-table part of this is in the DL entry. - RecordPlanStats still folds posteriors for captured shapes under join_order = fixed, where a captured order ignores the statistics. Whether that is section 3a's 'switched-off consumer' depends on DL's open reading of which consumers a switch turns off (sys views and ADVISE PLAN still read them), so it is not claimed.

Reader's notes: Everything here is [source-read]; nothing was built or run. Time read at start 1795161258, about 7 minutes used on reading. **Coverage.** Not read: free_map.cpp, page_write_fence.cpp, data_syncer.cpp and data_syncer.hpp's 5 changed lines, page_device.cpp, the body of device_page_store.hpp beyond its changed lines, the tests, and the wal side of DH (EncodePackedPageImage, redo's image arm). memory_page_device.cpp was only skimmed through the diff's added lines. **Quiet-wrong paths checked from the store's side; no defect found.** - **Eviction and re-fault:** a frame is evicted only when clean, and clean means it was copied, which sets the debt; the re-fault sets it again (InsertFrame, kFault). - **Writeback copy racing a write fetch:** the bit is set at the copy under the structure latch and read after the exclusive grant, so a fetch made before the copy and latched after it pays. - **Create racing a copy:** CreatePinned clears the debt after its latch, and the creation record heals. - **Mid-mutation copy at cores = 1:** although TryAcquirePageLatchShared returns true unarmed, ReserveFrame's inline drain runs only with t_pins_here == 0, so a held page is not copied. - **Images off through the mount:** LogOwedImage returns before clearing the bit. - **Fenced index build:** LogFullPageImage's Get drops the debt while the owner is registered, the FPI covers the page, and the next copy re-arms it. - **No-wait read:** FaultIn reads into a reserved, unpublished slot. A short preadv2 is answered as would-wait, the slot goes back through ReservedFrame, LoadingGuard erases the loading entry, and the loading_ refusal holds nothing. I found no half-read frame visible and no pin or latch leaked. **Not verified.** - Whether every creator of a logged-class page (undo, var-heap, Cabin-bound, index) logs a PAGE_INIT or whole image as its first record. If one does not, a torn first write is refused at mount, which is a refusal and not a wrong answer. - Whether StampPageLsn's unconditional SetPageLsn can lower page_lsn below a later record's when a record logged after release is stamped late. This is pre-3.4 and belongs to the "records appended after release" topic. **Observations that are not findings.** - G44 (PageRef::MarkDirty and MarkFrameDirty have only test callers) is already recorded. DB adds one fact to it: a read fetch that writes through MarkDirty would pay no image, so that hook is now also a bypass of wal.md section 10. - PageReader: several statements parked on one page each queue a read, and a fetch refused because another core is already loading the page still submits a warm read of it. Both are small. - WriteBack: an allocation failure in scratch.resize after the claim would leave the run's pins and pin_allowance_ counted, because ClaimRelease returns only the writing marks. This was already true of the pins before 3.4.

Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture at fba5529f; nothing was built or run. About 8 minutes of the 15 were used for reading, so coverage is the delta, not the whole slice. NOT READ: src/wal/analysis.cpp, recovery.cpp, writer, file_log_device.cpp beyond its one hunk, stream.hpp and manager.hpp in full, src/server/superblock.cpp, mount_recovery.cpp beyond its diff. The storage side of DB (owes_image set sites, the mount's images-off switch) was only touched at LogOwedImage. CHECKED AND FOUND SOUND (no finding): - Packed image codec: the encoder's one-check-per-word bound keeps every store inside kPackedImageMaxPayload; packed_len fits u16; the decoder zero-fills the page first, bounds every body read by packed_len, and refuses a zero mask, a stored zero, nonzero padding, and a body that is short or long. EncodeRecord zeroes the alignment padding (record.cpp:118-122), so the decoder's nonzero-padding refusal cannot fire on a record this build wrote. The record CRC is over the packed payload; the page checksum is not involved. - Redo of kind 35 unpacks into a local buffer before the page is touched and goes through the same page_lsn gate as kind 10, so an image is never laid over a newer page; kinds 10 and 35 are the only two image kinds and redo.cpp is the only reader. - TryRequestSyncNow asks the writer for flushed_lsn() only, read after the (possibly skipped) flush; a skipped flush cannot publish unflushed bytes as durable. ParkOnLog returns OK only on IsDurable(lsn). - The anchor's fold: encoded_floor_ keeps the landing from stepping back, D moves by CAS forward only, and a skipped recycle is retried only on the next advance, with the detach cumulative. A skipped recycle never detaches anything, so it cannot recycle below what is needed. - The DH gate: the spacing check runs under running_, and a spacing-refused core stamps no entry, so its turn is kept. - A null latch is owned by the try guard (latch.hpp:121-124), so the blocking Flush through FlushAttempt does not spin at one core. CONSIDERED, NOT REPORTED because documents already state them: the tried append rolling the segment itself on the task's reactor (CZ-Q19 (a)); LogFullPageImage's six call sites and BTREE_SPLIT staying verbatim (DH-Q13); a tear of the mount's own write refusing the next mount (wal.md line 7, known-gaps.md:3180). OBSERVATION, not a finding of this slice: CLAUDE.md's Access statistics row says "Page 11's bootstrap stays until DA-S3, which waits for the next superblock bump", while superblock.hpp's version 38 comment and known-gaps.md:3027 say DI-S3 removed it at superblock 38. The milestone row is stale. Finding 1's site is in src/storage (outside the wal slice proper); it is reported because it is the DB write-fetch image's per-fetch cost and was reached from the gate's LogPageImage.

Fetch answers NotFound or Corruption for an unallocated, never-written or torn page, and both end the walk rather than refuse the mount. - RetireDeleteMarks skips the unlogged chain; it is the only loop over kCatalogRoots. - DI binding columns: BindingOf clamps binding_n to 16, so the std::any_of range in RenewPatternDirectories stays in bounds. The row lookup, the mutate and the cache lookup all key on (fetch_id, binding). The ON path's check and insert stay under page 9's hold. EndPatternsNaming and the mount's dropped-relation ending both adjust the opted count. - Format events 36, 37 and 38 are refused at the superblock, and the SysPatternRow size (178) is static_asserted, so no path reads a row at the old width. - G29 fix: the pre-add bound in RowLayout::Build cannot wrap, and the slot region and bitmap are bounded by the post-loop check. - G40 fix: CreateCabin, CreateForeignKey and LookupIndexByName propagate a failed scan. The remaining `.ok()`-as-existence sites (index_ddl.cpp:992, cabin_ddl.cpp:208) only gate advisory warnings. - G27 fix: CheckNameFits is applied to table, column (dispatcher 6125), index, namespace and both renames. SEEN, NOT REPORTED: - Catalog::FindIndexByName has no engine caller; known-gaps.md:3245 already records it. - Catalog::RecountOptedPatterns is reached only from tests and sim/instance.cpp. - kCatalogPagePatternDefs (well_known.hpp:371) is an unreferenced constant kept for the reserved page 10, while DA-S3 retired page 11 by deleting its constant. - catalog.cpp ~2733: the comment says a cabin scan failure "is not fatal to opening the relation" and the code returns the status (pre-3.4 prose). - expeditor.cpp:1029-1030 still names "the access statistic" among the unlogged state a mount discards, which DA made memory-only (outside this slice).

Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] at fba5529f in the audit worktree; nothing was built or run, and the struct sizes are arithmetic, not compiler output. Clock read at start was 1795161720; I stopped at about 1795162130. Decision for the reader: the three findings are candidate mechanisms for the open cores-4-below-cores-2 question, not attributions. None is measured, and the first two are the only ones that apply to a read-only workload that takes no latch. Not reported, because the documents already carry them: - The frame table's one `frames_latch_` (three holds per fetch/unpin) is the premise of `workorder-cw-optimistic-page-read.md`. - The Cabin controller collector latch on every SELECT, the Cabin hit/miss three-latch count and the per-core statistics parts' `std::mutex` are in known-gaps.md (the DL section, around lines 3044-3072). - The administrator's plan table probe is in known-gaps.md around 2494-2556. - The whole catalog memo dropping on any schema word move, a root move included, is at known-gaps.md:1476. - The window latch on `LookupCommit` was measured (known-gaps.md:1390). - The WAL segment roll syncing under the stream latch is at known-gaps.md:447 and 1173. - A `SHOW CABIN_OPTIMIZER` stalling behind the controller's tick is at known-gaps.md:609. Observations that are not findings: - `WalStream::AppendLocked` (src/wal/stream.cpp:488) runs `EncodeRecord`'s memcpy and its CRC32C over the whole record (src/wal/record.cpp:116-129) under the instance's stream latch. DB's image records make that section up to about 8 KiB long, where a row record is tens of bytes; DH packs the image before the latch (src/wal/manager.cpp:465-478), but the copy and CRC stay under it. `workorder-al-m0-single-wal.md`:592-596 records that the reserve/copy/publish split was tried and abandoned, so I treat it as a recorded design decision. What is new since then is only the record size. - The lock table's 64 partition latches are 64 consecutive `make_unique<Latch>` allocations (lock_table.cpp:74-79) with no line alignment, so neighbouring partitions' mutex words can share a line. Not verified against the allocator. - In `InstanceVisibility`, `cores_with_unresolved_`, `open_ddl_`, `ddl_generation_`, `slots_in_use_` and `window_latch_storage_` are declared contiguously (instance_visibility.hpp:674-687). By layout arithmetic, the mutex word every commit locks shares a line with `slots_in_use_`, which every mint reads twice. This depends on the object's alignment, so it is left out of the findings. - No latch-order inversion or hold-across-park was found in what I read. `CapturedPlans` takes partition then queue consistently. The assertion enforcer takes chain latch then registry latch, as its header declares. `CancelRegistry` is touched per connection, not per statement. `WakerTable::Kick` is reached only when a waiter exists. Not read: the Cabin store's bodies, `OptimizerSurface`, `wal::CheckpointGate`, `plan_stats.cpp`, `managed_statistics`, the statement epoch, and nearly all of `device_page_store.cpp` and `command_dispatcher.cpp`. This slice was breadth-first and those structures were only grepped for latch sites.

Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] on worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture at fba5529f; nothing was built or run. Breadth slice: neither file was read end to end. CENSUS (script over the two files). One class, CommandDispatcher: 175 member definitions in command_dispatcher.cpp (15,675 lines, 71 includes) plus 34 more in sys_views.cpp (1,905 lines) - every sys view is a dispatcher member. About 111 data members in the header, 12 of them `*_for_test_` std::function seams, 7 friend classes (SelectRun, SuspensionHold, BuildRun, IndexBuildRun, DeclarationBuildRun, GatherRun, DeclarationBuildRunOf). The header is included by 11 src/include files and 89 test/sim/fuzz files. 3.4.* growth (v3.4.0..HEAD): cpp +5,995/-1,651 over both files (cpp diff 6,983 lines, hpp 663); since the F audit read it at ce5347e6: +4,214/-1,273. Responsibilities in the one class: statement classification and role gate (DispatchInner 323 lines, RequiredRole), statement staging and re-run policy (StageStatement 132, StructuralRerunDue, BeginMissRerun), seven coroutine drivers (DispatchAsync 208, AwaitWriteBlock 253, AwaitRelationLock 147, AwaitStatementWaits, AwaitPageRead, CompleteSelect, CompleteBuild), transaction control (HandleBegin 116, CommitLocal 83, RollbackLocal, BeginWrite, EndWrite 117, deferred strict commits), SELECT (SelectBody 382, SelectRun class, RunAggregated, RunAnalyze 136), the three row walks (UpdateParsed 747, DeleteInner 293, RunLockingRead 179, WriteWalk class 352, RunWalkWrite, WithReturning 153), INSERT (InsertOneRow 501, InsertParsed 266, InsertOnConflict 248, LogInsert), FK checks (CheckForeignKeyOnWrite 111, CheckNoChildrenBeforeDelete), lock borrows (BorrowChain 120, BorrowOrWait), DDL (HandleCreateTableSql 273, HandleIndex 236 + IndexBuildRun, HandleDropTable 217, HandleAlter 140 + ADD/DROP COLUMN, namespace, assertion, transition, Cabin), the DDL scope/owed-drop bookkeeping, PURGE (204) and PURGE DROPPED COLUMNS (185), Waystone opt-in (323), plan statements (CREATE/ALTER/DROP/ADVISE PLAN, SET JOIN_ORDER, about 650 lines), statistics statements (HandleStatistics 267 + GatherRun), backup verbs, sys views and SHOW (HandleCatalogView 279, HandleShow 118), sys.meta rendering (MetaSections 614), statistics recording (access shapes, index use, plan stats, optimizer signals, Cabin witness NoteCabinWrite 152), and the query layer host (LayerHostImpl). Coroutine drivers and what each drags: DispatchAsync/AwaitStatementWaits carry `line`, `Session*`, `DispatchOutcome*` and one shared `statement_deadline_ns`; a suspended SELECT is a SelectRun (pooled SelectScratch, its own binding, view, read borrow, epoch hold via SuspensionHold); a suspended build is a BuildRun (WriteScope/DdlScope or DdlBuildLock, slice policy, drain wake); a parked mid-walk write is Session::ParkedWrite (txn, snapshot, cursor, trail mark, context, subquery answers, binding). Checked and found defended (not findings): BuildRun's tail re-enters StageStatement + StampStatement (cpp:3184-3189) so `effective_durability_` is re-stamped before AwaitDdlDurability; the query layer's walks and a write's subquery run inline (RunToEnd), so LayerHostImpl's pointer into `statement_binding_` is not live across a suspension; RunAggregated (cpp:9775) runs exec::Execute to the end with no yield, so the single shared `aggregator_` is not clobbered today. Already recorded and therefore left out: `last_used` stamping another statement's head after a park (known-gaps.md:2169), the page wait sharing the lock fault net's deadline (known-gaps.md:1048), F26/F30 (WriteWalk, RunWalkWrite applied at 3f8c499c). Not examined: InsertOneRow/InsertOnConflict internals, the DDL handlers' bodies, HandleWaystone beyond its durability park, SelectBody beyond its compile head, kwp_session.cpp's use of the dispatcher. No quiet-wrong or logic-defect finding survived in this slice within the budget; that is not evidence of absence.

Slice delta since 0b98bc4f: only index_tree/index_page, btree, index_ddl, index_maintain, index_key and four assertion_* files changed; fk_check, assertion_catalog, clean_dropped, purge_key, transition*, btree_page.cpp have no diff and were not re-read. No DI change lands in this slice's files. Checked and found sound (no finding): - CV forward ordered index run: re-seek by key from the last entry read, `from` moved only from a leaf holding an entry at or past it, hop right inside a pass under the leaf hold; a divide between seek and fetch, between two leaves of a pass, or across a yield neither skips nor repeats an entry. Tie order is entry order (key, tuple key, covered), ascending tuple key. - CV reverse ordered index run: IndexFetchLeafAtOrBelow right-walks under the held leaf; a non-leftmost leaf's first entry equals its separator (no entry removed), so the returned leaf always holds an entry at or below the bound; PrecedingSortKey borrow is exact; end-by-id only fires on the leftmost leaf; ties come out by descending tuple key as IX8c says. The decline's sort of (key bytes, tuple key) pairs and its reverse match. - CV reverse relation walk (BtreeFetchLeafAtOrBelow): bound = min_key - 1 with immutable min_key; a split between steps is covered by the right-walk. Its treatment of an unreadable slot (`continue`) matches the forward walk's AcceptTupleAt (TryReadTupleIn), so it is not a new swallowed Status. - kIndexFlagBuilding: compile skips a building index at all three choice sites (step_compiler.cpp:1327, 1417, 1568) and schema.hpp:556; LiveIndexOf does not re-check the flag at run time, but the flag only ever moves building -> ready under one oid (REINDEX and a re-create take a new oid), so a chain cannot name a building index. - DF-S3 index usage counters are folded once per statement into a per-core part through a cached pointer (RecordIndexUse), not per entry on a shared line; DF-S4 LeafStats counts once per division into a per-core part. - G24/G26/G35/G36/G37/G19/G25 fixes read as intended; ReserveOne's TakeBackLast runs under the chain latch that serialises the tail. Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] at fba5529f in the worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing was built or run. Clock: started 1795162194, finished reading at about 1795162754. Not read: include/kds/server/stop_signal.hpp. expeditor.cpp 1-743 (Config and ApplyFile) was read once, without tracing each key to its consumer. tcp_server.cpp, kwp_session.cpp and command_dispatcher.cpp were consulted only at the ranges listed, so the streamed reply (CE) and the park arming (DE) were checked only where they meet session.hpp. Checked and found sound, so not reported: - A session move (TcpServer::MoveConnection / AdoptMoved) leaves no per-core state behind. The map node travels, the stream is re-armed by SubmitStatement on the new core, the wait statistics registry and the cancel registry are process-wide, and a failed or expired session is never a head. - A socket's fd is closed only when its map entry is erased, so a moved or accepted fd cannot collide with a lingering closing entry. - A cancel that races completion refuses the next frame. That is protocol.md section 10's stated contract. - BeginMissRerun refuses a rerun once a row has left (CE-R4). - Frames destroyed at teardown after their Session is gone: WaitScope's destructor returns for a task that is not running, and SelectRun and DeclarationBuildRunOf reach only the dispatcher. - A statement parked on a peer at STOP stays in that peer's shutdown checkpoint's active table, and the anchor fold keeps that CHECKPOINT_BEGIN in the replay range. - PageReclaimer's walk, plan and free order, the regate, and the re-drive after an index job is dropped mid-free. Observations that are not findings: - A refused reclaim job keeps ReclaimCounters::pending above zero until the next mount, so the 50 ms tick runs a full Step (inbox mutex, an unordered_set built in DropIndexJobsOfRelationJobs) for the rest of the run. Small. - TcpServer::DrainFrames bumps the source core's heads and active_now for a statement that then moves and runs on the destination, which counts neither. This skews the balancer's share term slightly. - core_runtime.hpp:149 still says the optimizer surface carries "the relayout mode", which BZ deleted. - rules.md line 46 and core_load.hpp:20 call the load slot "one cache-line slot"; it is 72 bytes padded to 128 (finding 1).

Census, one line a store (key / bound and full policy / drop or rename handling / ownership): - StatementStats: (fetch_id, kind, StatementBinding) / 4,096 a core, new shape dropped and counted / never told of a drop / process-static per-core parts. - RelationStats, LeafStats (OidParts): oid / 4,096 and 8,192 a core, dropped and counted / never told / process-static. IndexStats: same template, instance-owned. - AccessStats: (kind, rel oid, column mask) / flat table, half load, dropped and counted / told only by ADD COLUMN's position reuse / instance-owned per-core parts. - RefusalStats, TxnStats: fixed arrays, process-static, no latch. - PlanStats: per-core flat table of 1,024 PlanKeys (half load, dropped and counted), master of 16,384 (least-recently-seen evicted), priors by oid (erased lazily by the reseed cursor) / instance. - ManagedStatistics: oid / unbounded std::map plus the forgotten_ set / Forget at a committed drop / one latch. - PlanMemo: (PlanKey, arg_hash, statistics generation, catalog word, catalog version, two limits) / 512 sets x 2 ways LRU / self-invalidating by key / per core, no latch. Read through; no defect found. - CapturedPlans: (fetch_id, written-order oids) / 65,536, new shape refused and run as written / ForgetRelation at a committed drop / 64 latched partitions. Read through, including the persisting step; no defect found. - AdminPlans: CapturedKey / ForgetRelation at a committed drop. PlanAdvices: CapturedKey / 1,024, oldest evicted by scan / never told. - OptimizerSignals: (pattern, rel oid, col pos) / 4,096, coldest evicted by scan / never told / one instance latch. - TrailRecorder sightings: (InstanceKey, opted row oid) / 4,096, whole table cleared / per core. CabinStore sightings: same clear-all rule, per partition. - CatalogCache: name and oid maps, opted patterns by fetch id then binding / unbounded, dropped whole on any schema-word move / per core. - KWP statement and portal handles: SQL text only, 64 each per session; nothing compiled is kept, so no stale handle state across DDL. Already recorded and therefore not reported: dropped relations' rows staying in the statistics parts and counting toward their bounds (known-gaps 2231, 2279, 3037; DI-Q7 (c)); the collector's instance latch, its 4,096-entry eviction scan and its feed with the controller off (known-gaps 2268-2281 and the DL section at 3040); process-static stores shared by two instances in one process (known-gaps 2062, sys-views.md 615); the whole catalog memo dropping on a schema-word move (known-gaps 1476); a block with an unbound `?` searched at every compile under `planned` (CY work order line 344). DI's remainder: I found no store left whose key lets two objects share an entry after a drop and recreate or a rename, beyond what DI's own known-gaps entries state (a candidate is a column position not a column; PatternWaits keyed by the pattern alone, read by nothing that decides). Not read: src/stats/cabin_optimizer.cpp, trail_store.cpp, waystone_dir.cpp, waystone_page.cpp, the wait-statistics and ASH code, most of cabin_store.cpp and plan_stats.cpp, and src/exec/admin_plans.cpp beyond ForgetRelation. One lead looked at and dropped: DROP TABLE forgets its Cabins at the statement (command_dispatcher.cpp:5007), not at the commit, so a rolled-back drop inside BEGIN loses the sets. Checked and found sound, so not reported: - set_expr's typer and evaluator: decimal `+ - * / %` are checked in int128 and judged against the node's own precision; CAST and ROUND rescale with a checked multiply; a literal with more fraction digits than the scale is refused, not rounded; `NULL / 0` is NULL. - CASE, COALESCE, NULLIF, GREATEST and LEAST: NULL handling matches types.md section 3.2b. - DISTINCT, GROUP BY and set-operation keys: `char` values are NUL-trimmed at decode, so there is no padding mismatch; a uint64 above INT64_MAX keeps a distinct key in both encoders. - Outer joins: nullable-side marking, USING's hidden partner, NULL join keys and RIGHT/FULL null extension. A WHERE term is pushed only to a non-nullable, non-derived source, as BU-Q7 records. - INTERSECT ALL and EXCEPT ALL multiplicities, and `IN (subquery)` three-valued logic. - `sort_max_rows` refuses and never truncates, in `Runner::Cap` and in `LayerWalkSink::Emit`. Left out because already recorded: - The layer's per-stage row copies, the cached subquery result returned by value per outer row, and the linear `IN (subquery)` probe (known-gaps.md, "The query layer and the DML forms (BU)"). - An aggregate over an enclosing query's column, and a lone NULL typed as varchar (same section). - `Aggregator::Merge` is referenced only by tests/aggregate_test.cpp, but aggregate.md's AG-M declares it a contract, so it is not reported as dead code. The task asked about duplication between set_expr's evaluator and the layer's: there is none. The layer evaluates through `EvaluateQueryExpression`. The duplication that does exist is with aggregate.cpp's fold (finding 4). Not read: aggregate.hpp, functions.hpp, type_literals.hpp and budget.hpp beyond the cited lines; `LikeMatch`, `CoerceLiteralToColumn` and `FormatValue` bodies in row_codec.cpp; how the native compile resolves an alias-qualified pushed condition inside `WalkForLayer`.

### `net-wire` - read, 4 findings

Read in full: src/server/tcp_server.cpp; src/server/kwp_session.cpp; src/server/kwp_load_server.cpp; src/server/auth.cpp; src/server/tls_channel.cpp; src/server/config_file.cpp; src/server/main.cpp; src/server/connection_handoff.cpp; src/wire/frame_codec.cpp; src/wire/handshake.cpp; src/wire/kwp_types.cpp; src/wire/row_codec.cpp; include/kds/wire/kwp_types.hpp

Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] at fba5529f in the worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing was built or run. The audit took about 6 of the 15 minutes. NOT READ: src/server/scram.cpp, src/wire/error_registry.cpp, include/kds/wire/{error_registry,handshake,row_codec}.hpp, and most of include/kds/server/{tcp_server,kwp_session,kwp_load_server,auth,tls_channel}.hpp beyond the ranges listed in consulted. 3.4.* delta: `git log v3.4.0..HEAD` over the slice shows only small touches (DE-S1, DF-S2, CS, the F3-F25 audit fixes, CJ wait scopes). Every finding below is in code that predates v3.4.0; I found no inefficiency that the 3.4.* work itself added to this slice. Checked and found sound (no finding): - Frame length arithmetic in FrameDecoder (kMinFrameLength and kMaxFrame checked at both Feed and PopFrame). - DecodeRowBatch and DecodeBindParams bounds (size_t arithmetic, no wrap). - The load stream's shape digest and NULL tuple key (BI15, BI16): ValueOf, DescribedShape, the length check before every decode. - The SO_REUSEPORT fallback handoff: publish under the latch then kick; the destructor closes untaken sockets. - MoveConnection ordering: unregister, extract, offer; the cancel registry is keyed by session id. - Portal lifetime: no frame is processed while a statement is in flight (the DrainFrames loop is gated on !in_flight) and the idle sweep skips the running portal, so a sink cannot be freed under a running statement. - BN-R7's one-write reply and CE's resume_when predicate. - Partial writes in FlushOutbox (EAGAIN keeps the tail; write interest is synced). Observations that are not findings: (a) Because of that same in_flight gate, KwpSession::OnStatementComplete's "portal was closed while its statement ran - a client may pipeline C_CLOSE behind C_EXECUTE" branch (src/server/kwp_session.cpp:728-735) and the matching comment at src/server/tcp_server.cpp:665-667 describe an interleaving TcpServer cannot produce. A pipelined C_CLOSE is handled after completion. The branch is defensive; I did not check whether a unit test reaches it by driving KwpSession directly. (b) The send side never checks kMaxFrame (EncodeFrame, WireResultSink::Seal). A single row wider than 16 MiB, or more than 65535 ANALYZE lines in DeliverTextLines, would leave as a frame the peer's decoder refuses. I found no value-size ceiling by grep and did not establish that such a row is reachable, so it is not reported as a finding. (c) PayloadWriter::Str and EncodeRowDescription write a u16 length and then all the bytes. A name over 65535 bytes would desynchronise the payload. Every current caller passes a bounded name as far as I read; the query layer's column labels were not checked. (d) While a statement is in flight the reactor keeps reading the socket into FrameDecoder::buffer_ with no bound (an authenticated peer only), which is what makes finding 4's front-erase cost reachable.
