Not read end to end: parser.cpp 1620-1856 and 1857-2500 (column definition, CREATE TABLE, cabin, index, namespace, assertion, transition - read with comments stripped or by grep only), 2830-2950, 3760-3836 (the CREATE/DROP dispatch in Parse()). Headers ast.hpp, parser.hpp, lexer.hpp, token.hpp and most of fingerprint.hpp were not read; ast.hpp was only mined for field names. CX's plan statements (CREATE/ALTER/DROP PLAN, ADVISE PLAN) and WAYSTONE ON are not parsed in src/parser at all (no production in parser.cpp), so they are outside this slice. Checked and found sound (not findings): (1) dead AST fields - a script compared every ast.hpp member name against member accesses outside the parser directories; only TypeSpec::arg_byte_offset and AssertionStmt::bound_byte_offset have no reader outside the parser, and both are read inside it or by tests. (2) Clauses parsed and dropped - InsertStmt::value_exprs, ColumnDef REFERENCES / CABIN on ALTER ADD COLUMN (refused at command_dispatcher.cpp:4693-4700), Query::all, agg_distinct, at_most_one, shares_parent_names, FromJoin::comma (query_layer.cpp:701-712 refuses RIGHT/FULL after a comma rather than null-extending wrongly) all have consumers. (3) DC's ParseLockClause refuses every other strength and is called from both grammars, with the query grammar passing nullptr. (4) Boolean precedence in both WHERE grammars (OR < AND < NOT) and the query block's top-level AND/OR split read correctly. (5) fetch_id's select-list window: the only oddity seen is that an alias spelled `from` (`SELECT a AS from FROM t`) closes the window one token early; no collision between two valid statements was constructed. Observations below the bar: three separate ASCII case-insensitive compares exist (fingerprint.cpp:115 IsWord, lexer.cpp:36 IEquals, parser.cpp:67 IEquals); the parser's uses std::tolower, which the lexer's own comment (lexer.cpp:28-31) rules out as locale-dependent - harmless today because nothing calls setlocale (catalog/schema.cpp:66 says so). `IS [NOT] NULL` is parsed at three sites (parser.cpp:601, 1351, and HAVING's inline copy near 2880) and ORDER BY/LIMIT/OFFSET at two (2970 native, 4386 query), a consequence of the two-grammar design (parser-v2.md I19). Fnv1a::Field's length prefix is 16 bits (fingerprint.cpp:46-50), so a literal longer than 65,535 bytes hashes with a truncated length; only arg_hash is affected and Waystone is advisory. UPDATE ... FROM / DELETE ... USING wrap a WHERE parsed at depth 0 into an EXISTS (parser.cpp:3493), so its subqueries sit one level deeper than CheckSubqueryDepth counted. A top-level `NOT IN (v, ...)` list flattens into ordinary conjuncts with no cap, while an IN list is capped at 4,096 (parser.cpp:1485-1491).

The ones on answer paths (index_ddl.cpp:875 WriteAnchorRoot, cabin_ddl.cpp:237 DeleteDeclaration, cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:323/340 DropCabin followed by cabins_.Forget) each carry a comment arguing why the failure is tolerable; I did not verify cabin_optimizer_exec's case (a DropCabin that fails leaves a sys.cabins row whose store set was forgotten) beyond reading the site. command_dispatcher.cpp:1412 and 5120 drop Abort's Status; that is the txn slice's and was not traced. - Not read: src/parser/parser.cpp refusals beyond the scripted listing (the sweep shows nearly all carry a byte; 3233 'expected ON after JOIN' and 1762 do not), tests/, sim/, tools/, and the KWP session's translation of DispatchOutcome beyond OutcomeStatus. - Process note: the first attempt to write my sweep script was refused by the worktree guard, and a follow-up `sed -i` then targeted a pre-existing file of the same name (nobyte.py) in the shared scratchpad that another reader may own; the pattern matched nothing (the following grep printed no line), so the file should be unchanged. Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] of worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture at fba5529f; nothing was built or run. About 6 of the 15 minutes were used. METHOD. Four throwaway Python scripts (no ctags or rg on the host): - sweep.py: comment-stripped identifier census over src, include, tests, sim, fuzz, tools, scripts, bench, example; candidates are identifiers declared in the slice (src/ and include/kds/ for storage, wal, txn, catalog, stats, sched, base). - sweep2.py: slice headers with no production includer other than their own .cpp. - sweep3.py: public functions whose only production occurrences are their declaration and definition, sized by body. - sweep4.py: includes whose declared names the includer never uses. Each reported candidate was then grepped and read. LIMITS OF THE METHOD. - The census is by bare identifier, so a dead method that shares a name with a live one (Get, Insert, size) is invisible. - Struct fields written and never read were caught only when their name is unique. - The inner of two nested dead layers is not caught. - Nothing in src/exec, src/server, src/parser or src/wire was swept (not this slice). - All five findings are pre-3.4 code. SMALLER CANDIDATES, VERIFIED BY GREP BUT NOT RAISED AS FINDINGS. - Write-only counters outside the Cabin store (one increment and one declaration in production, read by tests only): - WalStats::records_appended and bytes_appended (src/wal/manager.cpp:446-447, bumped on every append; the struct's comment at include/kds/wal/manager.hpp:124 calls them "product surface"), and relaxed_commits (manager.cpp:508). - TrailRecorder::Stats trails_written, skipped_overflow, sighting_table_clears (src/stats/trail_recorder.cpp:126, 87, 25). - CatalogCache::Stats::pattern_fills (src/catalog/catalog_cache.cpp:122). - RedoStats::page_images and pages_created (src/wal/redo.cpp:461, 421). - RecoveryResult::undo_ran (src/wal/recovery.cpp:108). - Constants defined and used nowhere in the tree: kAshCrossCore (include/kds/stats/ash_record.hpp:49), kCabinStatusDemoted (include/kds/catalog/rows.hpp:965), kSegmentReservedOffset (include/kds/wal/record.hpp:403). kCatalogPagePatternDefs (include/kds/catalog/well_known.hpp:371) is likewise unused, but its header states the reservation. - Test-only public functions of a few lines each: BumpPageEpoch (src/storage/page_header.cpp:141; production moves the epoch with SetPageEpoch in btree.cpp:712 and 875), DevicePageStore::IsPinnedClass (device_page_store.cpp:3094), Scheduler::SubmitAt (scheduler.cpp:106), TxnManager::ActiveCount and ReadHorizon (manager.cpp:743, 799), Catalog::FindIndexByName (catalog.cpp:4312), DecodeCheckpointEnd, DecodeAssertDrop and EncodeFullPageImage (payload.cpp:734, 906, 478), SysObjectRegistry::GetByOid, WaitContext() (src/sched/wait_state.cpp:120, no caller at all). - include/kds/txn/trx_id.hpp:9 includes kds/catalog/well_known.hpp (562 lines) and uses no CamelCase or k-constant name it declares, by script only; it also includes kds/server/superblock.hpp, a txn-to-server include. ALREADY RECORDED, LEFT OUT. - G47: BufferPool / page_mgr, 720 lines, still in the library. - G44: PinnedGet aliases. - G50: EncodeAssertCommit. - G6, G9, G10. - GrowPatternDirectory: known-gaps.md:527 and its own header. - kAlloc, kFree, kTxnPrepare, kPageHandoff: reserved kinds, stated in CLAUDE.md. - The latch cost of Cabin hit and miss counting: known-gaps.md:3062, owned by DL. Reader's notes: All claims are [source-read] in worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture at fba5529f (v3.4.17); nothing built or run. Wall time used: about 7 of the 15 minutes. METHOD. Two throwaway scripts under the scratchpad (clone.py, clone2.py) over the 378 files of src/ and include/kds/ (142,515 lines): (a) comment- and whitespace-stripped lines, 8-line windows, exact hash; (b) identifiers replaced by a placeholder, 8-10 line windows, filtered to windows with control flow and calls, merged into maximal runs. Then targeted greps for the families the task named, and reading of the top candidates. HEADLINE. The tree is not clone-heavy at the block level: the exact pass finds only about 290 cloned normalised lines in 14 files, and the longest identifier-normalised run is 12 lines. The duplication that exists is of small primitives and of "protocol" blocks that differ in names and comments, which a window hash underrates; the five findings are those families, ranked by my estimate of duplicated lines (130, 110, 105, 100, 90). None is a 3.4.* regression in the main: every family is older, and 3.4 work added one or two sites to each (ash_record.hpp's PutLe/GetLe by CJ; access_stats, statement_stats, optimizer_signals and captured_plans each a hash mixer by DA-S1, DI-S1, DI-S2, CX-S0). SIXTH FAMILY, NOT GIVEN A FINDING SLOT (about 60 lines): hash mixers. splitmix64's finalizer is written out at src/txn/lock_table.cpp:16, src/stats/plan_stats.cpp:21, src/storage/memory_page_device.cpp:320, src/exec/captured_plans.cpp:14, src/server/tcp_server.cpp:590, and inlined at src/stats/access_stats.cpp:21, src/stats/optimizer_signals.cpp:47, src/stats/statement_stats.cpp:55; FNV-1a 64 at src/parser/fingerprint.cpp:16, src/exec/cabin_expr.cpp:101, include/kds/catalog/schema.hpp:258 and src/exec/bound_cabin.cpp:123. A copy has drifted: bound_cabin.cpp:123 says "FNV-1a" and uses the basis 1469598103934665603, which is the real basis 14695981039346656037 with its last digit dropped (since 8cd10c8e, AST04). Harmless - HashGroupKey only keys the in-memory groups_by_hash_ map and collisions are confirmed against the key - but the comment misstates the code. One Mix() and one Fnv1a() in include/kds/base/ would be the copy to keep. CHECKED AND DROPPED. (1) Per-core statistics stores: relation, leaf and index stats already share OidParts, the two logs share PerCoreRing; StatementStats, RefusalStats, TxnStats and AccessStats repeat only the parts()/Dropped()/ResetForTest frame (about 10 lines each) around different tables. (2) Histogram buckets: one WaitBucketOf; the lower-bound expression `k == 0 ? 0 : 1 << k` is written three times in sys_views.cpp (1475, 1636, 1661) - three lines. (3) Status-to-wire mapping is one place (wire/error_registry.cpp CategoryOf / ErrorFromStatus). (4) Catalog row and WAL payload codecs: each row's field list is written three times (offsets, Encode, Decode; rows.cpp 18 functions) - the largest raw line count the identifier-normalised pass reports, but it is the hand-kept codec rules.md asks for, not a clone; G13 was the one real parallel codec. (5) btree.cpp vs index_tree.cpp parent securing is G61 (refuted), lock_table entry lookup is G11, catalog in-place rewrites are G64, UPDATE/DELETE walks are F26/F30: not repeated. (6) Declaration catalog (assertion_catalog.cpp / transition_catalog.cpp) is already folded onto declaration_relation. (7) The spill-release loop after a keyless retire is written twice, src/exec/clean_dropped.cpp:103-121 and src/exec/purge_key.cpp:40-58 (about 18 lines, messages differ by one word) - real, below the five. (8) CREATE PLAN and ADVISE PLAN repeat a 10-line by-example parse (command_dispatcher.cpp:14567-14579, 14860-14871) - real, small. (9) sys_views.cpp's high self-similarity is column-list declarations. NOT REACHED: tests/, sim/, fuzz/, tools/ (the sweep was src and include only, as the slice says); lock-wait-then-recheck loops in command_dispatcher.cpp were not re-swept because F26-F32 folded that file's copies; parser.cpp's two 11-12 line runs (555-583, 3445-3460 vs 3661-3678) were listed by the script and not read.

Suspicions checked and found sound:

- PURGE DROPPED COLUMNS, zeroed cells read by an idle old view at its next statement: sound. The next statement's head revalidates the memo (StatementEpochScope, src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:1187) and the layout is always built unfiltered (InitTableAccess, src/catalog/catalog.cpp:2638; columns scan with a null view at 2582), so the old view binds the column as dropped and never interprets the cell. A row is cleaned only when its head writer is resolved for every reader (src/exec/clean_dropped.cpp:75, predicate at command_dispatcher.cpp:15266), and the READ ONLY lease is in that bound (LocalSnapshotBound, src/txn/manager.cpp:664-672)
- ADD COLUMN reusing a clean slot (generation + 1) read by an old view through an undo image: sound. The slot is flagged clean only on a pass that left no dirty head (command_dispatcher.cpp:15314-15360). The only UPDATE encoder is EncodeAssigned (command_dispatcher.cpp:12269), which copies dropped cells, so no writer produces a clean head over a dirty reachable before-image; a head newer than a live lease is kUnresolved and blocks the flag (15283-15291)
- Online index build finishing, new index chosen by a reader whose view predates versions: sound. IndexStagedRow walks each row's undo chain and appends every version until the superseding writer is ResolvedForEveryReader (src/exec/index_ddl.cpp:305-308, 355-398); a failed append fails the build (399). The same walk serves the X build and REINDEX (docs/spec/index.md:1019-1022)
- Ordered index walk (CV) under an old view, row emitted at a stale entry's position: sound. A row is emitted only from the entry whose key equals its visible version's (CV-R2, src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1799-1807), plus the seen set
- Read path stopping early on a unique index (an old view could hold two rows of one key from before a later CREATE UNIQUE INDEX): no such early stop exists; the only 'unique' uses in src/exec/step_vm.cpp and step_compiler.cpp are std::unique and comments
- DROP TABLE page reclaim against an idle old view: sound. The drop retypes the sys.objects row in place under the dropper's stamp (src/catalog/catalog.cpp:2416, OverwriteForDdl 375-403), so a filtered read under the old view skips it (ScanAll, catalog.cpp:127) and an unfiltered read does not match kTypeTable (1581); the name never resolves to the dropped oid, and the reclaim's epoch gate (src/server/page_reclaim.cpp:139-147) is only needed for statements in flight
- DROP INDEX / REINDEX tree reclaim against an old view that still 'sees' the delete-marked sys.indexes row: sound. No read path lists indexes under a view; the only ListIndexes(view) caller is the sys.indexes view (src/server/sys_views.cpp:650). A statement's index set comes from InitTableAccess's unfiltered ListIndexes() (src/catalog/catalog.cpp:2798), rebuilt after the word move
- K1 PURGE horizon against a READ ONLY lease: sound. A delete-marked slot is a target only when its deleter is ResolvedForEveryReader (command_dispatcher.cpp:15127); pinned by tests/read_only_txn_test.cpp:138 (ItsLeaseHoldsTheHorizonAgainstAPurgeOnAnotherCore)
- Undo recycling under a reader with no transaction id: sound. Undo pages recycle below the commit-order floor (src/txn/undo_log.cpp:66-79), and Reclaim pins the floor at any writer whose commit is above min(HorizonLsn, PendingCommitBound) (src/txn/instance_visibility.cpp:373-384); the lease feeds HorizonLsn through PublishCoreBounds (src/txn/manager.cpp:643-644)
- Mint-to-register window of BEGIN READ ONLY: sound. MintReadView lowers the core's slot before it reads the ceiling (src/txn/manager.cpp:122-123) and ResolvedForEveryReader reads the bound after the commit lookup (829-832)
- FK checks run under a REPEATABLE READ transaction's old view (a parent deleted after BEGIN still visible, or a child committed after BEGIN invisible): sound. Both directions mint a fresh check view (reverse: command_dispatcher.cpp:5941; forward: CheckView at 5379), never the transaction's
- READ ONLY session moved to another core while its lease belongs to the first core's manager: sound. The placer moves a session only when !in_explicit_txn() (src/server/tcp_server.cpp:670), which is true for a READ ONLY transaction (include/kds/server/session.hpp:137, 272-286)
- Lifetime-ceiling sweep dropping the lease under a running walk: sound. A connection with a statement in flight is skipped (src/server/tcp_server.cpp:928-931)
- Suspended KWP portal outliving the READ ONLY transaction's lease and continuing a walk under a dead view: sound. A suspended portal holds sealed row batches, not a live walk (src/server/kwp_session.cpp:936-958)
- Waystone trail recorded by a newer statement replayed under an old view: sound by contract. Only keyed steps are served and visibility goes through the same AcceptTupleAt (docs/spec/waystone-concpets.md:35-43); MVCC case listed as contract item 6 (line 279). Spec read only; replay code not re-read in this pass
- Var-heap value released while an old view still names it: no deferred-release drain exists (include/kds/txn/varheap_release.hpp:14-17); releases happen on rollback, on K1 PURGE and on the dropped-column clean, each gated as above
- Catalog delete-mark sweep against an old view: gated on ResolvedForEveryReader (src/catalog/catalog.cpp:1208)

Reader's notes: No finding in this lens. All claims are [source-read]; nothing was built or run. Every reclamation or in-place rewrite I could find that an across-statement view depends on is gated on the horizon (ResolvedForEveryReader, or the commit-order floor), not on "no statement runs". The READ ONLY lease enters that bound through the same LocalSnapshotBound that a REPEATABLE READ transaction's view does. The two real statement-epoch gates (PURGE DROPPED COLUMNS and dropped-page reclaim) protect a bound layout or memo, and an idle reader has none: it revalidates at its next head and the layout is always read unfiltered. Three things I saw and did not report: 1. Catalog resolution for a held view is not stable. ViewFor (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:9015-9024) filters name resolution only while some DDL is open anywhere in the instance. A REPEATABLE READ or READ ONLY reader therefore resolves a name as of its snapshot at one statement and against the latest catalog at the next. The premise in include/kds/server/command_dispatcher.hpp:1359-1363 ("an unfiltered read is correct for every reader" when no DDL is in flight) does not hold for a view older than a committed DDL. The outcomes I could construct are a refusal that comes and goes, or the non-isolation that docs/spec/ddl-transactional.md 5a and docs/spec/alter.md AL6/AL11 already state (DROP TABLE not isolated, rename and column changes not isolated). I found no case where it returns a wrong value from a row, so I left it out; it may deserve a sentence in the spec. 2. READ COMMITTED READ ONLY re-pointing the lease at a statement head (EnsureStatementBoundary, command_dispatcher.cpp:8996-8999) under a walk still running is round 1's txn-locks-delta#3. Not repeated. 3. The Cabin controller's seeded build (src/exec/cabin_optimizer_exec.cpp:147) and the key-set and DATE-cover serves share the root cause of round 1's exec-cabin-transition#1 (a set banked after the reader's snapshot). Not repeated. Not read in code: the unique-index write check (spec only, index.md 10.2), Waystone replay (spec only), and the RENAME TABLE write path (inferred from AL6 and OverwriteForDdl's bootstrap arm).

Suspicions checked and found sound:

- (1) Unordered probe/range re-check of the visible version: the probe never trusts an entry's key - the compiler leaves every equality and bound in step.residual (IndexChoiceOver reads them, never removes: src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:1197-1264) and every resolved row goes through AcceptTupleAt's residual on the version the view sees (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1697, 3012). A stale entry resolves to a row whose visible value fails the conjunct and is dropped.
- (1) Duplicate emission from v -> v' -> v round trips or a key set's several values: one seen set per step run, shared across a key set's values (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1599, 1610-1616).
- (1) Covering probe, 'any entry passes': the tuple key is claimed only after an entry passes the covered filter, and a later passing entry un-files it from the filtered set (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1588-1598). CoveredRowSurvives answers false only on a literal predicate on this step's own covered column - a predicate with rhs literal sits on its lhs relation's step (PredicateReadyAt, src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:749-755) and outer refs are skipped (step_vm.cpp:1732); a spilled covered value is undecidable and keeps the row (1746). Nullable covered columns are refused at declaration (src/catalog/catalog.cpp:4084), so no NULL cell is misjudged by its stale bytes.
- (1) Ordered walk emits a row only at its visible version's entry: the key check runs after every conjunct and before rows_matched, sub-chains and emission, so a stale entry neither emits nor counts toward LIMIT (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:3104-3108); the emitted set is filled only on a key match (2016), so a stale entry met first does not hide the row's true position. Key columns are forced into read_columns (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:3675-3679) and decoded from the visible version before the check (step_vm.cpp:3092-3098).
- (1) Ordered walk with covered columns: CollectEntry filters each entry on its own covered bytes (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1989-1996); entries of one (key, tuple key) differing only in covered bytes sit at the same order position, and the emitted set dedups them (2012).
- (1) Forward ordered walk resume: `from` moves only from a leaf holding an entry at or past it (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1875-1879), the entry equal to `from` is skipped (1858), and a re-seek that lands left walks right (1887); entries are unique (IX4b) and never removed, so a concurrent divide neither skips nor repeats.
- (1) Ordered decline (indexes off, index dropped or rebuilt under a prepared chain): the walk collects (encoded key, tuple key) instead of emitting, sorts, reverses for DESC and resolves in that order (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:2056-2075, 2105-2110) - a walk's tuple-key order never reaches an elided ORDER BY.
- (1) ORDER BY resolution agrees between the elision and the sort: DriverOrderOf (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:1363) and section 5a (3575) both resolve written.key.column through ResolveColumn against the same scope.
- (1) Ordered walk never replaces a key set, a correlated probe, a Cabin arm, a build or a filtered walk: OrderedIndexChoiceOf requires an index step with no key_from and no key_set, or a bare kScan with no conjunct of any kind and no build (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:1401-1412); an index whose order column is also bound by equality is passed over (1426-1427).
- (1) Truncated string keys cannot order a reply: OrderPreservingKey refuses char/varchar order columns (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:1380-1382, 1413-1415); integer, date, timestamp, decimal and wide decimal encodings are sign-flipped big-endian, so memcmp order is value order (src/exec/index_key.cpp:55-59, 88, 237, 259-260).
- (2) An old view finds its version under the old key: no code path removes an index entry (no remove on IndexLeafView, include/kds/storage/index/index_page.hpp; grep for Remove/Erase/Delete entry is empty), rollback and loser undo leave the surplus, and a byte-identical re-insert is reported, not stored twice (src/storage/index/index_tree.cpp:373-381).
- (2) UPDATE's 'moved nothing, append nothing' rule cannot leave a version without an entry: the before-image is decoded whole whenever the relation has an index (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11948, 12119, 12131-12133), `previous` is that whole row (12195-12197), and any key or covered move appends the full new entry (src/exec/index_maintain.cpp:148-198).
- (2) REINDEX and the X build keep entries for superseded-but-visible versions: the backfill always appends the head version (src/exec/index_ddl.cpp:325-331) and walks the undo chain until the writer of the version just appended is resolved for every reader (356-398); ResolvedForEveryReader reads the instance horizon (live views and reader leases, READ ONLY included) and the pending-commit bound after the commit lookup (src/txn/manager.cpp:809-835). A failed append of an older version fails the build (index_ddl.cpp:388-399). Pinned by tests/index_contract_test.cpp:819, 839, 859 and tests/index_maintain_test.cpp:325.
- (2) Delete-marked rows are backfilled: BackfillLeaf stages every slot ReadTuple returns, deleted included (src/exec/index_ddl.cpp:492-511); only a retired slot (NotFound) is skipped.
- (2) A dropped or rolled-back index DDL does not leave another core writing without the index: both endings of a catalog-writing transaction move the schema word before the relation X is released (src/txn/manager.cpp:336, 579), and a writer's first covering intention re-runs on a stale memo (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:13485-13498).
- (3) Online build, rows written during the build: writers granted IX before the word move are waited out (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:3304-3376; IX holders read under the partition latch, src/txn/lock_table.cpp:447-468), a writer granted after it re-runs on a stale memo (BorrowChain 13485-13498), and the high-water key is taken after the drain (src/exec/index_ddl.cpp:894-900) with the leaf cut-off resting on min_key immutability (488).
- (3) Online build, a head writer in flight at the copy is never trusted resolved: `trusted` returns false for it (src/exec/index_ddl.cpp:311-314), the row is re-read when the walk below it fails after the writer decided (400-403, 424-433), bounded, else TxnConflict (418-423).
- (3) No read chooses a building index: IndexProbeOf (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:1327) and OrderedIndexChoiceOf (1417) skip ix.building; the backfill is complete before the flag clears (IX15 step R).
- (4) DESC ties and direction: DriverOrderOf requires one direction for every key and admits the tuple key only last and in that direction (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:1364-1370); the reverse walk reads each leaf top-down (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1947-1956), so ties leave by descending tuple key as parser-v2.md I12 states.
- (4) Reverse walk cannot end early or loop on a non-leftmost leaf: a divide's separator is the right sibling's first entry (src/storage/index/index_tree.cpp:442-451) and nothing removes an entry, so a leaf's first entry is its routing bound; `first - 1` therefore routes strictly left, and IndexFetchLeafAtOrBelow moves right under the leaf's hold past a concurrent divide (598-624). The same-leaf exit (step_vm.cpp:1934) fires only on the leftmost leaf.
- (4) Equality-prefix rule: the ordered index's keys must be a permutation of the ladder's equality prefix followed by exactly the ORDER BY columns (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:1419-1423), and its probe's eq_prefix must equal that prefix (1426).
- Latest-state writer (unique check): entries for the key are collected, each named row is read at latest state, an undecided writer answers kBusy before any key comparison, and the full key columns are compared against the decoded row, so a stale entry or a truncated-prefix collision never reports a holder (src/exec/index_maintain.cpp:339-389).

- FOR UPDATE inside a subquery, a SET-value subquery or an INSERT's source: parser passes a null target outside the outermost block (src/parser/parser.cpp:3394-3398, refusal at :3124-3130); a pinned caller is refused at command_dispatcher.cpp:10083-10086
- FOR SHARE / FOR KEY SHARE / FOR NO KEY UPDATE / OF / NOWAIT / SKIP LOCKED accepted as another strength: each refused by name at src/parser/parser.cpp:3092-3123
- Point arm returning a row without its claim: claimed.push_back only on Claim's true (command_dispatcher.cpp:12665-12668); a held row sets parked_on_row and the statement ends with the blocked verdict before the emit pass (:12686-12687)
- Key-set arm returning a row without its claim or twice: WriteKeySet stops at the first parked or declined value (command_dispatcher.cpp:11724-11737), the caller ends on parked_on_row (:12695), and a declined descent hands the walk the cursor's key so keys below it are skipped by key, not offered again (WalkBtreeLeaves :8784-8790)
- Scan arm returning a row without its claim: the visit stops on parked_on_row and the statement is refused before the emit pass (command_dispatcher.cpp:12708-12714)
- A parked and resumed walk: a locking read never resumes - every park is Refused(walk.blocked_verdict) and DispatchAsync re-runs it whole with a fresh claimed vector; ParkedOutcome is not used on this path (command_dispatcher.cpp:12687, :12695, :12714)
- A re-run sending rows twice: no Describe or Emit happens before the claim pass ends (command_dispatcher.cpp:12718-12734); an emit-pass stale-descent TxnConflict is re-run only after sink->Rewind() and never once the sink Streamed (StructuralRerunDue :1155-1168)
- Rows returned from a version other than the one claimed: the emit re-reads by key and refuses Corruption unless the row is present, not delete-marked and its writer passes CheckWriteConflict for this transaction (command_dispatcher.cpp:12744-12751); a version reached through undo is never admitted by Claim (:11637-11646)
- A row newer than the view returned: Qualify sends a head by an invisible writer to Claim, where TransactionManager::CheckWriteConflict refuses it (src/txn/manager.cpp:202-212); test cell tests/locking_read_test.cpp:654 pins the refusal and the held unit
- Delete-marked rows: a delete by a visible writer or by this transaction is kNoVersion and skipped (include/kds/txn/visibility.hpp:65-69, command_dispatcher.cpp:11541); a delete by an undecided or later writer reaches Claim and waits or is refused; the emit refuses a delete-marked page version (:12746)
- Claim taken with no page protection: the leaf is held shared from the descent or the visit through Qualify and Claim with no suspension (LocateByTupleKey kRead :12673, BtreeLookup hands the descent's own leaf out at src/storage/btree/btree.cpp:1199-1201; VisitRelation kRead :12704-12705)
- A declared window not covering a claimed row: the window is folded from the AND-list's tuple key conjuncts only, a superset of what qualifies (command_dispatcher.cpp:13617-13634); BETWEEN is not misread as equality (:8871); a one-key window stays per-row (:13632)
- A subquery under OR/NOT escaping the collection pass: AttachBoolConjunct pushes a bool tree's sub-chains onto step.sub_chains (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:502-506), so WriteKeyOf/WriteKeySetOf decline and CollectTargets runs
- Heap hidden key or dropped column leaking into *: DescribeStar skips every column that is not visible() (command_dispatcher.cpp:7020-7023) and the names come from the chain's own ProjectColumns via CompileSelectList (step_compiler.cpp:2485-2493); tests/locking_read_test.cpp:271-304 compares heap and star replies byte for byte with the plain statement
- The claim released by a later statement's give-back: the three ReleaseIf sites release by mode - S on an absent FK parent (command_dispatcher.cpp:5907-5916), the named-key door's X (:7862-7865, :8441-8444), index-key units (:13712-13714) - never a tuple U; tests/locking_read_test.cpp:486 pins a claim outliving a later failed statement
- The claim across a deferred (strict, parked) COMMIT: CommitDeferred releases nothing (src/txn/manager.cpp:346-366); the borrows go last in FinishCommitBody, after the window entry and RetireInFlight (:325-343), and Abort releases them last after the compensations (:583)
- A refusal before the claim pass failing or leaking the scope: resolve and compile refusals return before any borrow is asked (command_dispatcher.cpp:12605-12625); BeginWrite on an explicit transaction only takes the statement boundary and the trail mark (:13254-13262)
- A refusal after a claim silently keeping the transaction committable: EndWrite poisons an explicit transaction unless a blocker, a relation wait or a structural re-run stands (command_dispatcher.cpp:13934-13940), and the refusal that ends the wait poisons (:1044, RefuseParkedWrite :13307)
- View used to qualify differs from the view used for the conflict verdict: HandleSelect's SnapshotFor takes the statement boundary and BeginWrite's second call is a no-op on statement_boundary_taken_ (command_dispatcher.cpp:8991, :13216, :13256), the locking read being synchronous between the two
- Text reply misread as a failure by EndWriteWith's 'ERR ' prefix test: a text reply starts with the comma-joined column names (include/kds/server/result_sink.hpp:117-121), which cannot begin 'ERR ' followed by a space
- A dispatcher with no lock table granting vacuous claims: Claim/BorrowChain return true with locks_ == nullptr (command_dispatcher.cpp:13407), but production always wires the table (src/server/expeditor.cpp:1999); test-only configuration

Suspicions checked and found sound:

- Residual check is on the written conjunct, not a coerced copy: the set's items are read from the same coerced leaves that stay in Step::bool_residual, and every located row goes through EvaluateBoolConjuncts (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:3026-3035; src/exec/chain_frame.cpp:296-315). The 'leaf first' shortcut only short-circuits to true when a child of that same OR is true, so a stale key_set_leaf_ or conjunct index cannot admit a row (chain_frame.cpp:303-309).
- Integer literal past int64 (lexer wrap): refused at coercion for every type that reads int_val (src/exec/row_codec.cpp:682-685) and again declined as a key (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:897); the write window fold declines it too (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:8876, 13548).
- Negative or NULL tuple-key item: left out of items, stays a leaf of the residual (step_compiler.cpp:890-899, 941-946); an all-NULL/negative list yields no set and the step walks (946).
- Tuple key above 2^48-1: no masking anywhere on the descent - SearchLeaf compares the full 64-bit id (src/storage/btree/btree.cpp:911, 922) so it is NotFound; pinned by tests/key_set_test.cpp:145-146.
- String vs integer mix, decimal scale mismatch: each leaf goes through LowerComparison -> CoercePredicate, so a mistyped item refuses the statement rather than matching nothing (step_compiler.cpp:452-453, 406-407; row_codec.cpp:750-757, 836-839).
- Dedup is after coercion/encoding on all three arms: tuple key by uint64 (step_compiler.cpp:948-954), index by encoded key bytes (1286-1290; two strings sharing an IX6-truncated prefix collapse to one superset probe, filtered by the residual), Cabin by CabinKey (1520-1529).
- An index-encoder refusal of any non-NULL item declines the list for that index instead of dropping the item (step_compiler.cpp:1281), so an out-of-width integer on a narrow column is still answered by the walk/residual.
- NOT IN is an AND of != leaves, never an OR, so it is never a set and a NULL item keeps no row (src/parser/parser.cpp:1590-1606; ListConjunctOn requires kOr with kEq leaves, step_compiler.cpp:910-923). A one-item list is returned as the plain comparison (parser.cpp:1612).
- List beside OR / nested / mixed-column: all_of(names_the_column) fails for any non-kCompare child or other column (step_compiler.cpp:910-923).
- List on the second index column whose first is not an equality: the prefix loop breaks at column 0 and with_list returns nullopt (step_compiler.cpp:1200-1208). Two lists on (a, b): only the first is taken, the second stays a filter (1202). List plus range on the next column: encoded per item with the range bounds kept (1238-1264, 1296-1304).
- Row emitted twice across list values (stale index entry under another listed value): one seen set shared across all per-value walks (step_vm.cpp:1539, 1589-1601, 1610-1632); Cabin union deduped by tuple key (step_vm.cpp:2133, 2148).
- Covering filter cannot drop a row on the list: CoveredRowSurvives reads only the flat residual, never bool_residual (step_vm.cpp:1731-1752).
- Leaf packing below the tuple key cannot corrupt a key: leaf index < 2^16 by ListConjunctOn's cap (step_compiler.cpp:920) and a key wider than 48 bits is refused Corruption before the shift (step_vm.cpp:1626-1630).
- ORDER BY <tuple key> elided over a set is sound: tuple-key items ascend (step_compiler.cpp:948), index set sorts collected keys (step_vm.cpp:1650), Cabin arm is excluded by kind (step_chain.hpp:195-203). DESC + LIMIT keeps its sort over a set (step_compiler.cpp:3618-3626). The ordered index walk refuses a step carrying a set (step_compiler.cpp:1401-1411).
- Cabin 'every value observed' is asked at run, per execution, all-or-nothing, with an announced (building) set reading as unobserved (step_vm.cpp:858-870; src/stats/cabin_store.cpp:125); an unkeyable value or more values than cabin_max_values walks (step_vm.cpp:847, 860).
- No reader treats a set step as a one-row lookup: statement class (step_compiler.cpp:3649), trail record/replay (step_chain.hpp:818-819; step_vm.cpp:3147; src/exec/trail_replay.cpp:44), AcceptTupleAt has no kind-specific branch (step_vm.cpp:2733-3160 scanned).
- Write by list checks the whole WHERE on each row found: the descent's slot goes through Qualify -> MatchesWhere (src/server/command_dispatcher.cpp:11521-11574, 11790-11793); a WHERE with a subquery names no set (step_compiler.cpp:2480-2483).
- Write by list does not skip an undecided row: an undecided holder makes Claim record a park, write_at/mark_at return kParked, the loop stops with the cursor at that key and the resume re-offers it via lower_bound (command_dispatcher.cpp:11657-11661, 11716-11737, 12468, 15619).
- Resume cannot double-write or drop: cursor is a key, values ascend, a declined descent hands the rest to the walk from that key (command_dispatcher.cpp:11718-11735, 12515-12550); the statement's trail mark survives a park (command_dispatcher.cpp:10868, 13287) so a resumed statement that parks again is not re-run whole.
- Lock units equal the walk's: no window is read off a list because the fold ignores a kBoolTree condition (command_dispatcher.cpp:8871), each written row takes its row unit in Claim (11631-11635), and a bound beside the list declares the same window the walk would (13602-13634). A walk without a tuple-key bound takes no range unit either, so there is no phantom protection the list form loses (also stated in known-gaps 'Key sets (CU)').
- UPDATE whose SET moves an indexed column cannot revisit a row: a write finds rows through no index (known-gaps) and the list loop visits each distinct key once (step_compiler.cpp:950-954); the unique-key second round re-applies the same row under its held U (command_dispatcher.cpp:12450-12464).
- FOR UPDATE by list: ORDER BY/LIMIT/OFFSET refused before the run (command_dispatcher.cpp:10130-10145); claimed stays ascending across a mid-list decline because the walk continues from the cursor key (12690-12716).

- Set run inline under DE's miss refusal: a refused page ends or taints the run, the note discards it and the sink is rewound before the re-run; rows that already left turn the re-run into a refusal (command_dispatcher.cpp:1283-1293, 729-739, 7284-7286).

Reader's notes: No finding on this path at fba5529f; all claims are [source-read], nothing was built or run. Why the path holds: every arm (tuple-key set, index set, Cabin set, write by list, locking read by list) only chooses where to look. The list's conjunct stays in bool_residual in its coerced form and is evaluated on every located row, and coercion is one routine for flat and tree leaves, so a value cannot wrap or truncate into a key whose row then passes the predicate. The three duplicate-emission risks (duplicate items, stale index entries under two listed values, a row in two Cabin sets) each have a dedup at the place it is needed. One thing seen on the way and deliberately not returned: a Cabin set step inherits the Cabin serve's blindness to the reader's view (CabinStore::Find takes no snapshot, step_vm.cpp:861 and 950), so `v IN ('a','c')` inside a BEGIN READ ONLY transaction can lose rows from a set banked after its view. That is round 1's exec-cabin-transition#1 (ALREADY-FOUND.md line 82), same root cause, so it is left out; whoever fixes it should cover ServeAllOrWalk (step_vm.cpp:844-874) as well as the single-value serve. I did not check whether a sliced autocommit SELECT whose inner step is a Cabin probe is a second route to the same defect. Not traced, so not cleared: char(N) padding equality between EncodeIndexKeyColumn/MakeCabinKey and CompareValues (the list uses the same encoders as the single-value arm, so any defect there would not be CU's); WalkBtreeLeaves' cursor handling beyond its documented contract; the query layer's (I19) own pushdown of a list into its single-relation walks; RETURNING row collection for a resumed list write; the sim corpus (only tests/key_set_test.cpp was checked for pins).

Suspicions checked and found sound:

- Hunt 1, key collision cannot change a result: a captured, memoised or administrator's order is only a permutation of written positions that is recompiled for THIS statement through CompileBound over a permuted scope (src/exec/step_compiler.cpp:2621-2644); nothing of another statement's chain, literal or page id is reused. The key carries n and every oid in written order (step_compiler.cpp:2657-2665), so a permutation is always over the same relations at the same written positions, a self-join's two roles included.
- Hunt 1, the fetch id drops only the leading select list (src/parser/fingerprint.cpp:316-352); FROM, ON, WHERE, GROUP BY, ORDER BY with direction, LIMIT/OFFSET and every alias are folded as text. Literals, '?' and strings share one shape tag (fingerprint.cpp:157-161) and are told apart only by arg_hash, which no plan key reads.
- Hunt 2, a permuted scope cannot rebind a column to another relation: a qualified name takes the first binding match (step_compiler.cpp:681-693), which is order-dependent only if two relations share a binding, and the parser refuses that (src/parser/parser.cpp:3252-3280 CheckDistinctBindings); an unqualified name is refused as ambiguous whenever two relations of the level have it (step_compiler.cpp:698-710), whatever the order.
- Hunt 2, probes, derived conjuncts, build keys, decode masks, projection and sort keys are all recomputed per order inside CompileBound from slot-relative ColumnRefs (PropagateEqualities at step_compiler.cpp:3146, placement 3149-3204, ladder 3248-3504, ProjectColumns 3555, sort keys 3571-3583); only step_id is rewritten afterwards (2640-2642) and no compile-time structure holds a step_id value.
- Hunt 2, step ids as written positions are safe at run time: trail replay is keyed (step_id, tuple key) and drops an entry whose rel_oid differs from the step's (src/exec/trail_replay.cpp:30-37, 51), so a trail recorded under one order locates the same relation's row under another; kernels, builds and stats are per-statement maps keyed by a still-unique id (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1207, 3181-3183), and the build store is statement-scoped (step_vm.cpp:230-232).
- Hunt 2, an order naming a relation twice or omitting one is unreachable: CREATE PLAN refuses it (src/exec/plan_ddl.cpp:337-348), a sys.plans row whose order is not a permutation is refused at decode for both administrator's variants and captured rows (src/exec/plan_catalog.cpp:358-359, 462-463), and a captured or memoised order is built from the chosen chain's own step ids (step_compiler.cpp:2966-2972, 2990-2991).
- Hunt 2, no reorder across a subquery or an outer join: a block with a subquery predicate anywhere, a boolean tree's leaf included, takes no order and no single-relation plan (step_compiler.cpp:2508-2534, 2610-2617); nested blocks are compiled with no PlanOptions (2226, LowerSubquery takes none); outer joins never reach the native compile (include/kds/parser/ast.hpp:617-620).
- Hunt 2, ORDER BY elision under a reorder: each elision tests the relation actually at slot 0 of the compiled order (step_compiler.cpp:3613-3636; DriverOrderOf requires every key to be slot 0's own column, 1364), a Cabin-probed driver keeps its sort (include/kds/exec/step_chain.hpp:205), and an index key set's rows are sorted by tuple key across all values before they are resolved (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1650, 1667).
- Hunt 3, the memo hit that skips AddedSortCannotRefuse: PlanMemoKey compares block, arg_hash, statistics generation, catalog word and version, join_build_max_rows and sort_max_rows by defaulted equality (include/kds/exec/plan_memo.hpp:35-45); arg_hash folds every literal token of the statement, LIMIT and OFFSET included (src/parser/fingerprint.cpp:355-381), and the memo is off when any '?' is present (step_compiler.cpp:2883-2886; command_dispatcher.cpp:10250). A stored order is one EstimateChain priced finite under those same inputs, and it prices an unbounded added sort infinite (src/exec/join_order.cpp:313-316), so a hit cannot bring a sort the search would have rejected.
- Hunt 4, variant choice cannot alter rows: Choose only selects an order and pins that then go through the ladder (src/exec/admin_plans.cpp:45-54; step_compiler.cpp:2700-2728). ARG numbering counts '?' as a position of kind kUnbound (src/parser/lexer.cpp:323-345), a negative integer is one fused literal token carrying its sign (lexer.cpp:172-182), and a rule reading ARG(k) past the example's value positions is refused at CREATE PLAN (plan_ddl.cpp:354-360).
- Hunt 5, BUILD ON forces nothing: under it the tuple key, index and Cabin classes are simply not tried (admin_plans.hpp:135-138) and the build arm runs under its unchanged rule (step_compiler.cpp:3433-3447, BuildKeyOf 1887-1915, which still declines a tuple key equality and a second correlated conjunct); an unmet BUILD ON refuses that compile and the written chain runs (3493-3497, 2713-2728). The run-time cap and publish rule are the unpinned build's (src/exec/step_vm.cpp:1207-1245).
- ACCESS pins restrict only: each rank is gated by pin.Admits and otherwise runs its own rule (step_compiler.cpp:3252-3262, 3325, 3339-3433); an unmet positive pin refuses the compile (3477-3485). A named INDEX pin still skips a building index in all three index choosers (step_compiler.cpp:1327, 1417, 1568).
- Pin-induced new step state, a tuple key equality left in a walk's residual (SCAN, NOT TUPLE_KEY, INDEX, CABIN, BUILD ON): the scan kernel has a dedicated Keystone term for column 0 and compares the decoded id (src/exec/row_codec.cpp:1410-1417), a correlated one is never a kernel term (src/exec/chain_frame.cpp:140-141), and the filter-scan rank ignores tuple key equalities (step_compiler.cpp:1047).
- CABIN (<column>) pin: the gate admits only the column Cabin on the named column and no expression or predicate Cabin (step_compiler.cpp:1455-1478); a name that matches no column or names position 0 admits nothing and the met-check declines the variant.

ADD/DROP COLUMN moves no oid and no written position, so the key and the order still fit.

Reader's notes: No quiet-wrong finding on this path at fba5529f; everything is [source-read], nothing was built or run. I used roughly 10 of the 20 minutes. Why the path holds: every non-written order, whatever its source (administrator's variant, captured order, memo hit, search), is applied by one mechanism. compile_order permutes the bound scope and runs the whole of CompileBound again for the statement in hand, then rewrites step ids to written positions. A plan key can therefore only select which permutation or pin set is tried; it never carries a chain. The two things that would break this are both guarded: name resolution depending on scope order (the parser refuses duplicate bindings; unqualified ambiguity is refused) and an invalid permutation (refused at CREATE PLAN and at sys.plans row decode). Not traced, so not cleared: - src/exec/plan_catalog.cpp beyond the two permutation checks, src/exec/captured_plans.cpp, src/exec/plan_rule.cpp's Holds, and HandleCreatePlan / HandleAlterPlan / HoldPlanRelations bodies. Hunt 6's RESTRICT interplay and the durable sys.plans row lifecycle were reasoned only from AdminPlans::EnabledPinning and the compile side. - The run-time posterior fold of a reordered chain's counts (statistics only, cannot change rows). - Whether a controller-made (auto origin) Cabin read through ACCESS ... CABIN (<column>) is always built under the announce and banking gate. The compile side only widens the gate (step_compiler.cpp:1470-1472, 1487); the serve path is the Cabin controller's and outside this hunt. - I searched tests/ only by file name (admin_plans_test, captured_plans_test, join_planner_test, plan_catalog_test exist); no cell was read to pin any claim above. One observation outside the lens, not a finding: a NOT <arm> pin has no met-check (step_compiler.cpp:3477 tests only non-forbid pins), so NOT SCAN on a step with no keyed arm still walks. That matches "restricts, never forces".

Confidence on the finding is medium, not high: I read every function from BindExcluded to the typer's refusals, but did not read EvaluateSetExpression's decimal arithmetic result kind (I rely on types.md TY5 and row_codec.cpp:634 that a decimal value is kDecimal), and did not confirm the parser accepts DO UPDATE after a SELECT source beyond the shared tail at parser.cpp:2587-2644 and the DO NOTHING cell at dml_forms_test.cpp:185. An unaliased first RETURNING item is headed by its text as written (parser.cpp:4155-4159), so `INSERT ... RETURNING ERR + 1` on a relation with a column named err is treated as a failed statement and unwound. Over KWP the reply is not prefixed, so it does not arise there. - Tuple-key DO NOTHING where an earlier row of the statement takes an issued id that a later row names is refused AlreadyExists at the later row rather than skipped (pre-pass at 7688-7701 decides before ids are issued). A refusal, not a wrong row. - An integer literal written into a DATE or TIMESTAMP column is stored as an epoch value (row_codec.cpp:286-311); types.md 178-186 records this as the operator's ruling of 2026-10-13, so it is left out. Not traced: the lock table's internals behind BorrowOrWait, AssertionEnforcer's AdmitInsert/ReserveInsert bodies, exec::CheckTransitionInsert, CheckForeignKeyOnWrite's body past its first lines, LogInsert, and the rollback path that undoes rows 1..k-1 (I read only EndWrite's head). Suspicions checked and found sound:

- WHERE re-evaluated after a wait, statement wrote nothing: a held row answers Refused and DispatchAsync re-runs the whole statement under a fresh snapshot - ParkedOutcome command_dispatcher.cpp:11749-11752, EndWrite keeps the blocker only when the trail is unchanged 13892-13896, re-run at 555
- WHERE after a wait, statement HAD written: resume keeps the original view and re-reads the row from the page; a head committed meanwhile is invisible, so it is either skipped (RC, head fails WHERE: SnapshotVersionDecides 11814-11818, the F4 ruling) or refused first-updater-wins (manager.cpp:202-212) - never written from a stale read
- A version reached through undo is never written: through_undo set at 11604 and Claim refuses Corruption if the conflict check admitted it 11637-11646; with collected targets the write pass carries head bytes and head trx_id, so the conflict check judges the head (11558-11571)
- Delete-marked head by an invisible writer cannot be overwritten or re-marked: Classify gives kNeedsUndoWalk (visibility.hpp:64-78), the claim then runs CheckWriteConflict on the head's writer, which admits only own id / a writer the transaction's view sees (manager.cpp:196-202); the snapshot is never older than the transaction's view on either arm (explicit: same view 13221-13224; autocommit: Begin at 13265 precedes the snapshot at 10899)
- Halloween by re-meeting a row in one statement: UPDATE is an in-place overwrite (12269-12335), no write is index-driven, the walk resumes by key through a fresh descent and skips every key below the cursor (8747-8790), a stop with no readable key is Corruption not a restart from zero (8807-8813)
- Key-set double write (id IN (1,1)) and resume: items sorted and de-duplicated step_compiler.cpp:948-954; resume starts at lower_bound of the cursor key 11718-11723; a declined descent hands the rest to the walk from that key 11733-11735
- Subquery in a write's WHERE reading the statement's own writes: every sub-chain, including one under OR/NOT, lands in step.sub_chains (step_compiler.cpp:502-506, 2428-2449), so WriteKeyOf/WriteKeySetOf decline (2470-2483) and CollectTargets runs a read pass first (11676-11693); targets are carried across a park (12531, 11756, 10864)
- SET scalar subquery run once and not re-run on resume: resolver returns the first run's answers by index 11887-11893, carried in ParkedWrite 10870, 10925
- SET evaluated against the before-image: values collected for every assignment before any is applied 12150-12163, applied to row only at 12196-12201; row is re-decoded from payload_copy per visit (12131) and spill scratch is cleared by the decoder (row_codec.cpp:1107, 1150); a column named twice is refused at compile (comment 11871-11876)
- Row already updated by this transaction (explicit): own id is visible to the transaction's view, the head is decoded as the before-image and the undo record chains to the earlier one (manager.cpp:197-200; 12310-12326)
- Transition old state on a second update in one transaction: checked against row[t->column_no] decoded from the head this writer holds, after the claim and before any write 12183-12189; the governed column is forced into the read mask or a whole decode 11960-11971; DELETE checks the completed frame after the claim 15471-15497
- Re-run after a stale descent's TxnConflict when the statement had written: structural_rerun requires the trail unchanged 13913-13915 and the note is cleared otherwise 13916; LocateByTupleKey clears the note when the scan answers 9298; the statement head clears it 1229-1230
- Assertion reservations vs the trail-based re-run test: AdmitAndReserveUpdate withholds the waitable refusal once it has reserved (assertion_check.cpp:765, 809-810); every park in the row function precedes the reservation (claim 12093-12096, unique check 12209-12217 before 12237; DELETE's reverse check 15506-15519 before ReserveDelete 15530); a failure after a reservation is non-waitable and poisons or aborts (13934-13953), and AbortTxn gives back every pending reservation (assertion_check.cpp:871-890)
- Mid-walk refusal in an explicit transaction: rows stay and the session is poisoned (13918-13941), stated as the contract at 12560-12567; autocommit aborts the owned scope with enforcer unwind first (13946-13953, 13998-14002)
- Unique-key move checked with the leaf released: nothing of the row is written before unique_pending returns (12209-12216), the row is re-qualified and re-decoded on the second visit, clearance is honoured only for the same row and same key values (12211-12213), key units held until the entries are in (12365)
- U claim vs a concurrent FOR UPDATE / declared window: the row unit is asked before the conflict is decided (11631-11635), a refused borrow is a conflict even when MVCC is silent (13831), a declared range and a tuple unit are checked against each other after the grant is published (lock_table.cpp:316-343)
- Foreign-key SET value never unchecked: a column-reading or clock-reading expression into an fk column is refused at compile (step_compiler.cpp:2380-2392); a value the extraction pass did not resolve is refused NotImplemented, not passed (5848-5867)
- UPDATE ... FROM double match: rewritten to an at-most-one EXISTS over the whole WHERE (parser.cpp:3679-3681), so it takes the collection pass
- ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE meeting one row twice or a row its view cannot see: CardinalityViolation at 8133-8140, TxnConflict on rows_affected == 0 at 8174-8182
- EncodeAssigned carries unassigned cells and the null bitmap untouched: StartRow rewrites only the Keystone word (row_codec.cpp:934-945), each assigned cell is zeroed and its null bit cleared before encode (1016-1018)
- Partial decode leaving stale slots: unread slots are poisoned kUndecoded once and only masked columns are re-decoded (12121-12133); a column >= 64 forces the all-columns mask (set_expr.cpp:2057) and DELETE completes the frame with the complement mask before any reader (15471-15478)
- RETURNING rows: only this statement's trail entries from the restored mark, de-duplicated by key (11344-11353)

Reader's notes: [source-read] at fba5529f (v3.4.17), worktree audit-v3-4-and-architecture; nothing built or run. About 11 minutes of the budget used. No quiet-wrong found on the UPDATE/DELETE walk itself. Every suspected execution in the brief ended at a guard, listed in cleared with path:line. The one finding is a narrow logic defect in how a write's verdict is derived from its reply text, reachable only over the text reply path. Observed and deliberately not reported, because each is a refusal (not a wrong answer) and the code or spec states it: - READ COMMITTED: a row whose head was committed after the statement's view and still matches the WHERE is refused TxnConflict rather than re-read (manager.cpp:204-208 says so); with a delete-marked head the same refusal stands where PostgreSQL would skip. - REPEATABLE READ: a row whose newer committed head matches the WHERE while the snapshot's version does not is refused first-updater-wins rather than skipped (Qualify tests the head first, command_dispatcher.cpp:11572-11574). - Autocommit: a writer that commits between BeginWrite's Begin and the snapshot mint is visible to Qualify but not to the conflict check, giving a spurious retryable TxnConflict (13265 vs 10899). - A statement refused before writing keeps the row U and parent S units it already took until the decide in an explicit transaction (over-locking only). Not traced to the end: exec::CheckTransitionUpdate/Delete bodies, exec::MaintainIndexes, NoteCabinWrite, txn::ResolveThroughUndo, TransactionManager::Abort's trail compensation, BtreeVisitLeafPage's slot iteration, ReadRowByKey on a delete-marked slot (DELETE ... RETURNING). The statement-boundary flag on a resumed READ COMMITTED write is round 1's txn-locks-delta#3 and was left out.


## 10. What the scheduled run of 2026-11-20 did with each

`[operator]` On 2026-11-20 (16:55 KST): *"At 10 PM KST today, schedule the
work of applying the items whose audit results are out. The time ceiling is
3 hours. After that, as an exception for this case only, push no verify.
Where a decision is needed follow CLA's proposal, but where a large issue
needs the operator's decision, withhold the application."* The run began at
12:59 UTC on worktree `audit-h-findings-apply`, from `origin/main` at
`054dbc5e` (whose source is `fba5529f`'s). Every choice below that is not
quoted is CLA's, not the operator's mark. Overhead was not measured.

**Applied** (each verified against the tree first; a gtest cell each unless
it is a comment or a removal):

- H3 - an integer literal past 64 bits is refused at its byte in a type
  argument and an assertion's bound (one digits decoder shared with
  `ParsePaginationCount`); a bound above `INT64_MAX` is refused by name.
- H6 - a call that compiled with no plan note clears its core's
  `plan_source`/`plan_label`, so a plan that stopped applying reads NULL.
  The cross-core merge is left as `sys-views.md` §6.1 states it (the core
  that saw the shape last, with a source); a merge by the latest compile
  across cores would change that sentence and is not taken.
- H8 - a set operation pairs a stored column with a computed value of its
  type (`set_expr`'s `SameType` rule), and the layer's join hashes a pair
  whose declared widths differ for every type but decimal and `char`.
- H10 - the 4 KiB pre-auth cap counts the KWP decoder's held bytes
  (`FrameDecoder::buffered()`).
- H11 - `BEGIN`'s clause refusals carry the offending token's byte.
- H12 - a quoted config value runs to its matching quote; text after it but
  a comment is refused `origin:line`, as is an unterminated quote.
- H13 / H25 - the plan statistics' master eviction takes no key merged in
  the same step.
- H16 - a decimal literal's precision is checked before it is scaled.
- H17 - the forward ordered index walk ends a slice only on a pass that
  moved, so it advances at `pages_per_slice = 1`.
- H21 - a cancel taken at a `C_SYNC` sends `S_ERROR` (`CANCELLED`) and then
  `S_READY` (`protocol.md` §3 item 4 and §10 together).
- H22 - three refusals given their byte; the fourth was unreachable and is
  removed. H34, H93, H94, H95, H97, H100, H106, H108 and H107's
  `KeywordText` - as each finding says.

**Withheld for the operator:**

- H1, H2 - quiet-wrong whose fix is a design choice (the banking gate's view
  of a `READ ONLY` view; whether pass 2 re-tests the `WHERE`).
- H4 - the lexer fix moves the golden corpus's line 169 from three hashes to
  `-`, which the corpus header does not admit; it needs a ruling under
  `fingerprint.hpp`'s bump rule. The patch is described in this run's
  report: the string arm returns `kError` at the opening quote.
- H5 - moves `fetch_id`s; `kFingerprintVersion`'s rule.
- H7 - `foreign-keys.md` §2a prescribes today's retryable refusal; B2's
  ruling covers the reverse walk and the unique check only. Options: (a)
  B2's ceiling wait in the forward check (CLA's proposal), (b) keep the
  refusal and warn only for an in-flight holder, (c) leave it.
- H26 - taking the verdict from the outcome would commit a write whose text
  reply still begins `ERR `; the fault is the text rendering, whose shape is
  the operator's. The attempt was backed out.
- H9, H14, H15, H18, H19, H20, H23, H24 - each needs a choice between a
  behaviour and a documented limit, or touches the scheduler's idle policy.
- H99 - a spec's two contracts; H117 - unverified, SQL-visible typing.
- H27-H60 (inefficiency and performance) - a hot-path change wants a
  measurement this run did not budget; H61-H91 (architecture and
  duplication) - larger than the ceiling.
- H107's `S_NOTICE` (a wire enumerator holding the numbering) and
  `varheap_slots_swept`; H98, H101-H105, H109 - removals whose callers are
  tests or whose comment names a heal path, not taken in this run.

Review (`critics-developer`): no correctness bug. Taken: H6's cross-core
merge put back to the spec's rule; a test's fatal assertion with a server
thread running made non-fatal. Not taken: folding `RefuseWhole` and the
`READ ONLY` gate onto the new `AtByte` (outside the findings), and a cancel
pending while a session already skips to `C_SYNC` (pre-existing, not a
finding of this record).
